# Changelog

## Unreleased

Breaking:

- A styled input has an underline mark and kinds of paragraphs, and four types gained for them: `SpanStyle` a field `underline`, `SelectionStyle` a field `underline`, `ThemeTextMarks` a field `underline` (one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..Default::default()`), `TextMark` a variant `Underline` (`TextMark::ALL` is five long) and `StyledEdit` a variant `Paragraphs { range }` (a `match` on them with no wildcard must take them).
- A child of a container with `presence()` added again, by its key or its id, while it leaves ends its leaving and is the one added, the same element with its state. It was a new one beside the one leaving until that one's exit ended, so a panel opened again while its rows faded out (an `Accordion`'s, a `Collapsible`'s, a `PropertyGrid`'s category's) held its rows twice and was twice as high, and a `Banner` shown again while it faded out stood twice in the page. What wants the old way gives the one coming back a key of its own each time (`.key(format!("saved-{shown}"))`), as `Toasts::show` does. A layer opened again while it leaves does the same: see the line under Fixes. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#children-of-a-container).
- `ScrollBarStyle` has three fields more, `thumb_inset`, `thumb_hovered_color` and `thumb_pressed_color`: a style written out field by field must add them. The design system's theme draws the design's bar: a thumb of 6 inside a lane of 8, round, `MutedForeground` under the pointer and while it is dragged, where its thumb was as wide as its lane and the same under the pointer; each bar of a container that scrolls both ways has its own, and where both are there each track ends before the other's lane. `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` keeps the bar of 0.61, `ScrollBarStyle::default()`. See [scroll](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#scroll).
- `ShrinkLimit` has a variant more, `ContentFirst`: a child that keeps to its content minimum while the children of its order can take the overflow, then gives way below it to its `min_width`, the last first. A `match` over `ShrinkLimit` with no wildcard must add it. The content minimum of a line on the other axis (a `Column` in a row) is the widest of its children's, a child with a `min_width` of its own counted at it, where it was the column's natural width. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#give-way-order-and-limits).
- A growing child with a flex basis in a `Row` or a `Column` is its basis and its share of the room the bases leave, as on the web: the free room of a line is its length less the gaps, the children sized on their own and the bases. The bases were left in the free room, so a child of `flex_full(1.0, 0.0, Some(50.0))` beside a child of 50 in a row of 200 was 200 wide and ran out of the row, where it is now 150; one that could shrink shrank back by its basis. A basis of 0, the common `flex_full(1.0, 1.0, Some(0.0))`, lays out as before. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#flex).
- A border on some sides of a rounded element no longer thins out round a corner whose other side has none, as CSS draws it: it goes on round the arc at its full width and fades out along it, from opaque at the bordered side to nothing at the other. Sides with the same placement and dash are now one shape whatever their colors, and two colors that meet at a rounded corner go over from one to the other along the arc, at full coverage; at a square corner they meet on the diagonal, where the side painted last covered the corner. A border outside or centered on the element can paint past a side with none by its width near such a corner. Layout and hit testing are unchanged, but every app with such a border is painted differently. `StrokeStyle` has a new field, `side_colors`, and `StrokeStyle::outset`. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#borders).
- A value that changes because the theme does, a color of the other appearance, is applied at once to an element that has a transition for it, as it is to everything else in the window. Such an element used to fade from its old color while the window around it had already changed: a button stayed light for its transition's length on a window that had turned dark. A transition is for what the element does itself: a state, a style set on it.
- **The default theme is the design system's.** `Theme::new()` has the design's palette (`pencil/lurq-design-system.pen`) in place of the blue one of 0.61: a neutral primary, other borders, a near-black foreground. It has a dark palette too and follows the system's appearance, so on a system in its dark look a window is dark: text that names no color is near-white, and a window whose root paints no background is cleared to the dark `Background`. The radii are the design's too: `Sm` 4, `Md` 6, `Lg` 8, where they were 3, 5 and 6. So is the type scale: body text is 14 where it was 16, so `Text::new(...)` with no style is smaller, headings are semibold with tighter letter spacing, and `TypographyStyle::Mono` is 13 on lines of 1.3, the line its family draws, where it was 16 on lines of 1.2. An app that paints light backgrounds of its own under text that names no color must say what it wants: `app.theme().apply(ThemeDefinition::legacy())` brings the whole 0.61 look back, with one palette, and `app.theme().set_preferred_appearance(Some(Appearance::Light))` keeps the new look in its light palette. See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md).
- `ThemeTypography` has two fields more, `sans` and `mono_family`: one written out field by field must add them.
- `TypographyStyle` has eight variants more, `Heading1` to `Heading6`, `Prose` and `Article`, and `ThemeTypography` a field for each: a `match` on the one must take them, and the other written out field by field must add them, or end with `..ThemeTypography::default()`. `TextStyle` has a field more, `case`: one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..TextStyle::default()`.
- **Markdown is on the theme's roles.** `ThemeMarkdown`'s colors are `TextColor`s, a palette role or a color: `MarkdownTextStyle::color`, `MarkdownBlockStyle::background` and `border_color`, `MarkdownLinkState`'s four colors and `MarkdownFocusRing::color`. Write `Some(PaletteColor::Info.into())` for a role, `Some(Color::from_hex("#2563eb").into())` for a color. A document is drawn in the theme it is in, light and dark; its default look is the design's (`pencil/lurq-design-system.pen`, Markdown), no longer dark code blocks and slate grays. `MarkdownLinkState` and `MarkdownFocusRing` are `Clone` and no longer `Copy`. `TextStyle` has a field more, `baseline_shift`, and `MarkdownTextStyle` one, `baseline_shift`: one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..Default::default()`. `ThemeMarkdown` has fields more (`task_item_spacing`, `bullet_gap`, `box_gap`, `bullet_indent`, `bullet_size`, `table_stripe`, `heading_2_rule`, `kbd`, `task_done`, `rule`, `image_box`, `image_height`, `image_caption`, `details_box`, `details_summary_box`, `details_summary`, `details_padding`, `footnote_reference`, `footnote`, `footnotes_rule_width`): one written out field by field must add them, or end with `..ThemeMarkdown::default()`.
- A Markdown code block is a `ui::CodeBlock` and a quote a `ui::Blockquote`, one look with those wherever they are: the code under a bar named by its language, with the button that copies it, the quote's words 14 in italics in the muted foreground after a line of 2. `ThemeMarkdown` has no fields of their own any more: `code_block`, `code_block_label`, `code_block_spacing`, `code_block_box`, `code_block_scrollbar`, `blockquote_box`, `blockquote_marker` and `blockquote_gap` are gone. Style the theme's `code_block` and `blockquote` recipes in their place (`theme.patch::<CodeBlock>(|recipe| recipe.base(CodeBlockPart::Root, ..))`, `BlockquotePart::Label` for a quote's words). A code block's lines are where the code block puts them, under its bar: an element lookup that counted on the old box sees the code block's parts. A selection across a document copies a code block's language and its lines, as before, and none of the glyphs of the document's controls.
- `MarkdownProps::on_link_click` is `on_link_select`, by the design system's rule for what a document of links tells: which one was followed. `on_link_activate` is as it was.
- A Markdown document with no style of its own is in the theme's `TypographyStyle::Article` (15 on lines of 1.65), its headings in `Heading1` to `Heading6`, where it was in `TextStyle::default()` with headings scaled from it.
- `MarkdownBlock` has two variants more, `Admonition` and `Details`, and `MarkdownInline` one, `Kbd`: a `match` on them must take them. A quote that starts `> [!NOTE]` (and the four others GitHub writes) is an `Admonition`, `<details>` and `<summary>` HTML blocks around Markdown are a `Details`, and `<kbd>` in a sentence a `Kbd`, where they were a quote and HTML.
- A Markdown footnote is drawn at the end of the document, numbered by the first reference to it, and a reference is its number: `rendered_text()` has `1` where it had `[^1]`. A bulleted item's marker is a dot (a selection still copies its `-`), and a task's `[x]` is a checkbox, no longer text.
- `TextHighlight` has two fields more, `padding` and `border`: one written out field by field must add them, or be made with `TextHighlight::new`.
- `ThemePalette` has a field for each of the design's roles (42 in place of 23). A palette written out field by field with no `..ThemePalette::default()` must add them; build one from `ThemePalette::light()`, `dark()` or `legacy()` instead.
- `Theme::set_palette_color(role, color)` sets the role in the light palette and in the dark one, so a color set once holds when the system changes its appearance; `Theme::set_palette(palette)` sets the palette of the appearance the theme is in and keeps it when the appearance changes and comes back. On a theme with a palette for each appearance both were lost at the next change of appearance.
- A window whose root paints no background is cleared to the theme's `Background`, which is white in the light palette as before and near-black in the dark one. It was always white.
- A text built with `Text::styled(..)` from a style that keeps the default text color names no color, and takes the `.foreground(..)` of an element above it, as a text with no style does. It took the theme's foreground whatever was above it.
- A notch of a mouse's wheel is animated (see Added), so the content is where the notch sent it about 150 ms later and not in the next frame. What could rely on the old way: an `on_scroll` handler also hears an event with a zero delta for every step of the animation, where it heard the wheel's event alone; `on_scroll_reach_top` and `on_scroll_reach_bottom` run when the content gets there; `Tree::has_active_timeline()` is true while it is on its way; and a harness that sends `SyntheticInput::wheel` or the MCP server's `wheel` and reads the offset at once reads it on its way, so it waits for the window to be idle (`lurq_wait`) first. `Tree::scroll`, a touchpad and a finger scroll at once as before, and `app.theme().set_smooth_scroll(false)` brings the old way back for the wheel.

- A disabled element hears the pointer come and go: its `on_mouse_enter` and `on_mouse_leave` run as the pointer comes onto it and leaves it, so a tooltip can say why it is disabled. They did not run. Nothing else of the pointer reaches it: it is not hovered, and no press, click or drop reaches it.
- A modal over the whole window (`Modal::target(ModalTarget::Root)`) in a window with a `WindowChrome` covers the window under the title bar, where it covered all of it with its top under the bar: a dialog is centred in what is seen, and a veil ends at the bar. What is passed to `WindowChrome::overlay` still has all of the window.
- `BackgroundColor` has a variant more, `CurrentForeground`: a `match` on it must take it.
- `AnimatableValue` has two variants more, `Percent` and `Dash`, and `AnimatableProperty` one, `BorderDash`: a `match` on them must take them.
- `AnimatableValue` has a variant more, `Fit { px, content }`: a `match` on it must take it.
- `StrokeStyle` has a field more, `side_colors`: one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..StrokeStyle::default()`.
- `EventHandlers` has a field more, `press_repeats`, and `SelectTriggerState` one, `input`: one written out field by field must add it.
- A single-select whose checkmark is after the label keeps the checkmark's room on the selected row only: the labels and trailing elements of the other rows run to the row's end. Every row kept an empty slot of the checkmark's width.
- `FormValue` has a variant more, `Range(f64, f64)`: a `match` on it must take it.

- `DragEvent` has a field more, `outside_window`: one written out field by field must add it.
- With `devtools`, `lurq::app::devtools::load_fonts` is gone: DevTools draws its icons with the theme's and loads no font of its own. Take the call out of the app.
- With `devtools`, `ComponentContextDebug` is no longer made of two fields: it has `reactive` too, and `value()` and `history()`. `ctx.provide`, `ctx.provide_if_changed` and `ctx.create_context` take any type, as they did.
- `PersistentStorageSnapshotEntry` has a field more, `fields`: the fields of a stored struct, each with its value as it is written, empty for any other value. One written out field by field must add it.
- With `devtools`, `ComponentValueChangeDebug` has a field more, `sequence`: which change it is among all the changes of every signal and memo, a later one a greater number. One written out field by field must add it.
- With `devtools`, `DevToolsNode` has a field more, `render` (how the component rendered, `None` on a node no component rendered), and `DevToolsProps` one, `system` (the system's settings as the inspected window knows them): one written out field by field must add them.

Deprecated:

- The palette roles of 0.61, each in favor of one of the design's: `Accent` → `Primary`, `AccentHover` → `PrimaryHover`, `AccentMuted` → `Highlight`, `SurfaceBase` and `SurfaceInput` → `Background`, `SurfacePanel` → `Canvas`, `SurfaceRaised` → `Card`, `BorderStrong` → `Input`, `BorderFocus` → `Ring`, `TextPrimary` → `Foreground`, `TextSecondary` and `TextMuted` → `MutedForeground`, `TextInverse` → `PrimaryForeground`, `Danger` → `Destructive`, `DangerMuted` → `DestructiveMuted`. The `PaletteColor` variants and the `ThemePalette` fields still compile, with a warning that names the replacement. Each keeps a color of its own, and setting a role also sets the deprecated roles it replaces, so `Select`, the form controls and a checkbox or slider with no style of its own, which still read them, follow a theme set through the new roles. `PaletteColor::replacement()` gives the role that replaces one.
- The engine's custom title bar is drawn in the theme's roles: `ChromeTitleBar::new()` is `Muted` under a line of `Border` (it was `#101215` under `#252a32`), `ChromeBorderPolicy::PlatformDefault` paints `Border`, and `WindowControls`' controls are `Foreground` on `SecondaryHover` with the close one on `Destructive` (`DestructiveHover` held) and its glyph `DestructiveForeground` (they were `#d9dee7` on `#232934` and `#2d3440`, close `#c0392b` and `#922b21`). An app that wants the old dark bar on every theme writes it: `ChromeTitleBar::new().background("#101215").border_bottom(Border::inside(1.0, Color::from_hex("#252a32")))` and `WindowControls::new().colors(WindowControlColors { foreground: Color::from_hex("#d9dee7").into(), .. })`.

Added:

- `TextInput::squiggles(InputSquiggles)` draws wavy lines under ranges of an input's text, what a spelling checker marks, on every row a range is on; `TextInput::ghost(InputGhost)` draws a suggestion after the end of what is typed, while the input has the focus with the caret there.
- `ui::Input::squiggles(ranges)` and `ui::Textarea::squiggles(ranges)`, in `Destructive`, and `ui::Input::completion(&str)`, a suggestion after what is typed that Tab takes: the marks of text editing the board draws (Desktop Tools, Text Editing), on the design system's fields. The board's edit menu is `ContextMenu::bar(true)`.
- `ui::RichTextEditor` (with the `markdown` feature): formatted text typed into a box under a bar of its tools. Its value is a `RichText`, bound (`new(Signal<RichText>)`) or held by the app (`controlled`), with `on_change`. The tools (`RichTextTool`: the style of the paragraph, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, a bulleted and a numbered list, a quote, code, a link, an image, undo and redo) work on the selection and show what it is; the keys do the same (Ctrl+B, I, U, Shift+X, E, K, Alt+0 to 3, Shift+7, 8, 9); Markdown typed becomes what it says (`## `, `- `, `1. `, `> `, `**bold**`, `*italic*`, `` `code` ``, `~~struck~~`). `RichTextController` runs its commands from outside it. It has a placeholder, rows, read only, disabled, the judgments, a footer that counts the words, and every word it draws can be said otherwise.
- `ui::Editable`: a text read where it stands and edited in place. A press, Enter or F2 puts a field where the text was, with the text selected; Enter and leaving the field keep the edit, Escape drops it, and the focus goes back to the reading form. Bound (`new(Signal<String>)`) or held (`controlled`), with `on_change`, `on_cancel`, `editing(Signal<bool>)` and `on_editing_change`, a placeholder, its two buttons or none (`actions`), `submit_on_blur`, read only and disabled.
- `TextInput::paragraphs(kinds, styles)` (with the `markdown` feature): a styled input keeps what each of its paragraphs is in a `Signal<Vec<ParagraphKind>>` beside its spans (`Body`, `Heading(1..=6)`, `Bullet`, `Ordered`, `Quote`, `Code`), in step with every edit and in its undo history, and draws each kind as a `ParagraphStyles` says: its type, the room before its text, a `ParagraphMarker` (a dot, a number, a bar), a fill, and the room between paragraphs. `TextInputController::set_paragraphs`, `selected_paragraphs`, `caret_paragraph` and `rich_text`; `StyledChange::paragraphs()` and `to_rich_text()`.
- `components::RichText`: the text, the spans and the kinds of a styled input as one value, with `from_markdown`, `to_markdown`, `plain`, `blocks` and `word_count`.
- An underline mark in a styled input: `SpanStyle::underline()`, `TextMark::Underline`, `ThemeTextMarks::underline`.
- `TextInputController::undo`, `redo`, `can_undo` and `can_redo`: what an undo button runs and is enabled by, on a plain input too.
- `TextInput::select_on_focus(bool)`: all its text is selected as it takes the focus, so that what is typed takes its place.
- `ui::Button::focuses(&ElementRef)` and `ui::Toggle::focuses(&ElementRef)`: a press of it gives the focus to another element in place of taking it, as the tools of an editor's bar leave it in the text.
- DevTools' **Components** tab is built anew from its board (`pencil/lurq-devtools.pen`) with the design system's components, in a theme of DevTools' own: the design system's, light or dark with the system, whatever the inspected app made of its theme. The window draws its own title bar (`ui::WindowChrome`). The tree is a `ui::Table` whose first column is the tree, with the node's kind and how many children it has beside it, built for the rows in view; a divider between the tree and the inspector is dragged (`ui::Splitter`). The inspector says what rendered the node (the nearest component it is in), how many times a component has rendered and how long its last render took (counted by the engine where a render is called: `ComponentRenderDebug`, on `DevToolsNode::render`; they were numbers written in the source before), and shows its props, contexts, reactive values (signals, memos, effects) and attributes in a table each, its layout as a box in a box and its style a row for each property. Ctrl+F (Cmd+F) goes to the search, which says how many nodes it found, the nodes shown only on the way to them left out and drawn muted. See [DevTools](docs/src/content/docs/devtools.md). In the tree, Left closes the selected row and Right opens it.
- DevTools' **Signals** tab is built anew from its board: the signals, memos and effects of the inspected window in a table with a filter (Ctrl+F goes to it), and beside it the one selected between what owns it and what it notifies, over its changes. The changes are the engine's own record, every one of the last 64 of each signal and memo with the moment it happened: the old tab compared two readings of the values a tenth of a second apart, while it was recording and shown, and lost what changed in between. Recording is now which of them are shown: it is on as the tab opens, stopping it leaves out what changes until it is on again, and Clear starts from nothing. A change is timed by the clock and the zone of the system, where it was UTC's.
- `#[derive(DevtoolsInspectable)]` takes a struct with a field DevTools cannot read: where the field's type does not write itself, the field is shown with its type and nothing of its value, and the derive no longer fails on it. It is found where the struct is compiled, so a field of a type parameter is read only where the parameter is bound by `DevtoolsInspectable`. With this, a type with `#[derive(lurq::DevtoolsInspectable)]` on it compiles whatever it holds.
- DevTools reads a value it is given with no bound on its type by an inspector the type registered: `#[derive(DevtoolsInspectable)]` registers the type it derives for (one without type parameters), `lurq::register_inspectable!(Type, ..)` a type whose `DevtoolsInspectable` is written by hand, and the engine its own (the numbers, `bool`, `char`, `String`, `&'static str`, `Arc<str>`, `Duration`, `PathBuf`). This is how the Context tab shows what a context holds while `ctx.provide` asks nothing of its type: a type that registered none is listed as `Type { unknown }`. The inspectors are gathered before `main` by the `inventory` crate, a dependency of the `devtools` feature alone.
- `DevtoolsInspectable` for more of `std`: `Arc<T>` and `Rc<T>` (the value they share), `Result<T, E>`, arrays and slices, `VecDeque`, `BTreeMap`, `BTreeSet` (how many they hold), `Duration`, `PathBuf`, `Path`, `Cow<str>`, `Range` and `RangeInclusive`. A `Vec<T>` writes its length whatever `T` is, where it asked `T` to be inspectable: a signal of a list of the app's own rows needs no derive on the row.
- DevTools has a **Context** tab, from its board: every context a component of the inspected window provides, with what it holds, how many components read it and which component provides it, and beside the list the one selected between its provider and its consumers, over its changes, each value written out field by field. For it the engine records what a provided context holds and its last 64 changes (`ComponentContextDebug::value`, `history`, `ComponentContextChangeDebug`): of a reactive context each value it is set to, of a plain one each other value it is provided with. The demo, built with `mcp`, opens DevTools with `--devtools` and shows it to agents, as the gallery does.
- DevTools' **Profiler** tab is built anew from its board, the last of the tabs: what records, the capture and the analysis of its spans are as they were, and everything drawn of them is the design system's. The toolbar is its toggles and buttons, with the status of a running recording beside Stop and the last error in it, cut where it is long (it was lines under a toolbar that wrapped). The recording's strip and the frames' tracks say at their start what each is and what it comes to, the selected pass's value or the range's highest; the bars are in the theme's colors (its warning and destructive over the budget, the selected pass in the foreground color, where they were green, yellow and red on fills of DevTools' own) and as wide as the passes of the range leave them. The frame's details are a sidebar: the pass, its screenshot, what it took, and four lists in tabs, **Phases** (each span with a bar of its share of the pass), **Causes** (component, trigger and type in columns, a memo's cause on a row under it), **Input** and **Counters**; they were one long column. The flame chart's spans have the color of their phase along the top (the theme's five colors of a chart; a component has that of the phase it is in, where a color was picked by a hash of the name), and what the pointer says of one is `ui::Tooltip`'s box. The call tree and the bottom-up table are `ui::Table`, built for the rows in view, so they list every row (they stopped at 400), and their headers sort as a table's do. The name of the span selected in the chart, or of the row selected in a table, is picked out in the tables and among the phases of the selected pass. What is shown of the analysis is kept while another tab of DevTools is shown. With this the last of DevTools' own colors and its icon font are gone. It holds in a narrow and in a low window: the toolbar's files go onto a row of their own, what a title says gives way to its numbers and its buttons, the frames scroll and leave the analysis a height it is read at, and a flame chart of more rows than its room is dragged up and down as it is dragged along its time (its deep rows were cut).
- DevTools' **Storage** tab is built anew from its board: a row for each key with the type of its value, the value and its bytes, the totals under them. A stored struct has a row for each of its fields, and the pointer on its type shows where the type is declared (`ui::Tooltip`).
- `Table::reveal(key, request)` and `DataGrid`'s through it: a mounted table brings the row of `key` into view once for each new `request`, as it does for a row the keys go to, for a row the app selects from outside the table. Asking again with the same number does nothing, so a person who scrolled away stays where they are.
- `Toggle::leading(element)`: an element before a toggle's label, as a button has, where an icon by name is not enough. DevTools' toggles that record have the board's mark there, a dot in the destructive color.
- DevTools is drawn in its own theme whatever the app made of its own, and a test holds it to that: with another look whole, roles, recipes and a renderer of the app's own, every tab paints what it paints in an app that changed nothing.
- `SearchField::ref_element(ElementRef)`: the ref of the field's text input, which the app focuses on the key the field's hint shows.
- The gallery, built with `--features devtools`, opens DevTools beside it with `--devtools`, and with `mcp` too DevTools is a window agents read and capture.
- `ui::VideoPlayer`: a video player of the design system that draws the app's picture with the board's controls over it in three forms that follow its width (large, compact, minimal), or the picture alone with the app's own layer over it; every value it shows is the app's, bound or held, and told back on a press, a drag or a key. `ui::Menu` has pages (`MenuItem::page`, `Menu::page`), and `CursorIcon::None` hides the pointer.
- `ui::AudioPlayer`: an audio player in four forms (`AudioPlayerVariant::Full`, `Compact`, `Voice`, `Mini`), built of the media parts, with bound and controlled forms of the position, playing, the volume, muted, the speed, shuffle and repeat (`AudioRepeat`), loading, an error with "Try again", a voice message's speed badge and unheard dot, and a mini player that keeps the track, play and next under 480 wide.
- `ui::AudioQueue` and `ui::AudioQueueItem`: the rows of the tracks a player plays, the current one on the accent fill with the equalizer while it plays, a more button under the pointer, unavailable tracks, and the arrow keys between the rows.
- `ui::SeekBar` is built to the board: played, loaded and the rest on a track the engine's slider drags, a white form over a picture, chapters 2 apart, the time under the pointer in a chip and the app's preview over it (`mount`, `on_hover`, `preview`), keys of a step of 5 seconds, a phone's `SeekBarSize::Lg`, and the forms `empty` (nothing loaded) and `live` (a live stream with nothing to go back in).
- `ui::MediaTime` is built to the board: the mono readings with their roles, over a picture in white, sizes of 11, 12 and 13 (`MediaTimeSize`), a live badge (`live`, `live_text`), and a button with the ring of the focus where it is given `on_click`.
- `ui::Waveform` is built to the board and is a seek surface: `Waveform::new(Signal<f32>, peaks)` beside `controlled` and `at`, a press, a drag and the keys telling `on_change` and `on_change_end` a fraction, `step`, the ring of the focus and a disabled state.
- `ui::PlayButton` is a component of the design system: a round primary disc in four sizes (32, 40, 48, 64) with the glyph of what a press would do (play, pause, play again, the turning spinner while loading), and an `Overlay` variant over a picture, black at 60% with a white glyph in both appearances; `PlayButtonPart`, `PlayButtonState`, `PlayButtonView` and `PlayButtonRenderer` are new.
- `ui::MediaVolume` is a component of the design system: a ghost button that mutes and a slider 72 long, the glyph of the level (loud, low, crossed), muting that keeps the volume and a slider moved up from nothing that turns the sound back on, a collapsed form whose slider comes under the pointer and with the focus (`mount(ctx)`), and a white form over a picture (`on_media`); `MediaVolumePart`, `MediaVolumeState`, `MediaVolumeView` and `MediaVolumeRenderer` are new.
- `ui::MediaSpeedMenu` is a component of the design system: the menu's panel 168 wide with the heading "Playback speed" and the speeds, the one in use checked at the end of its row; `on_change` is told only for another speed; it has a menu's `placement`, `offset`, `collision`, `inline`, `on_dismiss`, `on_open_change` and `label`, and `MediaSpeedMenuPart`, `MediaSpeedMenuState`, `MediaSpeedMenuView` and `MediaSpeedMenuRenderer` are new.
- `CursorIcon::None`: no pointer over an element, which the shells draw by hiding it.
- The gallery has a group `Media` of nine pages, each with a playground driven by a clock of the page's own: Video Player, Audio Player, Audio Queue, Seek Bar, Media Time, Waveform, Play Button, Volume, Speed Menu.
- The gallery's pages open in tabs, as a browser's do: a strip in its title bar (`ui::WindowTabs` in a `ui::WindowChrome`) over the engine's set of tabs (`Tabs`, `TabOutlet` with no bar of its own), each tab a router with its own history, its page kept as it was left while another is shown. The add button and Ctrl+T open one, a press of the middle button or a press with Ctrl on a page of the side bar opens that page in one, a tab is closed by its button, the middle button or Ctrl+W and dragged along the strip; the last one stays. In a browser the gallery has the one tab, and the browser's own.
- `RouterHandle::new(routes)`: a router made outside a render, for a place that is added while the app runs, such as a tab a person opens. `Ctx::router` makes the same.
- `Tabs::move_to(key, index)`: moves a tab among the others, as a strip of tabs a person drags along asks for; the tab keeps its history, its screens and whether it is the one shown.
- `ui::SidebarItem::on_open_aside(handler)`: called when a person asks for an item beside what is open in place of going to it, with the middle button or a press with Ctrl held (Cmd on macOS): an app with tabs opens it in a new one. Such a press does not choose the item.
- Frame sections (`perf_profile`): a pass's steps timed by name, the walks of `update_layout` and the paint's; `lurq::app::profiler::take_frame_sections()` gives them, and `LURQ_FRAME_SECTIONS_LOG=<file>` writes their means every 120 drawn frames with the tree's size. Nothing is timed on a thread until one of the two asks: the first `take_frame_sections()` starts it. See [Frame sections](docs/src/content/docs/profiling.md#frame-sections).
- `ui::CodeBlock`'s code is one read-only input: a stop of Tab whose arrows move a caret, Shift selecting, Ctrl+A taking it all and Ctrl+C copying, and a selection across its lines when they wrap too. `framed(false)` draws it without its frame, `header(false)` without its bar, and `row_height(..)` sets how high a line is: the gallery's code views are code blocks now. See [Code Block](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#code-block).
- `lurq::code` tells apart the names of types and modules (`CodeTokenKind::Type`: a name before `::`, or one with a capital), the marks between names (`Punctuation`), block comments and characters, as the gallery's own code had them. Markdown's code blocks and `ui::CodeBlock` draw them in their roles.
- `TextInput::wrap(false)`: a multiline input whose lines do not wrap, as wide as its longest line. `TextInput::line_gutter(LineGutter)`: the numbers of a multiline input's lines and bands behind the lines picked out, drawn where it lays its lines out. See [styling and events](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#lines-of-code).
- `ui::CodeBlock`: a block of code to the design (`kyzrV`), its words in the colors of what they are, under a bar with its title or language and a button that copies it (`copy_label`, `copied_text`, `on_copy`; mounted, it shows a check for a while). Line numbers (`line_numbers`, `first_line`), lines picked out (`highlight_lines`), lines that scroll sideways or wrap (`wrap`), `min_height` and `max_height`, and a selection across the lines. See [Code Block](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#code-block).
- `ui::Blockquote`: a quote to the design (`mlxnG`), with a line at its start, its text and what it holds under it. See [Blockquote](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#blockquote).
- `lurq::code`: the tokens of a piece of code (`tokens`, `CodeToken`, `CodeTokenKind`, `knows`), which colored Markdown's Rust and now colors `ui::CodeBlock` too, without the `markdown` feature. Markdown's code blocks draw from it as before, and a language named in capitals (`Rust`) is colored now too.
- `WindowHandle::copy_to_clipboard(text)`: what a copy button does, written where a copy shortcut writes once its handler returns. See [styling and events](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#copy).
- The roles of a document: `TypographyStyle::Heading1` to `Heading6`, the headings of the design's Markdown and Prose (30 bold, 24, 20, 16 and 14 semibold, 12 semibold in capitals), and `Prose` (14 on lines of 1.6) and `Article` (15 on lines of 1.65), text read at length in the interface and in a document. The Tokens page of the gallery shows them. See [typography](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#typography).
- `TextStyle::case` with `TextCase::Upper` and `Lower`: a text drawn in capitals or in small letters, like CSS `text-transform`, while the text, what is copied of it and where a caret stands are as written. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md).
- `Gradient::corners(top_left, top_right, bottom_right, bottom_left)` (`GradientKind::Corners`): a box filled with the bilinear blend of the colors at its four corners, mixed in sRGB values, on wgpu and dx12 and in the DevTools screenshot. The area of `ui::ColorPicker` is one such box, where it was three stacked under its rounded clip, which let the hue show as a rim around the black of its bottom corners. See [four corners](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#four-corners).

- `lurq::eyedropper::EyeDropper`: a color picked from anywhere on the screen, `EyeDropper::new().pick(&window)` for `ctx.future_action`, giving `Ok(Some(color))`, `Ok(None)` where the person pressed Escape, or `Err(Unsupported)`. On Windows a loupe follows the pointer (the pixels around it magnified, the one taken outlined, its hex), a press or Enter takes the color, the arrows move by a pixel, across monitors and at any scale; on macOS the system's own sampler (`NSColorSampler`), the color given in sRGB; Linux, iOS and a browser say `Unsupported`. `EyeDropper::is_supported()` and `EyeDropper::stand_in(..)` for tests. The eyedropper of `ui::ColorPicker` (new, at the end of its fields) and of `ui::ColorField` picks with it, shown where the platform can (`eyedropper(bool)` hides it). See [a color from the screen](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#a-color-from-the-screen).

- `Field::mount(ctx)`: a field built where it is declared mounts its control, so a control that keeps state of its own between renders is the same in a field as outside it, with the field's label, description and error: a shortcut recorder records, a date field and a time field keep what is half typed, the date, date range and time pickers open, the month and year pickers keep the year shown, a password input's eye turns, a rating lights the stars under the pointer, a file upload and a drop zone show a drag, a listbox is a virtual list. A field converted into an element (`.into()`) builds its control as it is, as before, which keeps none of that. `FieldControl` has a method more, `mount_in_field(self, &InField, &mut Ctx)`, with a default (`in_field`): a control of an app's own that keeps state implements it. See [Field](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#field).
- `ui::ShortcutRecorder`: a box that holds a key combination (`Accelerator`), bound to a `Signal<Accelerator>` (`new`) or a `Signal<Option<Accelerator>>` (`optional`), or held by the app (`controlled`, `controlled_optional`). Mounted, a press or Enter records: it takes every key of the window, shows the modifiers held and takes the combination; Escape gives up, Backspace and its cross empty it. `validate` refuses a combination with a sentence, `require_modifier`, the platform's names of the keys, and every prop of a typed control. See [Shortcut Recorder](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#shortcut-recorder).
- `ui::DockLayout`, `ui::DockPanel` and `ui::DockState`: panels arranged in a tree of splits whose leaves are groups shown as tabs, around an area of documents, which a person rearranges: a tab, a header or a floating group dragged shows the indicator over the group under the pointer and squares on the layout's edges, and lands as a tab of a group, a group beside it or along an edge; let go over nothing it floats, outside the window `on_detach` is told, Escape gives it up. Panels float, hide into a strip at an edge and are shown from it, are maximized and closed (`on_close`, which may refuse), and the same is done from a tab's menu and the keys (F6 between groups). What a panel holds is kept while it moves, its caret, its scroll and its focus. The arrangement is a `DockState` the app holds (`new(Signal<DockState>, ..)`, `controlled`, `on_change`), always made valid, written readably for a first layout (`DockNode::row`, `column`, `group`, `documents`), changed by calls (`show`, `move_panel`, `move_group`, `close`, `put_back`, `maximize`, …) and stored as text (`to_text`, `parse`; a value of the persistent storage with `persistent_storage`). See [Dock Layout](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#dock-layout).
- `DocumentTabs::on_drag_away(Fn(&T, &DragEvent))`: told while a tab is dragged away from the bar and when it is let go there; the bar marks no place and moves nothing meanwhile. See [Document Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#document-tabs).
- The focus follows a control that a render moves under another parent, where its element ref or its text input's value signal finds it again; it was dropped. See [Keyboard And Focus](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#keyboard-and-focus).
- `ui::FloatingPanel`, `ui::FloatingPanels` and `ui::PanelHeader`: a tool window inside the app's window, moved by its title bar and resized by its edges and its corner, kept inside its room, collapsed to its title bar, closed by its button and Escape, its place and size the app's (`position`, `size`) or its own; several over one content, the one pressed in front; and the header of a panel on its own. A press in a panel or the focus coming into it makes it the active one, its title in the foreground; a press on the content or the focus going outside every panel leaves none active (`on_active_change`, and `FloatingPanels::active` with the key of the active panel or `None`). The grips take the arrow keys. See [Floating Panel](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#floating-panel).
- `.captures_keys(bool)` on every element: while it is in the tree it alone hears the keys of the window, and nothing the window does with a key is done, the accelerators of a Mac's menu included. See [Taking Every Key](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#taking-every-key).
- `Accelerator::parse`, `from_key_event`, `matches`, `has_modifier`, `new` and its `Display`: the key combination of the menu is the one of lurq, read and written as `Ctrl+Shift+P`. `SyntheticModifiers` and `Accelerator` are `Hash`.
- `.form_value(Option<Arc<str>>)` with `form`: what an element that is no engine control gives the form under its `name`. See [Submission](docs/src/content/docs/forms.md#submission).
- `ui::Rating`: a number of stars, bound to a `Signal<f64>` (`new`) or a `Signal<Option<f64>>` (`optional`), held by the app (`controlled`, `controlled_optional`) or shown and not chosen (`at`), in whole stars or halves, `clearable`, `read_only`, three sizes and an icon of its own. A slider to a screen reader, one stop of Tab with the arrows, Home, End and the digits; mounted, the stars under the pointer are lit as a press would set them. See [Rating](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#rating).
- `ui::icons::STAR_FILL`: a solid star in lurq's icon font, the shape a rating lights.
- `ui::icons::LIST_MEDIA`, `LIST_CHECK` and `LIST_PAIRS`: three glyphs of lurq's icon font for lists: rows with a picture before their lines, lines with a check, and names beside their values.
- `ui::icons::PANELS`: a glyph of lurq's icon font for a window of panels, a bar across its top and a panel at its side.
- Ten glyphs of lurq's icon font for charts and data: `icons::CHART_BAR`, `CHART_ROWS`, `CHART_LINE`, `CHART_AREA`, `CHART_SCATTER`, `CHART_PIE`, `CHART_RADAR`, `GRID`, `GAUGE` and `TRENDING_UP`.
- `ui::icons::FILTER` and `COLUMNS`: two glyphs of lurq's icon font for a grid's tools, a funnel and three columns.
- `Color::parse(&str)` reads a color a person typed (`#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa` with or without `#`, `rgb()`/`rgba()`, `hsl()`/`hsla()`, by commas or spaces) and gives `None` for what is no color; `Color::to_hsv()` and `to_hsl_values()` with `node::color::Hsv` and `Hsl` and `Color::from` both, `to_hex_upper()` and `to_rgb_string()`. See [colors](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#colors).

- `ui::SwatchPicker` and `ui::Swatch`: one color chosen among swatches in a grid, the board's panel of 24 swatches six to a row or the grid alone (`framed(false)`), bound to a `Signal<T>` (`new`), a `Signal<Option<T>>` (`optional`) or held by the app (`controlled`), `T` the color or the app's value a swatch carries (`Swatch::of`), `on_change(&T)` told a person's choice, `columns`, `size`, `label`, `invalid`, `disabled`, one stop of Tab with the arrows in two directions, a `listbox` of `option`s to tooling, and `FieldControl`.

- `ui::ColorPicker`: a color chosen in an area of saturation and brightness (a slider of two axes, its keys by 1% and by ten with Shift, PageUp and PageDown, Home and End), on a rail of hue and one of alpha over a checkerboard (`alpha`), from swatches (`swatch`, `swatches`), or typed in the fields of hex, RGB or HSL (`format`, and `formats` a button switches between); bound by `ColorPicker::new(Signal<Color>)` or held by the app with `controlled(color, on_change)`, heard by `on_change(Fn(Color))` as a thumb moves and `on_change_end` once a change is over; it keeps the hue a grey does not say; `bare` without its panel; `ColorFormat`, `ColorPickerPart`, `ColorPickerState`, `ColorPickerView` for a recipe and a renderer. See [design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#color-picker).

- `ui::ColorField`: a color typed in a box (hex, `rgb()`, `hsl()`, read on Enter and as the focus leaves, what is no color put back) or chosen in a color picker that opens in a popover under it from its swatch, its eyedropper or Down, the focus back in the box as it closes; `ColorField::new(Signal<Color>)`, `optional(Signal<Option<Color>>)` and `controlled`, generic over what it holds (`ColorField<Color>`, `ColorField<Option<Color>>`, `ui::FieldColor`), with every prop of a typed control and `FieldControl`; `ColorFieldSize` is `TextBoxSize`. See [design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#color-field).

- `ui::ColorSwatch`: one color as a square or a circle (`ColorSwatchShape`), of 20, 24 or 32 (`ColorSwatchSize`), over a checkerboard where it is not opaque, `selected`, a button with `on_click`, `label` (its hex where it has none), `disabled`. See [design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#color-swatch).

- **A lurq app runs in a browser.** The `winit` shell with the `wgpu` renderer builds for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` and draws to a canvas of the page through WebGPU, or through WebGL2 where the browser has no WebGPU (feature `webgl`): a `<canvas id="lurq">` where the page put it, or one added to the body that fills the viewport. A page asks for what a desktop gives at once, so `main` awaits the GPU device (`WgpuDevice::request()`, given to each engine with `WgpuRenderEngine::with_device`) and loads its fonts (`App::load_font`, `App::set_generic_family`) before `WinitWindow::run`, which hands the app to the browser's loop and returns. The gallery is the example: `python scripts/gallery-web.py --serve`. What differs from a desktop and what is not done yet (input methods, several windows, the features that are not built for it) is in `docs/src/content/docs/web.md`.
- `WgpuDevice` and `WgpuRenderEngine::with_device`: a GPU device made ahead of the engine that draws with it. A browser needs it; on a desktop it moves the wait for the device off the first frame. `WgpuDevice::request_with(backends)` asks among some backends only, and `WgpuDevice::backend()` says which the device draws through (`WgpuBackend`).
- The `webgl` feature: in a browser without WebGPU the `wgpu` renderer draws through WebGL2. What is drawn is the same; the quad shader reads its gradients out of a float texture there, for want of storage buffers, and a frame with a backdrop blur of the window is painted beside the surface, which WebGL does not let be copied from. It adds wgpu's own implementation to the page (the gallery's `.wasm` grew from 12 MB to 16 MB) and changes nothing outside a browser.
- `App::set_generic_family(generic, family)` names the font a generic family draws with, in place of the one chosen from the system's fonts; `GenericFamily::SansSerif` is also the font of text with no family.
- `RouterHandle::follow_address()`: in a browser the page's address follows the router. The router's path is the address's fragment (`index.html#/settings`), a `push` is an entry of the browser's history and a `replace` the same entry with another path, the browser's back and forward buttons move the router, an address a person types is navigated to through the routes' guards, and a page loaded with a path in its address starts there, so a reload stays on its screen. It does nothing outside a browser. The gallery follows its address.
- In a browser, a signal written between the loop's turns, by a promise of the app's own or a listener of the page, wakes the loop as a write from another thread does on a desktop. It was drawn at the next event.
- In a browser, `core::Date::today()` is today by the browser's clock and zone.
- `lurq::time`: `Instant`, `SystemTime`, `UNIX_EPOCH` and `Duration` as the platform has them. It is `std::time` everywhere but in a browser, where std's clocks panic and the page's clock is read instead. lurq reads the time through it.
- In a browser, `copy_to_clipboard` writes the system clipboard and a paste shortcut reads it through the browser's promise, the text arriving a turn of the loop later; images decode on the page's one thread.
- `lurq::ui`: the design system's components and what a theme styles them with. A component is a builder that goes where an element goes and takes the theme of what renders it (`ui::current_theme()`), so it needs no context passed to it and is built again when the theme changes.
- `ui::Recipe`: how the parts of a component look, case by case. A recipe is a list of rules, each a case and the style of a part in it: always (`base`), for a `variant`, for a `size`, in a window `at` a breakpoint or wider, in a `state` of the component, `hovered`, `pressed` or `focus_visible`, or several of these together (`when(Selector::new().variant(..).hovered(), ..)`). The rules that hold are laid the more specific over the less. Variants, sizes, states and parts are names, so an app adds its own.
- `ui::PartStyle`: the style of one part, every field optional: background, foreground, border, radius, shadow, a `veil` over the fill, outline or the theme's `focus_ring`, opacity, padding, gap, width and height (pixels or a step of the control, density or icon scale), the text (a typography role and any field over it, in one of the theme's two families or a named one) and transitions with the theme's durations and easings. Rules for the pointer and the keyboard become state styles of the node, so a hover renders nothing again.
- A theme says what it wants of a component: `Theme::patch_recipe::<C>(..)` lays rules over its recipe, `Theme::set_recipe::<C>(..)` gives it one in place of the design's, `Theme::set_renderer::<C>(..)` builds other nodes for the same behavior, and `Theme::set_part_renderer::<C, _>(part, ..)` builds one part. `ThemeDefinition::ui` (`ui::UiDefinition`) holds the same for a definition. `ThemeDefinition::bare()` and `Theme::bare()` have the design's tokens and no recipe: every component comes out unstyled and still works, as the base for a look that shares nothing with the design's.
- The rest of the design's buttons, each on the recipe and renderer of the theme: `ui::Toggle` (a button that stays on: `on(bool)`, `on_change`), `ui::ButtonGroup` (buttons joined in one line around them, or filled and a pixel apart: a split button), `ui::ToggleGroup` (toggles of which one, or any number, are on), `ui::SegmentedControl` (one of a few choices on a track), `ui::Link` (text that is pressed, underlined under the pointer) and `ui::Fab` (a floating action button). `ToggleGroup` and `SegmentedControl` bind a `Signal<T>`, or a `Signal<Vec<T>>` for any number, or are `controlled` by the app; a choice is `(value, "Label")` or a `ui::Choice` with an icon.
- `Button::description(text)`: a second line under the label, for the design's compound button. A button that is an icon alone is named by its label for tooling that reads the tree.
- `PartStyle::underline(border)`: a line along the bottom of a part. `ResolvedRecipe::rules_for(part, inner)`: the rules a component that holds another gives it for its place.
- `ui::Icon`: an icon by name, `Icon::new(icons::SEARCH)`. It is a square of a step of the theme's icon sizes (16 by default, or a number of pixels), is drawn in the foreground of what it is in unless it names a color, and turns with `.spin(true)`. `ui::icons` has a constant for each of the 152 icons of lurq's font. The font is loaded, hinted, in every app: `App::load_hinted_font` is for an app's own.
- The theme's icons: `ThemeIcons`, what each name of an icon is drawn with (`IconGlyph`, a character of a font). `Theme::set_icon(name, glyph)` gives a name another glyph or adds a name, `Theme::icon(name)` reads it, and `ThemeDefinition` has the table (`icons`). lurq's components name the icons they draw, so a theme changes them too.
- `ui::SPIN`: the keyframes of a full turn, registered in phase in every window, which a spinning icon plays once in `ui::SPIN_PERIOD`. Keyframe identifiers from `0xFF00` up are lurq's own.
- `ui::Button` is the design's whole button: sizes `Xs` (28) and `Xl` (44) beside `Sm`, `Md` and `Lg`, each with its padding, label and icon; `icon(name)` and `trailing_icon(name)`, an icon of the theme's set in the color of the label; `icon_only(true)`, a square; `shape(ButtonShape::Pill)`, a pill, and a circle for an icon alone; `loading(true)`, a spinner in the place of the icon and no press until it is over. Its recipe has the parts `ButtonPart::Icon` and `ButtonPart::Spinner` and the states `ButtonState::Loading`, `IconOnly` and `Pill`.
- The theme is at a density: `Theme::set_density(Density::Compact)` and `Theme::density()`, `Default` unless set, and part of a `ThemeDefinition` (`density`). A recipe has rules for one, `Recipe::density(density, part, style)` and `Selector::density(..)`, which hold while the theme is at it, between the rules for a size and those for a breakpoint; a component is built again when the density changes. What draws rows of its own reads `theme.density_value(theme.density())`. The design's rows follow the density, its controls keep their sizes: an item of a `Menu` (so of a `ContextMenu`, a `Menubar`'s menus and a `CommandPalette`), an option of a `Select`, a `Combobox` and a `MultiSelect`, a row of a `TreeView`, a tab of `Tabs`, an `Accordion`'s header and a `Sidebar`'s item move from the design's height by `ui::ROW_STEPS`, 8 lower at compact and 8 and 12 higher at comfortable and touch (a row of 32 is 24, 40 and 44). `Recipe::row_density(selector, part, height)` and `Recipe::by_density(selector, part, style)` write such rules.
- `WindowControls::hover_foreground(kind, color)`: the foreground one control has under the pointer and while pressed, for a close control that turns white on its red. A control's glyph is drawn in the foreground of the control, as content of `WindowControlContent::element` that names no color is.
- `SliderPartStyle::outline(Outline)`: a ring around a part of the engine's slider, its track, its fill or its thumb, so that `thumb_focused` can be a focus ring.
- Focus groups: `.focus_group(FocusGroup::horizontal())` on an element makes the arrow keys, Home and End move the focus between the stops of Tab inside it. `FocusGroup::vertical()` and `both()` choose the arrows, `wrap(bool)` whether the ends meet, `activate(bool)` presses what the focus moves to (a radio group's choice follows its focus), and `one_stop(bool)` makes Tab stop in the group once, at the element marked `.focus_entry(true)` or the first. A text input, a slider and a select keep their arrow keys. An element of a group that is disabled while it has the focus passes the focus to the element before it in the group (or the one after it), and one that leaves the tree while its group stays to the stop that took its place (the next or the last).
- `ui::RadioGroup` and `ui::SegmentedControl` are one stop of Tab whose arrow keys move the choice; `ui::ToggleGroup` is one stop whose arrow keys move between its toggles; the arrow keys move between the buttons of a `ui::ButtonGroup`. `RadioGroup::orientation(Orientation::Horizontal)` lays the radios side by side.
- `ui::CheckboxGroup<T>`: checkboxes over one list, bound to a `Signal<Vec<T>>` or held by the app, which is told the whole new set (`on_change(&[T])`), one under the other or side by side (`orientation`), with `label`, `state`, `rules`, `force` and a `CheckboxGroupView` for a renderer.
- The controls of the design system (a button, a toggle, a segment, a link, the track of a switch) are stops of Tab wherever they are, in the order of the tree; a plain `components::Button` stays one only in a form or with a `tab_index`.
- `ui::Switch`: a setting that is on or off. A track with a thumb that slides to the end it is at, a label beside it that is pressed as the track is, `label_hidden` for the track alone, the parts `Root`, `Track`, `Thumb`, `Label` and the states `On`, `Disabled` for a recipe, and `SwitchView::track()`, `thumb()` and `travel()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Select`: one value chosen from a list that opens under a trigger shaped like an input. The engine's select dressed by a recipe, bound to a `Signal<T>` (`new`) or a `Signal<Option<T>>` (`optional`), or held by the app (`controlled(value, options, on_change)`, whose trigger shows what the app answers with), with `(value, label)` pairs or `SelectOption`s; `on_change(&T)`, told a person's choice and not what the app sets, of the type of the value: a `Select<T>` is generic over it (`()` by default), so a handler of another type does not compile. An input group's select and a pagination's page size take a select of any value (`ui::AnySelect`), a toolbar takes one as an item; the sizes of an input (`SelectSize` is `TextBoxSize`) and its judgments (`invalid`, `warning`, `valid`), `read_only` (the list does not open), `placeholder`, `full_width` and `disabled`, the parts `Trigger`, `Value`, `Placeholder`, `Chevron`, `Panel`, `Item`, `Check`, `Detail`, `Hint`, `Group`, `Separator` and the states `Open`, `Invalid`, `Warning`, `Valid`, `ReadOnly`, `Disabled`, `FullWidth`, and of an item `Selected` and `Disabled`, for a recipe, and `SelectView::select_style()` and `select(style)` for a renderer.
- `ui::Switch`: a setting that is on or off. A track with a thumb that slides to the end it is at, a label beside it that is pressed as the track is, `label_hidden` for the track alone, `bind(Signal<bool>)`, `invalid` (and in a field with an error), the parts `Root`, `Track`, `Thumb`, `Label` and the states `On`, `Invalid`, `Disabled` for a recipe, and `SwitchView::track()`, `thumb()` and `travel()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Select`: one value chosen from a list that opens under a trigger shaped like an input. The engine's select dressed by a recipe, bound to a `Signal<T>` with `(value, label)` pairs or `SelectOption`s, with the sizes and the judgments of an input (`invalid`, `warning`, `valid`), `placeholder`, `full_width` and `disabled`, the parts `Trigger`, `Value`, `Placeholder`, `Chevron`, `Menu`, `Item`, `Check`, `Detail`, `Hint`, `Group`, `Separator` and the states `Open`, `Invalid`, `Warning`, `Valid`, `Disabled`, `FullWidth`, `Chosen`, `ItemDisabled` for a recipe, and `SelectView::select_style()` and `select(style)` for a renderer.
- A field finishes its control: an `Input`, a `Textarea` or a `Select` in it is called by its label and judged as it is, and a press of the label focuses the control. Every control of the design system a form holds takes from a field what it has a prop for (`FieldControl`, `InField`), and a field's description or error is its control's description to what reads the tree. `Field::disabled`; `Textarea` has `read_only`, the sizes `Sm`, `Md`, `Lg` (`TextareaSize`) and `warning` and `valid`; `Input::label`, `CheckboxGroup::label`, `RadioGroup::label`.
- `ui::Slider::orientation` and `length`, and `ui::RangeSlider`: the board's vertical slider, and two thumbs on one track bound to a `Signal<(i32, i32)>` or a `Signal<(f32, f32)>`, drawn by the slider's recipe in the state `SliderState::Range`. `SliderView::values()` and `is_range()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Popover`: a panel beside the button that opens it, over the window, with a title, a description and anything in it. `trigger(button)` makes a button its anchor and its toggle; it takes the focus as it opens and gives it back as it closes, is in the order of Tab right after its trigger, fades in and out, and closes on a press outside it and on Escape. `placement`, `width`, `offset`, `collision`, `dismiss_on_escape`, `dismiss_on_outside_press` and `inline`, the parts `Panel`, `Text`, `Title`, `Description`, `Body` for a recipe, and `PopoverView::panel()` and `text()` for a renderer. `Popover::new(open, anchor)` takes the signal first.
- `ui::Menu` and `ui::MenuItem`: a list of commands in a panel over the window, opened from an anchor, with icons, shortcuts, separators, headings, checked, radio, destructive and disabled items and submenus. It takes the focus as it opens and gives it back as it closes; the arrow keys and typing a letter move between its items, Right and Left go into and out of a submenu, and choosing one (which closes it before its handler is called), a press outside, Escape and Tab close it (`Menu::new(open, anchor)`, `Menu::controlled(open, anchor, on_open_change)`, `Menu::heading`, `Menu::label`, `Menu::on_dismiss`, `Menu::dismiss_on_escape`, `Menu::dismiss_on_outside_press`, `Menu::offset`, `Menu::collision`, `MenuItem::on_select`, `MenuItem::submenu`).
- `ui::Dialog`: a panel over a veil that covers the window, the engine's `Modal` dressed by a recipe, with a title, a description, a body, a footer of buttons, a close button, the sizes `Sm`, `Md`, `Lg`, `Full`, and a form whose body scrolls between a header and a footer that stay. It takes the focus as it opens and gives it back as it closes; Escape and a press on the veil close it.
- `ui::Tooltip`: a short text beside an element while the pointer rests on it or the keyboard's focus is on it, mounted on that element with `mount(ctx, element)`. It shows after `TOOLTIP_DELAY`, at once within `TOOLTIP_SKIP_DELAY` of another and for the keyboard's focus on the element or anything in it; the pointer can be moved onto it; it goes after the pointer leaves, on a press and on Escape, which it takes before what is under it; a disabled element shows it too; it is kept in the window, its arrow pointing at the element. `shortcut`, `side` (`TooltipSide`), `arrow`, `delay`, `open` and `disabled`, the parts `Root`, `Content`, `Label`, `Shortcut`, `Arrow` and the states `Arrow`, `Shortcut`, `Multiline` for a recipe, and `TooltipView::content()`, `arrow()` and `aim` for a renderer.
- `ui::Toast` and `ui::Toasts`: a short message in a corner of the window that leaves by itself. The panel with an icon by its variant (`ToastVariant`: `Neutral`, `Info`, `Success`, `Warning`, `Destructive`) or one of its own or none (`icon(impl Into<IconChoice>)`), a title and a description, an action (a `Button` or a `Link`), a close control (`close_button`) and a timer; and a handle that shows toasts from anywhere, with what it mounts (`Toasts::mount(ctx)`) once in the app's root, that stacks them, holds their time under the pointer, animates them in and out and takes them away after their duration, on a swipe, or on a press. `ui::Button` and `ui::Link` are `Clone`.
- `ui::Tabs`: a list of tabs of which one is selected, with the panel of the selected one, as a muted rail, a row over a line with counts, or pills, along the top or down the side; one stop of Tab whose arrow keys select, an icon, a count, a disabled tab, a divider, and an overflowing form whose list scrolls between two buttons with a menu of every tab; `PartStyle::start_line`.
- `ui::Accordion` and `ui::Collapsible`: headers that open what is under them, one at a time or any number, bound to a signal or held by the app, with Tab and the arrow keys; what opens grows to the height of its content and back, as a transition of `Height` between a number and none now does.
- `ui::Breadcrumb`: the way to where one is, its steps links that a press or Enter follows and the last the page; a chevron or a slash between them, an icon on a step, and with more steps than `max_items` an ellipsis that opens a menu of the hidden ones.
- `ui::Pagination`: the pages of a list with one current, counted from 1, bound to a `Signal<usize>` (`new`, `for_items`) or held by the app (`controlled`). The first and the last page, the current one with `siblings` on each side and an ellipsis where pages are left out, in the same number of slots wherever the current page is (`Pagination::pages_shown`); previous and next disabled at the ends; each a stop of Tab with Left and Right moving between them, a `navigation` with pages called "Page 3" and `a11y_current` on the current one. A `compact` form with "1–20 of 284" or "Page 3 of 12", a `page_size` slot for a `ui::Select` of how many to a page, `summary` and `label` for other words, the parts `Root`, `Nav`, `Item`, `Current`, `Ellipsis`, `EllipsisIcon`, `Summary`, `PageSizeLabel`, `PageSize` and the states `Compact`, `PageSize`, `Disabled` for a recipe, and `PaginationView` for a renderer.
- `ui::Tooltip`: a short text beside an element while the pointer rests on it or the keyboard's focus is on it, mounted on that element with `mount(ctx, element)`. It shows after `TOOLTIP_DELAY`, at once within `TOOLTIP_SKIP_DELAY` of another and for the keyboard's focus on the element or anything in it; the pointer can be moved onto it; it goes after the pointer leaves, on a press and on Escape, which it takes before what is under it; a disabled element shows it too; it is kept in the window, its arrow pointing at the element. `shortcut`, `placement`, `offset`, `collision`, `arrow`, `open_delay`, `close_delay`, `force_open` and `disabled`, the parts `Root`, `Panel`, `Label`, `Shortcut`, `Arrow`, the side it is on (`TooltipSide`) as the variant and the states `Arrow`, `Shortcut`, `Multiline` for a recipe, and `TooltipView::panel()`, `arrow()` and `aim` for a renderer.
- `ui::Toast` and `ui::Toasts`: a short message in a corner of the window that leaves by itself. The panel with an icon by its variant (`Neutral`, `Success`, `Info`, `Warning`, `Error`), a title and a description, an action (a `Button` or a `Link`), a close control and a timer; and a handle that shows toasts from anywhere, with a host the app's root renders once, that stacks them, holds their time under the pointer, animates them in and out and takes them away after their duration, on a swipe, or on a press. `ui::Button` and `ui::Link` are `Clone`.
- `ui::Tabs`: a list of tabs of which one is selected, with the panel of the selected one, as a muted rail, a row over a line with counts, or pills, along the top or down the side; one stop of Tab whose arrow keys select, an icon, a count, a disabled tab, a separator, and an overflowing form whose list scrolls between two buttons with a menu of every tab, kept between renders by `mount(ctx)`; told the tab a person selects by `on_change`; `PartStyle::start_line`.
- `ui::Accordion` and `ui::Collapsible`: headers that open what is under them, one at a time or any number, bound to a signal (`Accordion::optional`, `Accordion::multiple`, `Collapsible::bind`) or held by the app (`controlled_optional`, `controlled_multiple`), and told what is open after a press: an accordion's `on_change` the item or none, or the whole set, a collapsible's `on_open_change` whether it is open; with Tab and the arrow keys; what opens grows to the height of its content and back, as a transition of `Height` between a number and none now does.
- `ui::Breadcrumb`: the way to where one is, its steps links that a press or Enter follows and the last the page; a chevron or a slash between them, an icon on a step, a step that is `disabled`, and with more steps than `max_shown` an ellipsis that opens a menu of the hidden ones, which a mounted breadcrumb (`mount(ctx)`) keeps open or closed; `label` and `more_label` name it and its ellipsis.
- `ui::Pagination`: the pages of a list with one current, counted from 1, bound to a `Signal<usize>` (`new`, `for_items`) or held by the app (`controlled`). The first and the last page, the current one with `siblings` on each side and an ellipsis where pages are left out, in the same number of slots wherever the current page is (`Pagination::pages_shown`); previous and next disabled at the ends; each a stop of Tab with Left and Right moving between them, a `navigation` with pages called "Page 3" and `a11y_current` on the current one. A `compact` form with "1–20 of 284" or "Page 3 of 12", a `page_size` slot for a `ui::Select` of how many to a page, `summary_text`, `previous_label`, `next_label`, `page_label` and `label` for other words, `force` for a gallery, the parts `Root`, `Nav`, `Item`, `Ellipsis`, `EllipsisIcon`, `Summary`, `PageSizeLabel`, `PageSize` and the states `Compact`, `PageSize`, `Disabled` and `Current` (of an item) for a recipe, and `PaginationView` for a renderer.
- `ui::Steps`: the stages of a process in order with one current, the steps before it done with a check and a line in the primary color up to it; a description, an icon or a mark of what went wrong on a step (`Step::invalid`), in a row or in a column, and steps that are done pressed to go back to them, with Tab and the arrow keys. `Steps::new(signal, steps)` sets the signal on a press and `Steps::controlled(index, steps, on_change)` leaves the answer to the app, and `Steps::at(index, steps)` only shows the steps, which nothing presses; `on_change` hears a person change the step.
- `ui::Progress` and `ui::RingProgress`: how far a piece of work is, as a bar along a rail in four colors and three sizes with a label and its value, a bar that does not know how far it is with a segment crossing its rail (still while motion is reduced), the thin bar across the top of a window, and a ring with the value in its middle whose arc moves round; a width or a height in percent, and a border's dashes, now transition and animate.
- `ui::Badge`: a short label of a state or a count in seven variants and three sizes, round or square, with an icon or a dot before its text, a dot alone, a count with a maximum ("99+") that is nothing at 0, and a badge on the top end corner of another element (the design's Indicator).
- `ui::Tag` and `ui::Chip`: a tag is a label on a thing in five colors and three sizes, with an icon, round as a pill (`TagShape`), and a remove button called "Remove" and its text (`remove_label`) that Tab reaches and Backspace or Delete press; a chip is a tag that is pressed, on or off, bound to a `Signal<bool>` or told of a press, and `ui::ChipGroup` makes several one choice of one or of any number, with one stop of Tab and the arrow keys; `lurq_inspect` reports a node's `a11y_pressed` as `state.pressed`.
- `ui::Avatar` and `ui::AvatarGroup`: who a person is, as initials on a fill, a picture clipped to the avatar's shape (with the initials while it decodes and when it fails) or an icon, in five sizes, round or square, with a dot of presence; and avatars laid over one another, ringed, with the count of those a group leaves out.
- `ui::Card` and `ui::Separator`: a surface with a title, a description and an action in its header, its body and a footer of buttons, outlined, elevated or filled, in two sizes, and pressed as a whole with Tab and the focus ring where the app gives it `on_click`; and a line across a column or down a row, with a word in its middle.
- `ui::Spinner` and `ui::LoadingOverlay`: a ring, dots or bars that say something is being done, at three sizes, in the color of the text around it, with a label beside it, a status to tooling; and a veil with a spinner over a part of the page while it loads, which keeps it from the pointer and from Tab. `Button::loading` turns a `Spinner`.
- `ui::Skeleton`: a line, a circle, a block and a paragraph in the shape of what is loading, as wide as the room they have or as the app sizes them, pulsing or with a shimmer and still where motion is reduced, with `Skeleton::status` to say once what a group of them stands for.
- `ui::NumberInput`: a number typed or stepped, bound to a `Signal<f64>`, a `Signal<Option<f64>>` (an emptied field is none) or the app's own value, with a range and a step of any number (`impl Into<f64>`), a precision, a format (`ui::NumberFormat`: prefix, suffix, grouping, a locale's separators, or the app's formatter and parser), the board's minus and plus at its ends or a stacked pair at its end, the arrow keys, Page Up and Page Down, Home and End, and buttons that repeat while held; every element has `repeat_press`, a press that runs its click again while it is held, and `TextInput::keep_text_while_focused` keeps what is half typed when a render binds the input to another signal. `NumberInput::controlled(value, on_change)` is the app's own value; `on_change` hears each change a person makes and `on_change_end` the end of one (Enter or the focus leaving after typing, each step of a key or a button); `increase_label` and `decrease_label` name the buttons, whose ids are the input's with `-increase` and `-decrease`; the parts are `Root`, `Control`, `Placeholder`, `Buttons`, `Decrease`, `Increase`, `Separator`, `Icon`.
- `ui::InputOtp`, a one-time code of the design system: cells of digits, or letters and digits, in groups with a dash between, that a person types, pastes or has filled in by the platform as one field, and that a form and tooling read as one text input whose value is the code; `on_change(&str)` as a person changes it, `on_complete` when the last cell is filled (each handler given is called), a mask, the sizes and judgments of an input, and `FieldControl`.
- `ui::PasswordInput`: an input whose text is masked, with an eye at its end that shows it and hides it again (a `bool` and `on_reveal`, a bound `Signal<bool>`, or kept by the input mounted with `mount(ctx)`), and a meter of the password's strength in four bars with what it says of it; a masked text input no longer copies or cuts its value.
- `ui::SearchField`: an input for a query with the search icon before its text, a clear button while it holds something (`clearable`), Escape that empties it and Enter that submits it, and the hint of the key that reaches it; a `searchbox` to tooling.
- `ui::InputGroup`: an input joined with texts, icons, a `Button` and a `Select` before and after it (`leading`, `trailing`) into one box, with the group's corners at its ends and a line between neighbours.
- `ui::NumberInput`: a number typed or stepped, bound to a `Signal<f64>`, a `Signal<Option<f64>>` (an emptied field is none) or the app's own value, with a range, a step, a precision, a format (`ui::NumberFormat`: prefix, suffix, grouping, a locale's separators, or the app's formatter and parser), the board's minus and plus at its ends or a stacked pair at its end, the arrow keys, Page Up and Page Down, Home and End, and buttons that repeat while held; every element has `repeat_press`, a press that runs its click again while it is held, and `TextInput::keep_text_while_focused` keeps what is half typed when a render binds the input to another signal.
- `ui::InputOtp`, a one-time code of the design system: cells of digits, or letters and digits, in groups with a dash between, that a person types, pastes or has filled in by the platform as one field, and that a form and tooling read as one text input whose value is the code; `on_complete` when the last cell is filled, a mask, the sizes and judgments of an input, and `FieldControl`.
- `ui::CheckboxCard` and `ui::CheckboxCardGroup`: a checkbox whose whole card is pressed, with a title, a description and an icon or a price, its box the checkbox's own, edged in the primary color while it is checked, bound to a `Signal<bool>` with `bind`; and cards over one list bound to a signal or held by the app, told the whole new set, one under another or side by side (`orientation`).
- `ui::RadioCard` and `ui::RadioCardGroup`: a radio whose whole card is pressed, its mark the radio's own (`selected`, `on_change`); and cards that are one choice bound to a `Signal<T>` or a `Signal<Option<T>>`, told the value selected (`on_change(&T)`), one stop of Tab that the arrow keys move the choice through, as in a group of radios.
- `ui::Fieldset`: fields under a legend and its description, in an edged box, that a disabled fieldset takes out of input together with anything else in it.
- `ui::ErrorSummary` and `ui::FieldError`: what is wrong in a form, at its top, with a line for each field that a press or Enter takes the focus to; `ErrorSummary::form` makes one from a `FormHandle`'s errors once a submit was attempted. A button given `.focuses(&ElementRef)` gives the focus on when it is clicked or pressed from the keyboard; it focused itself.
- `ui::Combobox`: a select a person types in, whose list what is typed filters (by label without regard to case, or by the app's function), with the focus in the box the whole time, Down, Up, Enter, Escape twice and Tab, options an app fetches as one types (`on_query`), a row of no results (`empty_text`) and one of loading (`loading_text`), custom values, `on_change(&T)`, `on_submit(&str)` on Enter that chooses nothing, a cross that clears (`clearable`, `clear_label`), `read_only`, `optional` and `controlled` as a select has them, generic over its value as a select is (`Combobox<T>`), and a select's options, groups, sizes, judgments, parts (`Panel`) and states (`Selected`); the engine's `Select` has a combobox mode (`Select::combobox`, `free_text`, `status`), and its list says to tooling that it is a listbox of options.
- `ui::MultiSelect`: several values chosen from a list that what is typed filters, held as removable tags in a box that grows with them, with a list of checkboxes that stays open while one chooses, Backspace taking the last value away, a count past `max_shown`, a clear control (`clear_label`), `on_change(&[T])` told the whole new set, generic over its values (`MultiSelect<T>`), `controlled(values, options, on_change)`, its own `new` and `controlled` as the `multiple` of a select, `read_only` (no cross on the tags), and a combobox's keys, sizes (`MultiSelectSize`), states (`MultiSelectState`), judgments and `on_query`, `empty_text`.
- `ui::TokenInput`: a box of text, on one line or on rows, in which the tokens the app finds stand as chips in the colors of a tag, with a cross that takes one away as an undoable edit; atomic to the caret and to Backspace, and a `textbox` of the plain text to tooling. The engine's input tokens have what it needs: `InputTokenStyle::padding_end`, `height`, `border`, `text_style`, `icon(InputTokenIcon)`, `hovered` and `selected`, `InputToken::atomic`, `TextInput::anchor_at` (an element the layout puts at a byte, for an overlay) and `TextInputController::caret_point`.
- `ui::TokenInput::list(Signal<Vec<String>>)`: a token input over a list, where the tokens and what is typed are two things. Each item is a token, and what is typed is a draft that is no part of the list until Enter or one of `separators(..)` makes an item of it; a pasted list adds each of its items. `draft(Signal<String>)` holds what is typed, `unique(bool)` keeps an item from being added twice, `commit_on_blur(bool)` makes an item of the draft when the focus leaves, `token(|item, token| ..)` dresses each item's token, and `on_change(&[String])` and `on_draft(&str)` say what a person did. `TokenInput::new(Signal<String>)` stays for a text whose tokens the app finds, as a template's variables are.
- `ui::Mentions` and `MentionsController`: a token input in which a trigger (`@`, `#`, `/`) opens a list of `Mention`s under it, narrowed to what is typed or found by a function, chosen with Up, Down and Enter or a click and put away with Escape; what is chosen stands as an atomic mention token.
- `ui::ListItem` and `ui::List`: rows of a list with a title, a description, what stands before them (an avatar, an icon, a checkbox, a radio) and at their end (a value, a note, a switch, keys, a chevron), pressed or not, a path in the router, a height for each density with the mobile row under a finger, lines between rows from an inset (`separators`, `inset`), and the contained list in a box with a header (`title`, `action`); `ListItem::on_click` is given the press; `router::current_navigator` for a builder that goes somewhere.
