- `ui::Listbox`: a list in the page of which one option or many are chosen, bound to a `Signal<T>` (`Listbox::new`), a `Signal<Option<T>>` (`Listbox::optional`) or a `Signal<Vec<T>>` (`Listbox::multiple`), or held by the app (`controlled`, `controlled_optional`, `controlled_multiple`), its `on_change` told `&T`, `&Option<T>` or `&[T]` as the form it was made with holds, and what a select takes, with groups, disabled options, and the keys of a listbox: one stop, the choice following the focus or waiting for Space, the page keys, typeahead, and ranges with Shift, Ctrl with a press and Ctrl+A, and `Listbox::mount` for thousands of options in a virtual list that the keys reach every option of; the engine's `FocusGroup::pages` and `FocusGroup::shift_extends`, which a group of `FocusRows` honors too, and one model of ranges in `ui::selection` that the tree view now shares.
- `ui::DataList`: terms with their values, side by side with the terms as wide as the widest or one over the other (`orientation(Orientation)`), a value a text or any element.
- `ui::Timeline` and `ui::TimelineItem`: what happened, in its order, each item a mark on a line that runs down to the next beside its title, description and time, which wrap; the mark a dot in the tone of the item's `variant`, ringed where it is `current` and hollow where it is `upcoming`, an `icon` in a well, the app's `media`, or a spinner while it is `pending`; content under an item (`child`), an `action`, and an item pressed with `on_select`; a `list` of `listitem`s to tooling; across (`orientation`) in a scroll area, a small size whose items are rows of the density, the text on alternate sides or the time in a column of its own (`align`), the timeline's `on_select(Fn(&T))` told an item's `value` with the pressed items one stop of Tab, and a history loaded a page at a time (`on_load_more`, `loading`).
- `ui::Timeline::mount`: a timeline that reads its own room, its alternate sides folding to one under `Timeline::ALTERNATE_LEAST` in a narrow panel of a wide window too, with no frame of the other form; built as an element it follows the window's breakpoint.
- `ElementRef::observe_rect_before_paint`: an observer of an element's rect whose changes are rendered and laid out again before the frame is painted, once a frame: a component whose form follows its own room shows the form that fits in the frame that laid it out.
- `ui::BarList` and `ui::BarListItem`: named values as bars of one scale in the board's card, the scale rounded up from the largest value, the values written by `value_text`, rows that follow the density, and rows pressed as one stop of Tab told by `on_select(Fn(&str))`.
- `ui::Meter`: a value of a known range in segments, each filled one in the tone of its part of the range (`low`, `high`, `optimum`, as a web page's meter judges them) or of its `variant`, its value written by `value_text`, a `meter` to tooling.
- `ui::Sparkline` (with `canvas`): a series as a line with no axes on the canvas, a line at 0 where the values are on both sides of it, a dot for one value, an `img` to tooling with a summary and a point for each value.
- `ui::StatCard`: one number with what it is, its change as a badge in its tone and what it is measured against, a sparkline or any element beside it (`media`), in the board's two sizes.
- `ui::Gauge` (with `canvas`): a value as the share of a half ring with its reading in it and its range under it, the share in the tone of its part of the range as a meter's, a `meter` to tooling.
- `ui::Progress` and `ui::RingProgress` say their numbers as `Meter` and `Gauge` do: `format(Fn(f32, f32))` is `value_text(Fn(f64) -> String)`, given the value as it is; a range from `min`; values in `f64`; `ProgressVariant::Default` is `Neutral`, with `Info` beside it; a bar's `description` under its rail; and to tooling its `min`, its numbers written with the decimals they have, and its description.
- `ScrollHorizontal::fill_width` and `ui::ScrollArea::fill_width`: what scrolls across is at least as wide as its viewport, and items that grow share it.
- A row or a column with no bound on its main axis but a least length (what a scroll that fills its viewport gives it) grows its growing children into that length, as a flex container with a least width does on the web.
- `ui::Navbar`: the way round a site or an app along the top of its window, its brand (`NavbarBrand`), its links (`NavItem`, of which one may open a panel of links) and what stands at its end, sticky where told. Its links are a `NavigationMenu` in its links variant; on a window narrower than `md` they go behind a menu button into a `Sidebar` over the page. See [Navbar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#navbar).
- `ui::NavigationMenu`: a row of links, some of which open a panel of links (`NavPanel` of `NavLink`s in columns, with a featured element) under them, by a press, under the pointer after a delay and from the keyboard, the open panel handed to its neighbour by the pointer and by Left and Right. Each item is a stop of Tab. The engine's part is `FocusGroup::panel_row`. See [Navigation Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#navigation-menu).
- `ui::Table` and `ui::TableColumn`: rows in columns that line up whatever is in a cell, from the engine's new shared column tracks (`ColumnTracks`, `.column_tracks`, `.measures_column_tracks`); sorting the app holds, selection with checkboxes, Ctrl and Shift, a header that stays while the rows scroll and a sticky first column (`ScrollBoth::fill_width`, and elements revealed clear of what sticks), rows built in view only in a mounted table of thousands, densities, empty and loading forms, a footer with a count and a pagination, the keys of a table of records, and Tab from a focused row into its buttons in a one-stop focus group.
- `ui::Calendar`: the days of a month to choose a date or a range from (two presses, the pointer showing where it would end), bound to a `Signal<Option<Date>>` or a `Signal<Option<DateRange>>` or `controlled` by the app, with one `on_change` told of another date or range, one or several months, the least and greatest dates and the app's days that cannot be chosen, week numbers, the locale's names and first day, the keys of a grid of days on the engine's new `KeyboardEvent::focus_key`, and `DateMemory` for what it keeps; on lurq's own dates (`core::Date`, whose line is in `CHANGELOG.md` already).
- `ui::DatePicker`: a field that holds a date, typed in the locale's short form, its numbers or ISO 8601 and read as it is typed, or chosen from a calendar in a popover from its button or Down; `format` and `parse` of the app's own, clearable, an input's sizes and judgments, and in a `Field` like an input; `controlled`, `on_input`, `on_submit`, `on_open_change` and `on_dismiss`, the popover's `placement`, `offset` and `collision`, `inline`, and the names of its controls (`trigger_label`, `clear_label`, `panel_label`).
- `ui::DateRangePicker`: a date picker over a range in one field, with presets beside the calendar (`preset`, `presets`) and one or two months.
- `ui::DateField` and `ui::TimeField`: a date or a time typed in by its segments, each a spin button and a stop of Tab: digits that fill a segment and go on, Up and Down round its range (the minutes by a `step`), Home, End and Backspace; the value is a date or a time only once every segment is filled in the range. `new`, `optional`, `controlled` and `controlled_optional`, a time field's `seconds` and `hour_cycle`, the locale's order, separators, names and placeholders, and in a `Field` like an input.
- `ui::TimePicker`: a field that holds a time, typed in it or chosen from a popover of columns of hours, of minutes a `step` apart and on a clock of twelve hours of the halves of the day.
- `ui::MonthPicker` and `ui::YearPicker`: the months of a year, or twelve years from a decade, in a grid under a heading whose buttons show the page before and after, with a calendar's keys.
- `core::Time`, `DayPeriod` and `HourCycle`: a time of day with no date and no zone, ISO 8601 read and written, a clock of twelve hours, round the clock arithmetic and the system's now. `DateText` answers the time (`hour_cycle`, `format_time`, `parse_time`) and what a field of segments and a month or year picker ask, each with a default, and `DateLocale` in its seven languages. See [dates](docs/src/content/docs/dates.md#the-time-of-day).
- `ui::TreeView` and `ui::TreeItem`: things inside things, a file tree, with folders and files, an open icon, what stands at a row's end, disabled rows, children an app brings when a row is first opened, guide lines, one selected or many with Ctrl and Shift, activation by a double press or Enter, and the keys of a tree from the engine's new `FocusGroup::nested` with `focus_level` and `focus_expanded`, which a sidebar's nested items use too; typeahead in a long group no longer searches the group for each element. `TreeView::mount(ctx)` puts a tree of thousands of rows in the virtual list with every key reaching every row, and `TreeItem::trailing_on_hover` shows what is at a row's end only while the row is pointed at or focused. A row opens and closes as an accordion's item does, in either form: what it holds slides open under it (`TreeViewPart::Panel` and `Body`) and what is below moves with it, its chevron turns (it is the down one, `icons::CHEVRON_DOWN`, turned to the right while its row is closed), a press on the way turns it round, the keys and typeahead pass by the rows of a closing row, and a mounted tree opens a row of thousands building only the rows in view; at once where motion is reduced.
- `ui::TokenInput::list(Signal<Vec<String>>)`: a token input over a list, where the tokens and what is typed are two things. Each item is a token, and what is typed is a draft that is no part of the list until Enter or one of `separators(..)` makes an item of it; a pasted list adds each of its items. `query(Signal<String>)` holds what is typed, `unique(bool)` keeps an item from being added twice, `commit_on_blur(bool)` makes an item of the draft when the focus leaves, `dress_token(|item, token| ..)` dresses each item's token, and `on_change(&[String])` and `on_query(&str)` say what a person did. `TokenInput::new(Signal<String>)` stays for a text whose tokens the app finds with `find_tokens(..)`, as a template's variables are.
- `ui::Mentions` and `MentionsController`: a token input in which a trigger (`@`, `#`, `/`) opens a list of `Mention`s under it, narrowed to what is typed or found by a function, chosen with Up, Down and Enter or a click and put away with Escape; what is chosen stands as an atomic mention token. A `Mention` has `id`, `key` and `disabled`; the sizes are `MentionsSize`.
- `ui::ListItem` and `ui::List`: rows of a list with a title, a description, what stands before them (an avatar, an icon, a checkbox, a radio) and at their end (a value, a note, a switch, keys, a chevron), pressed or not, a path in the router, a height for each density with the mobile row under a finger, lines between rows from an inset, and the contained list in a box with a header; `router::current_navigator` for a builder that goes somewhere.
- `ui::Navbar`: the way round a site or an app along the top of its window, its brand (`NavbarBrand`), its links (`NavItem`, of which one may open a panel of links) and what stands after them (`trailing`), sticky where told. Its links are a `NavigationMenu` in its links variant; on a window narrower than `md` they go behind a menu button (`menu_label`) into a `Sidebar` over the page. See [Navbar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#navbar).
- `ui::NavigationMenu`: a row of links, some of which open a panel of links (`NavPanel` of `NavLink`s in columns, with a featured element) under them, by a press, under the pointer after a delay and from the keyboard, the open panel handed to its neighbour by the pointer and by Left and Right. Each item is a stop of Tab. Choosing a link closes the panel and then runs it. The engine's part is `FocusGroup::panel_row`. See [Navigation Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#navigation-menu).
- `ui::Table` and `ui::TableColumn`: rows in columns that line up whatever is in a cell, from the engine's new shared column tracks (`ColumnTracks`, `.column_tracks`, `.measures_column_tracks`); sorting the app holds, selection with checkboxes, Ctrl and Shift, a header that stays while the rows scroll and a sticky first column (`ScrollBoth::fill_width`, and elements revealed clear of what sticks), rows built in view only in a mounted table of thousands, densities, empty and loading forms, a footer with a count and a pagination, the keys of a table of records, and Tab from a focused row into its buttons in a one-stop focus group.
- `ui::Calendar`: the days of a month to choose a date or a range from (two presses, the pointer showing where it would end), one or several months, the least and greatest dates and the app's days that cannot be chosen, week numbers, the locale's names and first day, the keys of a grid of days on the engine's new `KeyboardEvent::focus_key`, and `DateMemory` for what it keeps; on lurq's own dates (`core::Date`, whose line is in `CHANGELOG.md` already).
- `ui::DatePicker`: a field that holds a date, typed in the locale's short form, its numbers or ISO 8601 and read as it is typed, or chosen from a calendar in a popover from its button or Down; `format` and `parse` of the app's own, clearable, an input's sizes and judgments, and in a `Field` like an input.
- `ui::DateRangePicker`: a date picker over a range in one field, with presets beside the calendar and one or two months.
- `ui::TreeView` and `ui::TreeItem`: things inside things, a file tree, with folders and files, an open icon, what stands at a row's end, disabled rows, children an app brings when a row is first opened, guide lines, one selected or many with Ctrl and Shift, activation by a double click or Enter, bound by `TreeView::new(Signal<Option<T>>, items)` of one row or `TreeView::multiple(Signal<Vec<T>>, items)` of any number, or held by the app with `controlled(selected, items, on_change(&T))` and `controlled_multiple(selected, items, on_change(&[T]))` (`TreeView<T, OneSelected>`, `TreeView<T, ManySelected>`), its open rows by `open(signal)` or `open_ids(ids, toggle)`, and heard by `on_change`, `on_activate` and `on_open_change`, and the keys of a tree from the engine's new `FocusGroup::nested` with `focus_level` and `focus_expanded`, which a sidebar's nested items use too; typeahead in a long group no longer searches the group for each element. `TreeView::mount(ctx)` puts a tree of thousands of rows in the virtual list with every key reaching every row, and `TreeItem::trailing_on_hover` shows what is at a row's end only while the row is pointed at or focused.
- `FocusGroup::nested(true)`: a vertical focus group that is a tree, its elements at levels (`.focus_level(n)`) and those that open saying whether they are open and what opens them (`.focus_expanded(open, toggle)`). Right opens a closed element and goes into an open one, Left closes an open one and goes out of a closed one to the element it is in, `*` opens the closed elements beside the focused one; a flat list of rows and elements inside elements take the same calls. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#a-nested-list).
- `FocusRows`: a focus group of rows a list builds only in part, a virtual list of thousands, gives its rows to the group (`.focus_rows(rows)`), and each built row says which it is (`.focus_row(i)`). Up, Down, Home, End, PageUp and PageDown, the keys of a nested group and typeahead step over the rows, built or not; a row not built is asked for and has the focus once a layout has it, and keys pressed meanwhile go on from it. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#rows-a-list-builds-in-part).
- `FocusGroup::pages(true)`: in a vertical group PageUp and PageDown move the focus by a page, to the farthest element no more than the height of the scroll the group is in away and at least one on; a group in no scroll is one page. A listbox's options and the rows of a table that is not mounted have it. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#pages-and-ranges).
- `FocusGroup::shift_extends(true)`: a key that moves the focus with Shift held presses what it moves to with Shift, so that a list of which any number are selected extends its range with Shift and the arrows, Home, End and the page keys through its rows' own press, with no rows counted by the list. In a group that `activate`s, typeahead presses what a letter moves to as well. Both hold in a group of `FocusRows`: the row the keys move to is pressed once it is built and focused. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#pages-and-ranges).
- One model of selecting in a list for `ui::Listbox` and `ui::TreeView`: a press selects one row alone, Ctrl (Cmd) adds or takes out one, Shift with a press or a key selects from the last selected row to it and keeps that row last, Space adds or takes out the focused row, Ctrl+A selects every row that takes input, over rows that are not built too.
- Interaction groups: `.interaction_group(true)` on an element lights the `.within_style(..)` of the elements in it while the pointer is on it or the focus is on it or in it, as an action at the end of a row that shows only then. It paints only, and an element in two groups answers to the innermost. A design system's recipe names it `within` (`Recipe::within`, `Selector::within`, `PartStyles::within`). See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#within-an-interaction-group).
- `.within_hovered_style(..)` and `.within_pressed_style(..)`: a style by the hover or the press of the interaction group an element is in, and not by its focus, as a cell of a row that stays while the row scrolls sideways takes the row's veil. In a recipe, `within` with `hovered` or `pressed` (`Recipe::within_hovered`, `Recipe::within_pressed`, `PartStyles::within_hovered` and `within_pressed`). See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#within-an-interaction-group).
- Columns that rows share: `ColumnTracks`, a list of tracks in pixels, shares or as wide as their content, each with a least and a most, that a row lays its children out in (`.column_tracks(&tracks)`) and what holds the rows measures (`.measures_column_tracks(&tracks)`), so the cells of a table's header and rows line up whatever is in them, each row its own element. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#columns-that-rows-share).
- `ui::DataGrid`: a table whose cells are worked in. Its cells take the focus and the arrows move it by cell (Home, End, Ctrl+Home, Ctrl+End, PageUp, PageDown, Tab along the row); a cell is edited in place with its column's `CellEditor` (`text`, `number`, `choice`, `checkbox`: an `Input`, a `NumberInput`, a `Select`, a box in the cell), started by Enter, F2, a double press or a letter, committed by Enter (down) or Tab (on), put back by Escape; `on_edit` is told the row's key, the column's id and the `CellValue`, and may refuse it. `read_only(bool)`. It builds the rows and the columns in view: ten thousand rows of fifty columns. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::Table`: columns pinned at the start or the end (`TableColumn::pin(ColumnPin)`, which replaces `sticky(bool)`; `TableState::Sticky` is `Pinned`); columns made wider or narrower by a person (`Table::resizable`, `TableColumn::resizable`, `on_column_resize`, `on_column_resize_end`, `resize_label`), by a drag of their header's edge or Ctrl and an arrow; a mounted table builds the cells of the columns in view only (`in_tracks`); a header's title ends in an ellipsis where its column is too narrow.
- `.track_span(n)`: a child of a row of `ColumnTracks` takes `n` tracks, one room for the columns a table does not build where they are out of view. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#columns-that-rows-share).
- `.focus_column(column)`: the cells of a grid in a group of `FocusRows`, each saying its row and its column, keep their column as Up, Down, PageUp and PageDown go from row to row, built or not, and Ctrl+Home and Ctrl+End go to the first cell of the first row and the last of the last. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#rows-a-list-builds-in-part).
- `ui::DataGrid::on_demand(columns, total, fetch)`: rows the app gives on demand. The grid asks `fetch` for the ranges in view through the engine's futures (`GridRequest { query, range }` answered by a `GridPage { rows, total }` now or later), shows skeleton rows where a range has not come and a failed range with a retry, and leaves the sort, the filters and the search to the app as one `GridQuery` (`on_query_change`), which has a text form and is a storage value; `SourceRow<R>`, `source_labels(SourceLabels)`. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::DataGrid` search: `search(Signal<String>)`, `search_controlled`, `on_search_change`, `search_options(FindOptions)`: every match of the query in the cells' text is marked as a find marks it, the current one stronger; F3 and Shift+F3 go through them across rows not built yet, each brought into view; `find_bar(true)` opens `FindBar` over the grid with Ctrl+F (`find_bar_with` for its words); `on_matches_change(|matches: SearchMatches| ..)`. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::DataGrid` filters: a column says how it is filtered (`TableColumn::filter_text`, `filter_set`, `filter_values`, `filter_number`, `filter_date`, `filter_bool`), its menu opens a panel to filter by it ("Filter…", "Clear filter", "Clear all filters"), and `filter_row(true)` puts a row of filters under the header (ranges written `>10`, `<5`, `5..20`). The grid shows the rows every filter lets through; groups and summaries are of those, and the count reads "12 of 340 rows": `Table::count_text` and `DataGrid::count_text` take an `Fn(TableCount) -> String`, with `count.shown`, `count.total` and `count.selected`. `filters(Signal<TableFilters>)`, `filters_controlled`, `on_filters_change`, `filter_labels(FilterLabels)`; `TableFilters` and `ColumnFilter` with a text form and storage as a column layout's; `TablePart::FilterIcon`, `FilterRow`, `FilterLabel`. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::PieChart`, `ui::RadarChart` and `ui::Heatmap` move as the other charts do (`motion`): a pie's slices sweep in from the top and their ends move to a change, a slice hidden closing and the others taking its room; a radar's polygons grow from the center and their corners move to new values; a heatmap's cells fade in and go from their old step's color to the new.
- `ui::Heatmap`: a grid wider than its frame has smaller cells and gaps, and the plot is as high as they are drawn at its width, for any count of columns. The charts' ground: `ChartPlot::height_for(width)`, the height of a plot that follows its width, laid out again as the width changes.
- `ui::Table` and `ui::DataGrid` sorted by several columns: a press of a header with Shift adds its column to the sort after the others, turns it, then takes it out, and each sorted header shows its place in the sort (`TablePart::SortOrder`, `TableHeader::sort_rank`, `sort_order()`). The sort is a list, the first column first: `sort` takes a `Signal<Vec<TableSort>>`, `sort_controlled` a `Vec<TableSort>` and an `Fn(Vec<TableSort>)`, and `on_sort_change` an `Fn(&[TableSort])`. `TableSort::sort_rows(&sorts, &columns, &mut rows)` sorts rows by the list, `TableSort::after_press(current, column, adding)` gives the list after a press, `adding` while Shift is held, and `TableSort::list_to_text` and `parse_list` store it (`Vec<TableSort>` is a storage value). See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#table).
- `ui::DataGrid` and `ui::Table` at scale: a million rows of two hundred columns show in about 40 ms and render in a few, whatever the count. The rows' keys are reckoned as they are asked for, a flat table's lines hold nothing per row, the header's box and a range by Shift look the selection up through an index of the keys made once for the rows given (`new_shared`), grouped rows are laid out once, and before a table knows its width it builds the columns a wide window shows. `TableColumn::wrap(bool)`: a column whose text wraps, its row growing to hold it; a mounted table measures the rows it builds and keeps what is in view still as their heights become known. `BodyWindow`, what a table's renderer is given of where its rows are, holds what they measured: it is `Clone` and not `Copy`, and `room(from, to)` is the height of the lines from `from` up to `to`.
- `ui::Heatmap`, behind `canvas`: a grid of cells, a row for each series and a column for each category, each in its value's step on a scale between two roles (`Muted` to `Chart1`, five steps unless told, `steps`, `range`), the rows' names at the start and the columns' under the grid where told (`column_axis`), the scale under it from "Less" to "More" (`less_text`, `more_text`), a cell with no value (`missing_text`), `cell_size`; the cell under the pointer or the focus ringed with its tooltip, the arrows from cell to cell. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#heatmap).
- The charts' ground: the props' `height` is the plot's over the size's, where a chart is given one (the canvas took the plot part's height before).
- `ui::RadarChart`, behind `canvas`: a value for each of several axes round a center, the first at the top, a polygon for each series filled and edged in its color, the rings at round numbers (their numbers with `value_axis`), the axes' names round a square plot in the middle of the frame; the series and the axis under the pointer or the focus picked out with the tooltip of every series' value there, Left and Right round the axes and Up and Down between the series. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#radar-chart).
- The charts' ground: `ChartProps::plot_height(recipe)`, the plot's height for a chart that lays its plot out from it; a narrow plot's tooltip is centred on its point where it fits.
- `ui::PieChart`, behind `canvas`: the parts of a whole as slices from the top and clockwise, the legend beside the pie with each slice's share (`legend_value`, `share_text`), a donut with a hole of 0.66 (`donut`) that writes its total over the app's words (`total_text`), the slices' own colors (`colors`); the slice under the pointer or the focus picked out with its tooltip, the arrows round the slices, a slice hidden through the legend. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#pie-chart).
- The charts' ground: a plot of its own width (`ChartFrame::plot_width`, `plot_justify`), the legend beside the plot with a value for each item (`ChartFrame::legend_beside`, `ChartPart::Body`), a chart's own legend (`ChartFrame::own_legend`), words placed in the plot (`ChartLabel`, `ChartPlot::labels`), a tooltip's key in another color than its series' (`ChartPlot::tip_color`); `ChartLegendItem::value`, `ChartLegend::orientation` and `size` (`ChartLegendPart::Value`, `ChartLegendState::Vertical`).
- `ui::BarChart` and the charts' ground, behind `canvas`: a value for each category as a bar from zero, one series, several side by side or stacked (`stacked`), a current category (`current`), lying across (`orientation`), values under zero. Every chart's frame, words and behavior are shared: `ChartSeries`, `ChartPoint`, `ChartSize`, `ChartPart`, the title, the description, the legend and the tooltip, `value_text`, `grid`, `value_axis`, `hidden`, `empty_text`, `loading`, `on_select`, one stop of Tab with the arrows between points and series, a figure to tooling with its data as the canvas's items; `ChartLegend` and `ChartTooltip` stand on their own; `LinearScale` (round ticks) and `BandScale`; `ChartPlot`, `ChartFrame`, `chart_recipe` and `chart_props!` for a chart's author. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#charts).
- `ui::LineChart` and `ui::AreaChart`, behind `canvas`: a value for each category joined by a line from the plot's start to its end, of several series, straight or smooth (`curve(LineCurve)`), with gaps where a value is missing; at the place under the pointer or the focus a cursor, a mark on each line and a tooltip of every series beside it; ten thousand values drawn as one path, the pointer finding its place at once; the texts under the plot of its own (`axis_labels`). An area chart has the fill under each line at 0.16, overlaid or `stacked`. `ChartPlot::tip_place` (`TipPlace::Over`, `Beside`) says where a plot's tooltip stands. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#line-chart).
- `ui::ScatterChart`, behind `canvas`: points by an x and a y on two value axes, several series, the point nearest the pointer in two dimensions, the numbers across under the plot (`x_text`), and the line of a series' trend (`trend`). See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#scatter-chart).
- `Tree::set_software_canvases(bool)`: every canvas of the tree is drawn on the CPU, so that a headless test reads its pixels with `CanvasHandle::snapshot`. See [testing](docs/src/content/docs/testing.md#a-canvass-pixels).
- The charts move: a plot moves in as it is first shown and to a change of its data, bars growing from their baseline and moving to their new place, for the theme's slow duration, drawn again at each frame only while it moves; nothing moves where motion is reduced or while a chart is resized; `motion(false)` keeps one still. `ChartPlot::paint_moving` and `ChartMotion` for a chart's author, `chart_motion`. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#charts).
- The line, area and scatter charts move: a line is drawn on as it is first shown and its values move to a change of the data, the areas with them (stacked too), a value added or removed at the end coming out of or going into the last one; a scatter's points grow in and move from where they were, the trend following them. A series hidden sinks to zero (or onto the one under it in a stack) and one shown rises from it. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#line-chart).
- `Canvas::paint_moving(key, motion, of, value, |canvas, at| ..)`: a drawing that moves in and to what it is given next, `at` a `CanvasMove` (`from`, `to`, `progress`), drawn at each frame only while it moves, still where motion is reduced and while it is resized; `CanvasMotion`; `Tree::has_moving_canvas`. See [canvas](docs/src/content/docs/canvas.md).
- `Canvas::paint(key, |canvas| ..)`: a canvas drawn when it is first laid out, when its size or scale changes, and when its key changes, and at no other render; `ElementHandle::canvas()`. See [canvas](docs/src/content/docs/canvas.md).
- `ui::DataGrid`: what a person arranged is stored between runs, as a dock's state is. `ColumnLayout::to_text`, `parse`, `Display` and `FromStr`, and `ColumnLayout::valid(ids)`, which leaves out a column the app no longer has and puts a new one at its place (the grid does it as it shows an arrangement); `TableSort::to_text` and `parse` (`"name" descending`); `GroupKey::to_text`, `parse`, `list_to_text` and `parse_list` (`group("Passing" "Ada")`); `TableTextError`. With `persistent_storage`, `ColumnLayout`, `TableSort`, `Option<TableSort>` and `Vec<GroupKey>` are values of the storage, stored as their text. A column new to a stored arrangement now takes its place after the column before it, where it was put after the others.
- `ui::DataGrid`: every word it writes is said otherwise: `column_menu_labels(ColumnMenuLabels { .. })`, `menu_trigger_label`, `group_label`, `summary_labels(SummaryLabels { .. })`, `row_numbers_label`.
- `ui::DataGrid`: row numbers. `row_numbers(true)`: a column of the rows' numbers at the start that stays while the columns scroll, pressed to select a row (with Ctrl and Shift as a row's press), its header to select every row. New `TablePart::RowNumber`; `TableRow::number`, `TableView::numbered` and `number_header` for a renderer.
- `ui::DataGrid`: summary rows. `TableColumn::summary(SummaryKind::Sum)` (`Average`, `Count`, `Min`, `Max`), which the grid computes from a column of numbers, or `TableColumn::summary_text(|rows| ..)`, the app's: a row at the bottom of the grid that stays there, and one at the end of each group open. New `TablePart::Summary`, `SummaryLabel`, `SummaryValue`; `TableView::summary`, `TableView::summaries` and `TableSummary` for a renderer.
- `ui::DataGrid`: group rows. `group_by(["status", ..])` groups the rows by the values of columns, nested where it is more than one: a group's row with a chevron, its value and its count, opened and closed by a press, Enter, Space, Right and Left (`open(signal)`, `open_controlled`, `on_open_change`, a `GroupKey` of the values), the rows of a closed group not built. New `TablePart::GroupRow`, `Chevron`, `GroupLabel`, `GroupCount`; `TableView::groups` and `TableGroup` for a renderer, `TableRow::position` and `GridCell::position` for a row's line among the lines shown. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::DataGrid`: a column's menu and an arrangement of the columns. Each header has a menu (`column_menu(bool)`, on unless told), opened by its button shown under the pointer and with the focus or by Alt+Down: sort ascending, descending or none, pin at the start or the end or unpin, size to fit, hide, and show the columns hidden. A header dragged to another place moves its column, as Ctrl+Shift+PageUp and PageDown do. Which columns are shown, their order, widths and pins are a value of the grid (`column_layout(signal)`, `column_layout_controlled`, `on_column_layout_change`, a `ColumnLayout` of `ColumnPlace`s) the app holds and stores; a resize is kept in it. New `TablePart::MenuTrigger` and `DropLine`, `TableState::Open`, `DropBefore` and `DropAfter`. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::DataGrid`: a range of cells. A drag from a cell, Shift with the arrows (Home, End, PageUp, PageDown, with Ctrl the corners) or with a press, and Ctrl+A select it; it is drawn as one area in `Selection` edged in `Ring`, the focused cell inside it, and kept apart from the rows' selection (a press of a cell no longer selects its row, and Ctrl+A selects every cell, where it selected every row). `cell_selection(signal)`, `cell_selection_controlled`, `on_cell_selection_change` tell it as a `CellRange` of the rows' keys and the columns' ids. Ctrl+C copies it as tab-separated text, Ctrl+V pastes into it cell by cell through `on_edit`, Delete clears what is edited (`CellValue::Empty` for a number or a choice), Ctrl+X does both. New `TableState::InRange`, `RangeTop`, `RangeBottom`, `RangeStart`, `RangeEnd`. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `on_copy` and `CopyEvent`: a copy or a cut (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X) on a focused element that is not a text input, whose handler says the text the engine writes to the clipboard; a paste on such an element reaches its `on_paste` handlers. `Tree::set_clipboard_writer` and `ClipboardWriter`: where copies go in a test. See [events](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#copy).
- `.scrollbars_clear_of_sticky(true)` keeps the bars clear of what sticks at the end too: the bar down stands before a column that stays at the right, and the bar across ends where it starts (and above what sticks at the bottom). `.reserve_bar_across(true)` on `ScrollHorizontal` and `ScrollBoth`: the bar across has a lane of its own under the content while the content is wider than the viewport, and none while it fits. `ui::Table` asks for both, so its bars lie over neither a column pinned at the end nor its last row. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#sticky-elements).
- `.scrollbars_clear_of_sticky(true)` on `ScrollVertical`, `ScrollHorizontal` and `ScrollBoth`: the bars run beside what scrolls, the one down under what sticks at the top and the one across after what sticks at the start, so a table's bar does not lie over its header; painting, the pointer and a drag of the thumb go by that track. Off unless set. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#sticky-elements).
- `ScrollBoth::fill_width(true)`: content that scrolls both ways is at least as wide as the viewport, and scrolls sideways only where it needs more. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#scroll).
- Tab into what a row holds: in a focus group that is one stop, the stops inside its stop (`.stops_inside(true)`) are kept with it, so Tab goes from a focused row into its buttons and on out of the group. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#focus-groups).
- An element scrolled into view, by the focus or by a request, is shown clear of what sticks at that edge of its scroll container: a row the keys reach is not left behind a table's header. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#scrolling-focus-into-view).
- `VirtualizedList::reveal_request(n)`: the row of `reveal_key` is revealed again each time `n` changes, whether the key changed or not, for a list asked twice for one row it was scrolled away from between. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#scroll).
- `ui::Sidebar`: the way round an app down one side of its window, with groups, nested items that open with their height, counts, badges and shortcuts, the selected item, a header and a footer (`SidebarHeading`), the rail of icons with tooltips and menus whose width moves, the sidebar over the page on a narrow window, and `SidebarTrigger`, whose names can be said otherwise; `mount(ctx)` keeps which lists are open, `build(&theme)` draws it at rest; the gallery's own sidebar is one now. Where the app selects another item (a router, a search) it is scrolled into view and the lists it is in are opened, and a closed item or group that holds the selected item shows it.
- `ui::Drawer::scroll(false)`: what a drawer holds is laid out at the panel's height and does not scroll, beside the page and over it, for what has a scroll of its own with something that stays beside it, as the sidebar's header and footer stay while its items scroll.
- `ui::Sheet::body_scrolls(false)`: a sheet's body laid out in the room the panel has for it, between the header and the footer, and not scrolled; a drawer over the page with `scroll(false)` gives it to its sheet.
- `ui::Menubar`: the menus of an app in a row, File, Edit, View, of the `MenuItem`s a `Menu` has. A press of a title opens its menu, and with one open the pointer on another title opens that one; the bar is one stop of Tab, Left and Right move between its titles and hand an open menu to the one beside it, Down, Enter and Space open a menu at its first item and Up at its last, Alt alone puts the focus on the bar with the titles' first letters underlined, and Alt with a letter opens that title's menu. Which menu is open is the app's `Signal<Option<usize>>`. The engine's part is `FocusGroup::menu_row`, which a bar of buttons and menus of an app's own can have too. See [Menubar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menubar).
- `ui::Sidebar`: the way round an app down one side of its window, with groups, nested items that open with their height, counts, badges and shortcuts, the selected item, a header and a footer (`SidebarHeading`), the rail of icons with tooltips and menus whose width moves, the sidebar over the page on a narrow window, and `SidebarTrigger`; the gallery's own sidebar is one now. Where the app selects another item (a router, a search) it is scrolled into view and the lists it is in are opened, and a closed item or group that holds the selected item shows it.
- `ui::Drawer::body_scrolls(false)`: what a drawer holds is laid out at the panel's height and does not scroll, beside the page and over it, for what has a scroll of its own with something that stays beside it, as the sidebar's header and footer stay while its items scroll.
- `ui::Sheet::body_scrolls(false)`: a sheet's body laid out in the room the panel has for it, between the header and the footer, and not scrolled; a drawer over the page with `body_scrolls(false)` gives it to its sheet.
- `ui::Menubar`: the menus of an app in a row, File, Edit, View, of the `MenuItem`s a `Menu` has. Each menu is a `MenubarMenu::new(title, entries)`, which may be `disabled`, and the bar has a `label`. A press of a title opens its menu, and with one open the pointer on another title opens that one; the bar is one stop of Tab, Left and Right move between its titles and hand an open menu to the one beside it, Down, Enter and Space open a menu at its first item and Up at its last, Alt alone puts the focus on the bar with the titles' first letters underlined, and Alt with a letter opens that title's menu. Which menu is open is the app's `Signal<Option<usize>>`. The engine's part is `FocusGroup::menu_row`, which a bar of buttons and menus of an app's own can have too. See [Menubar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menubar).
- `FocusGroup::menu_row(bool)`: a row of menus, each element of the group opening a menu hung from it. With one of those menus open, Left and Right that nothing in the menu takes close it and open the menu beside it; Alt with a letter opens the menu of the element whose label starts with it, from wherever the focus is; Alt pressed and let go alone puts the focus on the row and, again, gives it back. On a Mac Alt does neither. For a menubar of an app's own buttons and menus. See [a row of menus](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#a-row-of-menus).
- `FocusGroup::panel_row(bool)`: a row of panels, a row of menus whose elements are links as well as buttons that open a panel, as a site's navigation menu. Left and Right that nothing in an open panel takes close it and focus the element beside it without pressing it, opening its panel where it says with `focus_expanded(open, toggle)` how it opens; a link is only focused. Alt does not reach it. See [a row of panels](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#a-row-of-panels).
- `impl From<MenuItem> for MenuEntry`: a menu item is an entry of a menu with `.into()`, for a list that mixes items with `MenuEntry::Separator` and `MenuEntry::Heading`, as a menubar's `MenubarMenu::new(title, entries)` takes.
- `ui::Toolbar`: a row of tools that is one stop of Tab, with groups, lines, a spacer, tooltips of its icons and their shortcuts, and with `overflow(true)` a menu at its end of the controls that do not fit, the last first; the engine's `drop_marker` lays out a line's More button only while something is dropped.
- `ui::ActionBar`: the toolbar of a selection, with its count, its actions and its clear button, coming up over the list it acts on. Mounted with `mount(ctx)`, a bar given `return_focus(element)` gives the focus to that element, the list it acts on, as it leaves with the focus in it: so that clearing the selection from the keyboard does not leave the focus nowhere.
- `.drop_marker(bool)` for a child of a single-line `Row` or `Column`: it takes its room only while another child of the line is dropped (`ShrinkLimit::Drop`, `shrink_drop_below`), and is dropped itself while none is; the line gives way without it first, and with its room taken only when that drops a child. For the More button at the end of a row whose children drop where it is too narrow, which opens a menu of what went: the dropped children say so through their element refs, at no size. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#what-did-not-fit-the-drop-marker).
- A `ui::Button` in a `ui::Toolbar` that does not fit goes into the bar's menu as an item of its label and icon, disabled where it is, which presses it as Enter would: so that a narrow bar loses none of its buttons.
- A `ui::Toggle` in a `ui::Toolbar` that does not fit goes into the bar's menu as an item checked while it is on, which turns it: so that a narrow bar loses none of its marks.
- A `ui::ToggleGroup` in a `ui::Toolbar` is among the bar's controls, its toggles reached by the bar's arrow keys, and goes into the bar's menu whole where it does not fit, a checked item for each toggle: so that a group of choices behaves as the bar's other controls do.
- Markdown draws every block of the design: ordered lists with their numbers at the end of a column, nested bullets (a dot, then a ring), tasks on the checkbox's look, read only unless the app takes their change (`MarkdownProps::on_task_change(|index, checked| ..)`), tables with a striped body and a line between columns, the rule, an image with its caption (or the box of its place), GitHub's admonitions as callouts with a bar in the tone of their kind, `<details>` that open and close by their summary, footnotes at the end with the way back to where the text refers to each, a reference going to its footnote, and code and keys in a sentence on the boxes of `ui::InlineCode` and `ui::Kbd`. See [Markdown](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#markdown).
- `MarkdownProps::build(theme)` and `From<MarkdownProps> for Element`: a document once, with nothing kept. `ThemeMarkdown::resolve(&palette)`: the theme with every role turned into the palette's color.
- `BackgroundColor: From<TextColor>`: a text's color as a fill, a role staying a role.
- `TextStyle::baseline_shift`: a span of a rich text drawn above (or below) the line's baseline, its line, wrapping, carets and selection as they are. `MarkdownTextStyle::baseline_shift`, in ems of the text it is in; a Markdown footnote's number is raised, its top at the line's top, as the design draws it. See [Baseline Shift](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#baseline-shift).
- `ui::Highlight`: a text with marks on ranges of it, the matches of a search, one of them the current one, as the design's Highlight draws them, a mark's words at 500 (the `Mark` part's weight); `keep_weight(true)` keeps the text's, for a find in a text that must not wrap again as the query is typed. Mounted, it brings its current mark into view as it changes. `ui::find_matches(text, query, FindOptions)` finds the ranges of a query under a find bar's options (the case, whole words, a regular expression with the new `regex` feature), for an app that searches a plain text.
- A paragraph of runs of their own style (the engine's rich text) is laid out, drawn, selected and highlighted without the `markdown` feature: the words of a `ui::Highlight` mark in another weight are such runs. Links in a paragraph still come with `markdown`. A run whose style keeps the default text color takes the foreground of an element above it, as a text's style does.
- `TextHighlight::padding(x, y)`: a fill that reaches past its range's glyphs, as a mark around its words, with the text where it was; a negative one draws inside them, a box lower than its line (code in a paragraph of tall lines). `TextHighlight::border(width, color)`: a line around each of its fills, inside them, as the current match of a search.
- `Ctx::scroll_range_into_view(&text, range)`: the lines a range of a text covers are brought into view, as `scroll_into_view` brings an element: the current match of a search in a long text. See [scroll](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#scroll).
- `ui::CommandBar`, `ui::CommandBarTab` and `ui::CommandBarCommand`: the tabs of a window's commands over the commands of the selected one in labelled groups, a ribbon, built on `ui::Tabs` and `ui::Toolbar`: large commands with the icon over the label, stacks of two small ones, toggled commands, what does not fit behind a chevron a group whole, and a tab's secondary commands only there. `CommandBar<T = ()>` is generic over the tabs' values, bound (`new`) or held by the app (`controlled`).
- `ui::FindBar`: the search of what a view shows, hung from the top of the view over its content: its field with the options of the search in it (`match_case`, `whole_word`, `regex`, each a value of its own), the count the app gives it (`matches`, `count_text`), the next and the previous match by Enter, Shift+Enter, F3 and Shift+F3, the words of its options (`match_case_text`, `whole_word_text`, `regex_text`), the replace row (`replace`, `replace_open`, `on_replace`, `on_replace_all`), and Escape and its close button that dismiss it. Mounted, it puts the focus in its field as it opens and gives it to `return_focus` as it closes. It is a `search` to tooling.
- `ui::ToolbarItem::stack(items)`: controls one over another in one place of a toolbar, which go into its menu whole. `ui::Toolbar::more_icon(name)`: the icon of its More button. `ui::Toolbar::secondary_command(command)` and `secondary_commands(..)`: commands only in the menu of More, which is then always there. `ToolbarPart::MoreButton`: what a recipe says of the More button alone, as a command bar makes its chevron 28 by 52.
- `ui::Splitter` and `ui::SplitterPane`: panes that share a room by dividers a person drags, holding the pointer with its cursor for the whole drag, with pixel and share panes, least and most sizes, panes that close, the keys, a double press back, the app's saved sizes and the board's Resizable grip; the engine's `on_drag_*` sessions keep their cursor until let go, and `ElementRefMut::set_size` resizes an element without a render.
- `ui::DocumentTabs` and `ui::DocumentTab`: the open documents of an editor in a bar across its top, bound to a signal (`new`, `optional`) or held by the app (`controlled`, `controlled_optional`), `on_change` told the tab shown; a tab's icon, a document not saved (`dirty`), a pinned tab and a preview in italics; closed by its control, a middle press or Ctrl+W (`on_close`, on the bar and on a tab), moved by a drag along the bar or Ctrl+Shift+PageUp and PageDown (`on_reorder(from, to)`), `on_add` for the new tab button, `on_keep` for a double press of a preview, a context menu the app fills (`context_menu`), one stop of Tab, and, mounted, the tabs that do not fit in a menu behind a count at the end; the bar follows the density. See [Document Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#document-tabs).
- `DocumentTabs::shortcuts(bool)`, on unless told: the editor's keys from anywhere in the window, as `WindowTabs` has them: Ctrl+Tab and Ctrl+Shift+Tab, Ctrl+PageUp and PageDown, Ctrl+W (not of a pinned tab, which no key closes now) and the keys that move a tab. Where a window has both with their keys on, a press is the documents': `WindowTabs` passes by a key something took before it (`prevent_default`).
- `ui::StatusBar` and `ui::StatusItem`: the bar along the bottom of a window, its items at its start and its end (`leading`, `trailing`), each with an icon, a tone (`variant`, also of the whole bar), a tooltip, a rail of progress, and `on_select` for one that is pressed; the pressed items are one stop of Tab, and a bar too narrow drops its items by their `priority` from the end `drops_first` says. It is a status to tooling. See [Status Bar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#status-bar).
- In a focus group that is one stop, the stops in a `FocusGroup::none()` inside it are stops of their own for Tab, as they already were out of the group for its arrows: Tab skipped them before.
- `ui::ScrollArea`: content that scrolls in a room of its own with the design's bar, down, across or both ways (`ScrollAreaOrientation`), its bars shown while the content overflows, always, under the pointer, only while it scrolls or never (`ScrollAreaScrollbars`), over the content's edge or in a lane of their own (`gutter`), with the app's `ScrollState`, and with a `label` a region that Tab reaches and the keys scroll: for a dialog's long body, a list in a panel, a table's cells.
- A scroll container that has the focus itself scrolls with the keys, as a browser's focused region: Up and Down, Left and Right, PageUp, PageDown, Space, Home and End; a control in it and a multiline text input keep theirs. So that content with nothing that takes the focus can be read from the keyboard.
- To what reads the tree, each bar of a scroll container whose content overflows is a scrollbar with its value: `scrollbar_vertical` and `scrollbar_horizontal`, from 0 at the start to 100 at the end, in `lurq_read_tree` and `lurq_inspect`. So that an agent knows what can be scrolled and how far it is.
- A drag holds the pointer: an `on_drag_*` session gets every move until the button is let go, over other elements and out of the window, and the window shows the cursor that was under the pointer at the press for the whole drag (`Tree::cursor`), where it fell back to the default cursor once the pointer left the window. For a divider or a handle that is dragged past what it resizes. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#a-drag-holds-the-pointer).
- `ElementRefMut::set_size(width, height)` and `size()`: an element laid out at another width or height than it was built with, in pixels or a share of its parent, with nothing rendered again; the next pass lays it and what holds it out. For what a person resizes continuously, a pane under a dragged divider, without rebuilding what is in it. See [ctx](docs/src/content/docs/ctx.md#sizing-an-element-without-a-render).
- `.inert(bool)` on every element, and `ElementRefMut::set_inert(bool)` with no render: an element and all it holds out of the focus and the pointer while it is still painted. Tab, a focus group's arrows and typeahead pass it by, a focus request for it is refused, the pointer reaches what is under it, and the focus in it leaves as it leaves an element taken out of the tree. For a closed pane, a panel on its way out. See [focus and keyboard navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#inert-elements).
- `ElementRefMut::describe(name, value)`: a value said to tooling in place of the element's `.describe(name, ..)`, with no render; `lurq_inspect`, `lurq_read_tree`, `lurq_find`, DevTools and the new `ElementHandle::description(name)` read it at once. For a value that changes as a person drags, where a divider stands. See [MCP](docs/src/content/docs/mcp.md#making-your-app-agent-friendly).
- `ui::CommandPalette` and `ui::CommandItem`: a search over the window that finds a command or a place by typing, near the top over a veil, with groups, icons, details, shortcuts and keywords, a filter of every word (or the app's), the focus kept in its input while Down and Up move a highlight round the rows, Enter running and Escape clearing then closing, an empty and a loading list, a footer of its keys, and commands an app fetches; the gallery's search is one.
- `ui::ContextMenu`: a menu opened at the pointer by a secondary press, at the finger by a long press, or at its element by the Menu key and Shift+F10, kept inside the window, one open at a time, with a bar for the edit menu of selected text; the engine's `Overlay::at` and `Popup::at` stand a popup at a point of the window.
- `ui::Alert`, a message of the design system in the flow of a page: a title, a description, an icon by its variant (`AlertVariant`: neutral, info, success, warning, destructive, through `variant(impl Into<Name>)`) or one of its own or none (`icon(impl Into<IconChoice>)`), an action, a close control once `on_close` is given, a `label`, and elements of the app's under its text; an `alert` to tooling for a warning and a destructive one, a `status` for the others.
- `ui::CommandPalette` and `ui::CommandItem`: a search over the window that finds a command or a place by typing, near the top over a veil, with groups, icons, details, shortcuts and keywords, a filter of every word (or the app's), the focus kept in its input while Down and Up move a highlight round the rows, Enter running and Escape clearing then closing, an empty and a loading list whose words can be said otherwise (`empty_text`, `loading_text`), a row of its keys (`key_hints`), commands an app fetches (`on_query`), a `label`, and `on_dismiss`, `dismiss_on_escape` and `dismiss_on_veil`; a command closes the palette before its handler is called. The gallery's search is one.
- `ui::ContextMenu`: a menu opened at the pointer by a secondary press, at the finger by a long press, or at its element by the Menu key and Shift+F10, kept inside the window, one open at a time, with a bar for the edit menu of selected text (`ContextMenu::new(open, target)`, with a menu's `heading`, `label`, `inline`, `on_dismiss`, `dismiss_on_escape`, `dismiss_on_outside_press`, `offset` and `collision`); the engine's `Overlay::at` and `Popup::at` stand a popup at a point of the window.
- `ui::Alert`, a message of the design system in the flow of a page: a title, a description, an icon by its variant (`AlertVariant`: neutral, info, success, warning, destructive) or one of its own, an action, a close control and elements of the app's under its text; an `alert` to tooling for a warning and a destructive one, a `status` for the others.
- `ui::Banner`, a message across the full width of what holds it at the top of a window or a page: its icon, its text on one line where it fits, a link in its variant's color or another action, a close control, its content centred or from its start, and `open` and `sticky`; it fades in as it is shown and out as it is taken away.
- `ui::Callout`, a note set apart inside prose or a form: the icon of its variant, a title, its text and elements of the app's, and with `bar(true)` a document's note with a bar along its start.
- `ui::Kbd`: a key, the keys of a shortcut (`Kbd::shortcut("Mod+K")`), steps pressed one after another (`Kbd::sequence`) or an accelerator of the app's menu, in words or with a Mac's glyphs in a Mac's order (`KeyPlatform`), in a box each or as the plain text a menu writes, in three sizes; and `ui::InlineCode`, a short text of code on a muted fill. The gallery's `kit::keys` is gone for it.
- `ui::EmptyState`: what stands where content would be when there is none, with an icon in a well or the app's element, a title, a description and one or two actions, in three sizes (a table's, the default in an edge, a phone's), centred in the room it is given.
- `ui::Outcome`, the design's Result: what a page or a dialog says when something is over, in five variants (success, error, warning, info, not found) with their marks and colors, the app's icon or element, what the app puts under the text, and its buttons, in a box or on a page.
- `ui::MessageBox`: a dialog that asks one question or says one thing, with an icon by variant (info, question, warning, destructive), a title, a description, a detail behind "Details", a "Don't ask again" checkbox, and answers by role (default, cancel, destructive, plain) whose buttons the message box's recipe styles apart from buttons elsewhere; Enter gives the default answer, Escape and the veil the cancel one, and the focus goes to the confirming answer or, where that answer is destructive, to cancel. Every element has `.autofocus(bool)`, where the focus goes first as the modal or the overlay it is in opens; `ui::Dialog` has `on_dismiss`, `alert`, `label` and `described`.
- `ui::Popconfirm`: a question in a small panel hung from what was pressed, over `ui::Popover`, with a confirm and a cancel button, the focus on confirm as it opens and on cancel where it is destructive, Escape and a press outside that cancel and then run `on_dismiss`, a destructive look, an icon that can be another or none, `label`, `width`, `offset(x, y)`, `collision`, `dismiss_on_escape`, `dismiss_on_outside_press` and `inline`; `Popconfirm::new(open, anchor)` takes the signal first. `ui::Popover` has `alert`, `label`, `described` and `on_dismiss` (each handler given runs), and `Overlay` and `Popup` have `on_dismiss`, what runs after a press outside or Escape closed them.
- `ui::Outcome`, the design's Result: what a page or a dialog says when something is over, in five variants (info, success, warning, destructive, not found) with their marks and colors, the app's icon, none, or an element, what the app puts under the text, and its buttons, in a box or on a page.
- `ui::MessageBox`: a dialog that asks one question or says one thing, with an icon by kind (information, question, warning, error), a title, a message, a detail behind "Details", a "Don't ask again" checkbox, and answers by role (default, cancel, destructive, plain) whose buttons the message box's recipe styles apart from buttons elsewhere; Enter gives the default answer, Escape and the veil the cancel one, and the focus goes to the default answer or, for a destructive question, to cancel. Every element has `.autofocus(bool)`, where the focus goes first as the modal or the overlay it is in opens; `ui::Dialog` has `on_dismiss`, `alert`, `named` and `described`.
- `ui::Popconfirm`: a question in a small panel hung from what was pressed, over `ui::Popover`, with a confirm and a cancel button, the focus on cancel as it opens, Escape and a press outside that cancel, and a destructive look; `ui::Popover` has `alert`, `named`, `described` and `on_dismiss`, and `Overlay` and `Popup` have `on_dismiss`, what runs after a press outside or Escape closed them.
- `ui::Popconfirm`: a question in a small panel hung from what was pressed, over `ui::Popover`, with a confirm and a cancel button, the focus on confirm as it opens and on cancel where it is destructive, Escape and a press outside that cancel and then run `on_dismiss`, a destructive look, an icon that can be another or none, `label`, `width`, `offset`, `collision`, `dismiss_on_escape`, `dismiss_on_outside_press` and `inline`; `Popconfirm::new(open, anchor)` takes the signal first. `ui::Popover` has `alert`, `label`, `described` and `on_dismiss` (each handler given runs), and `Overlay` and `Popup` have `on_dismiss`, what runs after a press outside or Escape closed them.
- `ui::ProgressDialog`: a dialog that nothing but the app closes while work runs, with what is being done, a `ui::Progress` with a value or without one, how much is done and what is left, a detail behind "Details", buttons of the app's, and a cancel button that Escape presses.
- `ui::Sheet`: a panel that slides in from an edge of the window (`SheetSide::Start`, `End`, `Top`, `Bottom`) over a veil, in the board's sizes, with a header of a title, a description and a close button, a body that scrolls between it and a footer of buttons, the board's grabber and stacked buttons at the bottom, the focus going in and back and Tab kept inside; mounted at the bottom it is the engine's `Sheet` a finger drags between detents, which now takes `SheetProps::dress`, `dismiss_on_escape` and `takes_focus` and stands under a window's own title bar. An offset keyframe in percent moves an element by its own size, and `PartStyle` has `end_line` and `top_line`.
- `ui::Drawer`: a panel at one side of a layout that opens and closes with its width (or its height at the top or the bottom), pushing the page aside, or lies over the page as a `ui::Sheet` with `overlay(true)`.
- `ui::HoverCard`: more about what the pointer rests on in a panel beside it, with media, a title, a description, a row of facts and anything it holds, which opens after the theme's slower duration or at once for the keyboard's focus, stays while the pointer crosses to it, closes a moment after it left both and on Escape, lets Tab go from the element into its controls and on (`Overlay::tab_after_anchor`, new: an overlay that Tab goes through after its anchor without taking the focus), and is a dialog or a tooltip to tooling by whether it has controls.
- `ui::Toggletip`: a small button with an "i" that opens a few words in the primary color, with a link or a button in them, closed by another press, a press outside or Escape (`dismiss_on_outside_press`, `dismiss_on_escape`), each of which tells `on_dismiss`, the focus staying on the button and Tab going into the panel; to tooling the button says it is expanded and the panel is a status.
- `ui::TeachingTip` and `ui::Tour`: a panel that points at one thing in the app with an arrow that has its edge, media, a title, a description, a close control, a step count and two buttons, in a plain and a primary look, which does not take the focus unless the app says so and is closed by its close control or Escape and not by a press elsewhere unless `dismiss_on_outside_press` says so, and then tells `on_dismiss`; a tour shows the app's steps one after another with Back, Next and Done.
- `WindowHandle::title_bar_inset()` and `set_title_bar_inset(..)`: how much of the window's top the app's own title bar is drawn over. A `WindowChrome` sets it to the height of its title bar, and an app that draws a title bar another way sets it itself.
- `FocusGroup::none()` (`FocusAxis::None`): a group of no keys, which takes what is in it out of the focus group around it. That group's arrow keys pass it by and do nothing while the focus is in it: the content of an accordion's open item, between two headers.
- `KeyboardEvent::focus_key(key)`: a key's handler moves the focus to the stop of its focus group with that `.key(..)`, once the handlers have run and what they changed is rendered, from the keyboard and into view, so that a group's keys can go by what the app knows of its elements and not by their order: a calendar's day a week on, in the month the key shows next. See [focus groups](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#focus-groups).
- `core::Date`, `YearMonth`, `DateRange` and `Weekday`: lurq's own dates, with no time, no zone and no dependency, with their arithmetic (days, months and years on, the weeks of a month, ISO weeks), today by the system's zone, ISO 8601 written and read, and days from 1970 to convert another library's dates through; and `core::DateLocale`, how a locale writes and reads them (the first day of its weeks, the names of its months and days, its short form) for English, Ukrainian, German, French, Spanish and Polish, or one of the app's own. See [dates](docs/src/content/docs/dates.md).
- `core::DateText`: everything of a date's text that a calendar and a date picker ask (the first day of the week, the names of months and weekdays, what a day is called to tooling, how a date and a range are written and read back, and the names of the controls), a trait an app implements over its own tables, `chrono`, ICU or a catalog of translations. `DateLocale` is lurq's, and has `with_style(DateStyle::{Numeric, Short, Long, Iso})`, `with_separator`, `with_format` and `with_parse` for another way of writing dates, and `labels` (`DateLabels`) naming the controls in its seven languages.
- `App::set_date_locale(..)` and `App::date_locale()`: how every calendar and date picker of the app writes its dates, English until it is set, a `DateLocale` or the app's own `DateText`; a mounted component, or one built in a render, is built again when it changes. `ui::current_date_locale()` reads it as `current_theme()` reads the theme. `Calendar::locale`, `DatePicker::locale` and `DateRangePicker::locale` take any `DateText` and are above it. See [dates](docs/src/content/docs/dates.md#how-an-app-writes-its-dates).
- `BackgroundColor::CurrentForeground`: a fill that is the foreground the element inherits, the color a text there with no color of its own is drawn in.
- `.offset_by_size(x, y)`: moves an element by shares of its own width and height once it is laid out, on top of `.offset`. `(-0.5, -0.5)` puts its middle where its top left corner was, whatever its size.
- `.repeat_press(bool)`: a press of the primary button on the element runs its `on_click` at once and again while the button is held and the pointer is on it, first after 400 ms and then every 50 ms: a button that steps a value. `.on_press_end(handler)` is told as the held press is let go, wherever the pointer is: a held stepper's change is over at its release.
- `.autofocus(bool)`: the element the focus goes to first, in place of the first stop of Tab, when a modal or an overlay that takes the focus opens, or Tab is pressed in a window with no focus. A message box's default answer.
- `Overlay::on_dismiss(handler)` and `Popup::on_dismiss`: what runs after a press outside the overlay or Escape closed it. `ui::Popover::on_dismiss` and `ui::Dialog::on_dismiss` hand it on.
- `Overlay::tab_after_anchor(bool)` and `Popup::tab_after_anchor`: Tab goes through the overlay's elements right after its anchor's while the focus stays where it is as the overlay opens. A hover card's links.
- `Overlay::at(point)` and `Popup::at`: a point of the window the overlay stands at in place of its anchor, kept inside the window. A context menu at the pointer.
- The engine's `Sheet` takes its look from a component: `SheetProps::dress(..)` with `SheetNode` (`Backdrop`, `Panel`, `Grabber`), `dismiss_on_escape(bool)` and `takes_focus(bool)`; its room is the window under its own title bar.
- `icons::HELP_CIRCLE`, in the icon font. `ui::Popover::alert`, `label` and `described`; `ui::Link::rules`.
- `TextInput::keep_text_while_focused(bool)`: a focused input keeps the text it shows when a render binds it to another signal, for a control that makes its text anew from a value in each build.
- The engine's `Select` has a combobox mode: `Select::combobox(TextInput)` puts an input in its trigger that keeps the focus, opens the list as it is typed in and moves the highlight with the arrows; `Select::free_text(bool)`, `Select::status(element)` (a row after the options that cannot be chosen), and `SelectOption::value()`, `label()`, `starts_group()`, `is_disabled()` for what filters the options.
- A token in a text input takes its look from a component: `InputTokenStyle` has `padding_end`, `height`, `border`, `text_style`, `icon(InputTokenIcon)`, `hovered(..)` and `selected(..)`; an icon that `removes` takes the token away on a click, as one step of the undo; `InputToken::atomic(true)` makes a token one thing to the caret, which never rests inside it. `TextInputController::caret_point(byte)` says where the caret before a byte stands, and an element ref can follow it for an overlay hung from the caret.
- A masked text input copies and cuts nothing, as a native password field: it put its plain value on the clipboard.
- An element that takes the focus itself and has `.focuses(&ElementRef)` passes the focus on to its target on a click, and on Enter or Space when it is a focused button.
- A wrapping row or column with `align_items(Alignment::Stretch)` makes each item of a line as high (as wide) as the highest of that line. It ignored `Stretch`.
- `icons::EYE_OFF`, in the icon font.
- `.stops_inside(true)` on a button makes Tab stop at what takes the focus inside it too, after the button: a card pressed as a whole with buttons of its own.
- `PartStyle::corners(top_left, top_right, bottom_right, bottom_left)`: a radius for each corner of a part.
- A width or a height in percent is animated as a share of the parent's (`AnimatableValue::Percent`), where a transition of it ran from or to nothing; and the dash and the gap of a border are animatable as one value (`AnimatableProperty::BorderDash`, `Transition::border_dash()`).
- `lurq_inspect` reports `state.pressed` for a node described with `a11y_pressed`.
- A transition of `Height` between a height in pixels and none runs to and from the height of the content (`AnimatableValue::Fit`), where the height changed at once: a panel opens and closes with its content's height.
- `PartStyle::start_line(border)`: a line along the start side of a part, beside `underline`, and `PartStyle::min_height(length)`.
- `lurq_inspect` reports `state.selected` for a node described with `a11y_selected`.
- `PartStyle::max_width`; `padding_top`, `padding_bottom`, `padding_start` and `padding_end`, each laid over `padding_y` or `padding_x`, and `paddings()`, the four as they come out; `enter` and `exit`, keyframes a part plays as it appears and as it leaves (`PartAnimation`, with a duration and an easing of the theme's).
- `ui::FADE_IN` and `ui::FADE_OUT`: an opacity from nothing to whole and back, keyframes every window has (`0xFF01`, `0xFF02`).
- `Popup::takes_focus(bool)` and `Overlay::takes_focus(bool)`: the focus goes into the popup as it opens, to the first element in it that takes it, and back to what had it as it closes with the focus inside; while it is open its elements are in the order of Tab right after its anchor's.
- `Modal::takes_focus(bool)`: the focus goes into the modal as it opens, to its first stop of Tab, and back to what had it as it closes, as a browser's `<dialog>` does.
- `.focuses(&element_ref)` on any element: a click of it focuses the first focusable element under the one the ref is on, as a `<label for>` focuses its control.
- `ElementRef::focus_visible_within()` and its signal: a focus that shows its ring on the element or on something in it, CSS `:focus-within` for the keyboard.
- `FocusGroup::follow_pointer(bool)`: the pointer coming onto an element of a group the focus is in moves the focus there, as in a menu. `FocusGroup::typeahead(bool)`: typing a letter moves the focus to the element whose label starts with it, the label being `.focus_label(..)` or a button's one text. `KeyboardEvent::focus_within`: whether the focus is on the node whose key handler runs, or inside it.
- The engine's `Slider` has `on_change(Fn(f32, f32))` and `on_change_end(Fn(f32, f32))`: told the low value and the high one (a slider of one value's twice) of each change a person makes (a drag as it moves, a press on the track, a key, `lurq_set_value` of the MCP server) and of the end of it (the drag let go, the press or the key done) where the value is another than it was as it began, never of what the app sets nor of a range or a step that snaps the value. Each call adds a handler.
- The engine's `Slider` has `orientation(SliderOrientation)` (`vertical()`), laid out, painted and dragged along its height with the least value at the bottom, and `Slider::new_range` / `new_range_f32`: two thumbs on one track, the fill between them, a press moving the nearer thumb, the thumbs not crossing, each a stop of Tab. Every slider takes Home and End (to its ends) and Page Up and Page Down (a tenth of the range in whole steps). `SliderHandle::values()` reads a range's two values, and `set_from_ratio` moves its nearer thumb.
- A range slider in a form: `FormRangeSliderInput`, a field of its own kind (`FormHandle::range`, `range_field`, `range_control`, `ctx.range_control`, `FormValue::Range`, `FormValues::get_range`) submitted as two values under its name, with `FormOptions::validate_range` and `validators::range_span`.
- The engine's select has groups with a label over each and a line between them (`SelectOption::group`, `SelectStyle::group_label` and `separator`), a note at an option's end (`SelectOption::hint`, `SelectStyle::option_hint`), `SelectStyle::trigger_gap`, and a ring on its trigger (`SelectPartStyle::outline`).
- The MCP server: `lurq_set_value` on a range slider takes `[low, high]` and sets both values; `lurq_inspect` reports a node's `a11y_description` as `description`, and `a11y_checked` as `state.checked` for a control drawn of plain nodes (the design system's checkbox, radio and switch).
- `ui::Input` and `ui::Field`: an input is the engine's text input bound to a `Signal<String>` in the design's box, with a placeholder, a leading icon, the sizes `Sm`, `Md`, `Lg`, an edge that says its state (hover, focus, `invalid`, `warning`, `valid`), `read_only`, `disabled`, `full_width` and `on_submit` for Enter; a field holds any control with its label, a `required` or `optional` mark, a description or an `error` under it, or its label beside it (`orientation(Orientation::Horizontal)`). Parts and states for a recipe, and `InputView::control()`, `icon()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Textarea`: text of more than one line in the design's box, bound to a `Signal<String>`, with a placeholder, `rows(min, max)` between which it grows with its text before it scrolls, `invalid`, `disabled`, `name` and `on_input`, 260 wide as the design draws it unless `full_width`; the parts `Root`, `Control`, `Placeholder` for a recipe and `TextareaView::input()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Checkbox`: the design system's checkbox, checked, indeterminate, invalid and disabled, with its label beside it; its state is the app's (`checked`, `indeterminate`, `on_change`).
- `ui::Radio` and `ui::RadioGroup`: one of a few choices that exclude each other. `RadioGroup::new(signal, choices)` is bound to a `Signal<T>` like `ToggleGroup::new`, `RadioGroup::controlled` takes the choice from the app (`None` for none yet); radios are one under the other, 12 apart, with `invalid` and `disabled` for the whole group and `Choice::disabled` for one. A radio is a round mark with a dot while it is chosen and a label that is pressed as the mark is; a press of the one chosen does nothing. The parts `Root`, `Mark`, `Dot`, `Label` and the states `Chosen`, `Invalid`, `Disabled` for a recipe, and `RadioView::mark()` and `dot()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Slider`: a value picked along a track. The engine's slider dressed by a recipe, bound by `Slider::new` to a `Signal<i32>` or a `Signal<f32>` (`ui::SliderNumber`), or held by the app with `Slider::controlled(value, on_change)`, with `min`, `max` and `step`, `on_change(f64)` for each change a person makes and `on_change_end(f64)` for the end of it (the drag let go, the key's step made), the parts `Root`, `Track`, `Fill`, `Thumb` and the states `Disabled`, `Horizontal` and `Vertical` (`SliderOrientation` is `ui::Orientation`) for a recipe, and `SliderView::slider()`, `dressed()` and `value()` for a renderer. `RangeSlider` has the same, told the pair: `RangeSlider::new` of a `Signal<(i32, i32)>` or a `Signal<(f32, f32)>`, `RangeSlider::controlled((low, high), on_change)`. The engine's `SliderPartStyle` has an `outline`: a ring around a slider's part, the thumb's focus ring.
- `ui::Checkbox`: the design system's checkbox, checked, indeterminate, invalid and disabled, with its label beside it; its state is the app's (`checked`, `indeterminate`, `on_change`), or a `Signal<bool>` it is bound to (`bind`).
- `ui::Radio` and `ui::RadioGroup`: one of a few choices that exclude each other. `RadioGroup::new(signal, choices)` is bound to a `Signal<T>`, or a `Signal<Option<T>>` where none is selected yet, `RadioGroup::controlled` takes the choice from the app (`None` for none yet), and `on_change(&T)` is told the value selected; radios are one under the other, 12 apart, with `invalid` and `disabled` for the whole group and `Choice::disabled` for one. A radio is a round mark with a dot while it is `selected` and a label that is pressed as the mark is; a press of the one selected does nothing. The parts `Root`, `Mark`, `Dot`, `Label` and the states `Selected`, `Invalid`, `Disabled` for a recipe, and `RadioView::mark()` and `dot()` for a renderer.
- `ui::Slider`: a value picked along a track. The engine's slider dressed by a recipe, bound to a `Signal<i32>` or a `Signal<f32>` with `min`, `max` and `step`, the parts `Root`, `Track`, `Range`, `Thumb` and the state `Disabled` for a recipe, and `SliderView::slider()`, `dressed()` and `value()` for a renderer. The engine's `SliderPartStyle` has an `outline`: a ring around a slider's part, the thumb's focus ring.
- `Ctx::in_theme(&theme, |ctx| ..)` builds and mounts a subtree in another theme and returns it as an `Element`: a dense inspector, a dark side bar in a light window. The theme reaches the whole of the subtree: what a component takes from it as it is built (recipes, renderers, sizes, type, density, icons), and the roles on the nodes (palette, radii, border widths, shadows, spacing, typography styles, scroll bar, caret), which layout and paint read from that theme for the subtree. `.theme(&theme)` on any element is the second half alone, and `ui::in_theme` the first for what a closure builds.
