A popup, a modal and the menu of a select declared in the subtree are in its theme too. A change of the subtree's theme lays the window out again; a transition in the subtree runs between the colors of its theme.
- `ui::Button`: variants (`Primary`, `Secondary`, `Outline`, `Ghost`, `Destructive`), sizes, an element before and after the label, `disabled`, `full_width`, `on_click` (a click, Enter or Space), `.style(part, ..)` and `.rules(..)` for one instance, and `.force(interaction)` to draw it hovered, pressed or focused with nothing pointing at it. `ui::Themed` is what a component of an app implements to be themed the same way.
- `.part(&resolved_part)` on nodes and components gives an element the style of a part of a themed component, for a renderer.
- `lurq::VERSION`.
- `ThemeDefinition`: a theme as one value, its palettes, typography and every scale. `ThemeDefinition::neutral()` is the design system's theme and `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` that of lurq 0.61. `Theme::apply(definition)` installs one at once, leaving what a person or the system set (the appearance, the text scale, bold text, reduced motion), `Theme::definition()` reads the theme back as one, and `Theme::from_definition(...)` and `Theme::legacy()` make a theme from one. An app's own theme is a built-in one with some fields changed. See [theme](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#themes-as-one-value).
- The design's other scales, each a role enum with an `Extra` variant and a table on the theme: `ControlSize` (32, 36, 40), `IconSize` (12 to 24), `Density` (28, 36, 44, 48), `MotionDuration` (100, 160, 240, 400 ms), `MotionEasing` (standard, enter, exit), `OpacityLevel` (disabled 0.5, muted 0.7) and `BlurSize` (8, 20, 40), with `Theme::control_size_value(...)` and its like to read one and `set_…_value` to set one. `ThemeFocusRing` holds the focus ring's width, offset and color, and `outline()` gives it as an `Outline`.
- The theme's two font families: `Theme::set_sans_family(family)` changes the font of the whole interface, every typography style that was in the interface family, and `Theme::set_mono_family(family)` that of code. lurq bundles no font: the defaults are the system's own font and the generic `monospace`. `ThemeTypography::legacy()` is the typography of 0.61.
- `RadiusSize::Xl` (12) and `RadiusSize::Full`, a radius larger than any element, for a pill or a circle. `ThemeRadii::legacy()` has the radii of 0.61, and `ThemeShadows::legacy()` its elevations: the `Md` and `Lg` shadows are the design's now, softer and a little larger.
- `Theme::set_preferred_appearance(Some(Appearance::Dark))` chooses light or dark for the app whatever the system is in, and leaves the system's text size and bold text to the theme: the setting an app offers as "Light, Dark, System". `Theme::set_palette_color_for(appearance, role, color)` sets a role in the palette of one appearance.
- The design system's palette: `PaletteColor` and `ThemePalette` have the design's roles, surfaces with the color of what is drawn on them (`Background`/`Foreground`, `Card`, `Popover`, `Primary`, `Secondary`, `Muted`, `Highlight`, `Destructive`, each with its `…Foreground`), hover fills (`PrimaryHover`, `SecondaryHover`, `DestructiveHover`), veils for any fill (`StateHover`, `StatePressed`), status colors with a muted tint each, lines (`Border`, `Input`, `Ring`), and `Canvas`, `Scrim`, `Selection`, `Chart1`–`Chart5`, `GlassThin`, `GlassRegular`, `GlassThick`. `ThemePalette::light()` and `ThemePalette::dark()` are the design's two palettes, `ThemePalette::legacy()` is the palette of 0.61 color for color, and `ThemePalette::roles()` lists the built-in roles. In the light palette `Background` and `Card` are both white, and `Canvas` (`#e6e6eb`) is what is behind panels and cards. See [theme](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#palette).
- Smooth scrolling for the mouse wheel: a notch animates the content to where it sends it over 150 ms along a cubic ease-out, instead of drawing it 40 pixels on in one frame. Notches that come on the way add to where the content is going, one the other way turns it round, and whatever else scrolls the container stops it. `Tree::wheel` is the entry for a mouse's notches beside `Tree::scroll`, which scrolls at once; the winit shell sends it a whole number of lines (any line delta on macOS) and keeps a touchpad's travel on `scroll`. `ScrollState::scroll_target()` is where the content is going, `Theme::set_smooth_scroll(false)` turns the animation off, and reduced motion turns it off too. See [the mouse wheel](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#the-mouse-wheel).
- In a browser a notch of a mouse's wheel is animated as on the desktop. A browser tells a mouse's wheel and a touchpad alike, in pixels, so the shell takes a delta that comes to a whole number of notches along one axis, give or take a tenth, for a mouse's (on Windows Chrome and Edge tell a notch as 100 pixels, Firefox as 102 or 108) and scrolls 40 logical pixels for each; it scrolled a notch in one frame, and Firefox's 41 or 43 pixels. A touchpad's steps move the content at once as before, as does every wheel on macOS, where the browser tells the system's distance. See [the web build](docs/src/content/docs/web.md#input).
- `lurq::files`: what a person hands the app, dropped or chosen, as one type: `DroppedFile` (its name, its size, its path where the platform has one), `FileKind` by its extension, `DroppedFile::is_accepted` for extensions and kinds (`"image/*"`), and `format_size`. `FileDialog` opens the system's dialog for files to open, off the loop, as a future for `ctx.future_action` (`rfd`, behind the new feature `file_dialog`; on Windows modal to the window it is opened over; a browser's file input gives names and sizes, iOS none); under an MCP server with a `FileDialogBroker` registered it asks the broker and opens no system dialog. See [styling and events](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#files-a-person-gives).
- `ui::DropZone`: where files are dropped or chosen, the design's zone of 320 by 140. `accept` takes extensions and kinds, with `multiple`, `max_size` and `max_files`; `on_files` is told what it took from a drop or its dialog and `on_reject` what it refused and why (`RejectedFile`, `RejectReason`). Mounted, it draws a drag it takes in the info colors ("Drop to add") and refuses one it takes nothing of in the destructive colors, and a press, Enter or Space opens the dialog.
- `ui::FileUpload` and `ui::UploadFile`: the design's upload, a "Choose files" button over the list of the files given, each with its kind's icon, its name cut with an ellipsis, its size or how far it is sent with a bar, the check once sent, and for a failed file its error and a retry. The list is the app's (`new(Signal<Vec<UploadFile>>)`, `controlled`, `at`), moved on by the app with `uploading`, `done` and `failed`; `on_files`, `on_reject`, `on_remove`, `on_retry`; `zone(true)` puts a drop zone in place of the button. Mounted, the focus of a removed file's control goes on to the next file.
- `ui::Button::ref_element`: the ref of a button's element, for an app to focus or measure it.
- `ui::PropertyGrid`: an inspector's names and the editors of their values in categories that open and close, the design's of 300 wide. A `Property<V>` is typed, its `default` checked when the app compiles: `text`, `number` and `number_with` a unit (with the board's chevrons `stepper`), `slider`, `toggle`, `choice`, `color` (the board's swatch beside its hex, the picker in a popover, `eyedropper` where asked), `xy` and `pair` (two numbers with their letters), `custom`; a value not its default has its name marked and a reset. `PropertyCategory` with `default_open`. The editors are `ui`'s controls; `on_change` is told the id of the property a person changed. To the keys it is a tree with editors in it (Up, Down, Left, Right, Tab or Enter into the editor, Escape back), the divider between the names and the editors is dragged, and to tooling it is a `treegrid` whose rows say their values. A category opens and closes as an accordion's item does, its rows sliding open and its chevron turning, in either form of the grid; in a virtual list by the motion a mounted `TreeView`'s rows have too. Mounted, it keeps the open categories and the divider, and a grid given a `height` builds only the rows near its view (`FocusRows`).
- `ui::WindowChrome` and `ui::WindowControls`: the title bar of a window the app draws itself, on the design system's recipe, as Windows, macOS, GNOME and KDE draw theirs (`WindowPlatform`, the platform the app runs on unless it names another): the app's icon, the title, the app's own controls at the start and the end of the bar (`leading`, `trailing`: a menubar goes in the leading room), tabs (`tabs`), and the window's controls in the platform's order and look, named by `minimize_label`, `maximize_label`, `restore_label` and `close_label`. Mounted it is the window's frame through the engine's `WindowChrome`: the bar drags the window, a double press maximizes it, a right press shows the system's menu, and it is drawn muted while the window is not in front and with a restore control while it is maximized. Converted into an element it is the still form, a picture of the window. See [Window Chrome](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-chrome).
- `WindowHandle::show_system_menu()`: the system's menu of the window where the pointer is, as a right press on a title bar the system draws shows it (Windows; nothing elsewhere). `ChromeTitleBar` shows it on a right press, and `ChromeTitleBar::draw(|window, height| ..)` draws the whole bar from the app's own element, which the bar still drags the window around. See [components](docs/src/content/docs/components.md).
- A `Row` or a `Column` laid out with unbounded room and a least length, as the content of a `ScrollBoth::fill_width` is, grows the children that have a basis into what that least length leaves, as a flex container with a least size does on the web: tabs that share the width of a strip, and scroll past their least. It was laid out at that length with its children at their bases. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#flex).
- `.window_control(WindowControl::Minimize | Maximize | Close)` on any element says it is one of the window's controls, in a title bar the app draws itself, and the winit shell tells the system: on Windows the window answers the system's hit test (`WM_NCHITTEST`) with `HTMINBUTTON`, `HTMAXBUTTON` or `HTCLOSE` where the element is laid out and nothing over it takes the pointer, so Windows 11 opens its snap layouts over the maximize control as the pointer rests on it ([#128](https://github.com/ivanzaida/lurq/issues/128)). The system then sends the non-client mouse messages there, and the shell gives them to the element as the pointer's own: it is under the pointer, held down, clicked and given its cursor as any element is, and its own `on_click` acts on the window. `WindowControls` and `ui::WindowChrome` mark their three controls. `Tree::window_control_at(x, y)` answers a shell that drives a `Tree` itself. `ui::WindowControl` is now this `node::WindowControl`. Elsewhere the mark does nothing. See [components](docs/src/content/docs/components.md#the-windows-controls-in-a-title-bar-of-your-own).
- `ui::WindowChrome` shows the window's own icon by default: the image the app set as the window's icon (`WindowHandle::set_icon`, the shell's `with_icon`), followed as it changes, and the box where there is none. `icon(..)` takes a `WindowChromeIcon`: a glyph's name, `None`, or an image of the app's (`ImageData`, with the `raster` feature). `WindowHandle::icon()` reads the window's icon. See [Window Chrome](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-chrome).
- `DragEvent::outside_window`: the pointer of a drag is out of the window, past any of its edges. See [styling and events](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#a-drag-holds-the-pointer).
- `.wheel_across(true)` on `ScrollHorizontal` and `ScrollBoth`: a wheel turned up and down scrolls the container sideways, as a browser's strip of tabs does. Off by default. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md).
- `ui::WindowTabs` and `ui::WindowTab`: the tabs of a desktop app's title bar, as a browser or an editor has them, bound to a `Signal<T>` (`new`) or a `Signal<Option<T>>` (`optional`), or held by the app (`controlled`, `controlled_optional`). A tab has an icon, a dot for unsaved changes (the x in its place under the pointer) and a close button; the strip tells `on_close` (the close button, a middle press, Delete, Ctrl+W), `on_add` (its add button shown once it is given), `on_reorder(from, to)` for a tab dragged along it or moved by Ctrl+Shift+PageDown and PageUp, and `on_detach` for one dragged out of it. Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+Shift+Tab, Ctrl+PageDown and PageUp select and Ctrl+W (Cmd+W on macOS) closes from anywhere in the window, unless `shortcuts(false)`; the tabs are one stop of Tab whose arrows select and whose Delete closes. Tabs that do not fit share the room of a row or a column down to 72 each and then scroll, a wheel turned up and down over them scrolls them sideways, a tab let go out of the window past any edge is detached, and a press on a tab does not reach the title bar around it. Mounted (`mount(ctx)`), the dragged tab follows the pointer. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- `ui::DataGrid` writes its rows out: Export in each column's menu, the rows shown or those selected (or the range of cells) as CSV or tab-separated text, the titles of the columns shown first; `on_export(|export: &GridExport| ..)` is told the text to save it, and with no handler it goes on the clipboard (`export(bool)`, `export_labels(ExportLabels)`). `ui::to_csv(&columns, &rows)`, `to_tsv` and `to_delimited(.., ExportFormat)` write any rows in a table's columns. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-grid).
- `ui::DataGrid` rows as a tree and a row's detail: `tree(|row| parent_key)` shows each row under the row whose key its parent is, indented 18 a level in the first column with a chevron that opens and closes it (Right and Left on that cell, Left on a closed row going to its parent), a filter keeping the rows a kept row is under, the rows open the app's or the grid's own (`expanded`, `expanded_controlled`, `on_expanded_change`); `detail(|row| element)` shows an element under an open row as wide as the view, measured as it is laid out (`details`, `details_controlled`, `on_detail_change`). Both are lines of the grid's windowing: only what is in view is built. New parts `TablePart::Detail` and `TablePart::TreeToggle`; a `TableRow` says its `tree` level.
- `ui::DataGrid::toolbar(true)`: the board's Table Toolbar over the grid, built from a `Toolbar`: a search field that is the grid's search (the app's, or the grid's own), a tag for each filter set whose × takes it off, Filter (a menu of the columns that filter, each opening its filter), and at the end the density, which the grid takes once a person chooses one, the columns to show and Export. `toolbar_labels(GridBarLabels)` says its words otherwise.
- `ui::Persona`: a person as an avatar with a name and a line under it (`new(name)`, `description`, `presence`, `presence_label`, `avatar(Avatar)` for a picture, an icon or a shape of the app's own, `size(PersonaSize)`, the avatar's five sizes with the text to match). A name or a line longer than its room ends in an ellipsis and the avatar keeps its size; to tooling it is a group called by the name. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#persona).
- `ui::Presence`: the dot of an avatar on its own, `Presence::new(AvatarPresence::Online)`, with `size(AvatarSize)` and `label`; it is the avatar's recipe's dot, with no ring. `Avatar::presence_label(&str)` says the presence to tooling in the app's words. See [Avatar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#avatar).
- `ui::InfoLabel`: a label with a mark after it that explains it in a tooltip (`new(label)`, `info(text)`, `info_label`, `icon`, `placement`, `offset`, `collision`, `arrow`, `force_open`, `mount(ctx)`). The tooltip shows while the pointer rests on the label and when Tab comes to the mark; a label longer than its room ends in an ellipsis. `ui::Field::info(text)` draws the same mark after a field's label. See [Info Label](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#info-label).
- `ui::AiLabel`: the mark on what a model made, sparkles and "AI" in the info color (`new()`, `text`, `label`, `icon`, `size(AiLabelSize)`). See [AI Label](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#ai-label).
- `ui::BackToTop`: a round button that shows once the content of a `ScrollState` is scrolled past its `threshold` (300 unless told) and takes it back to its top (`new(&scroll)`, `label`, `icon`, `on_click`, which may refuse, `force_shown`, `disabled`, `mount(ctx)`). See [Back To Top](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#back-to-top).
- `ui::PageLoader`: what stands in the place of a whole page while it starts, a ring of 28 over a title and a description in the middle of the room it is given (`new(title)`, `description`, `spinner(Spinner)`). See [Spinner](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#spinner).
- `Ctx::scroll_to_top_of(&ScrollState)` and `Tree::scroll_state_to_top(&ScrollState)`: take a scroll container back to its top as a wheel's scroll goes, over the frames that follow, and at once while motion is reduced or the theme's smooth scroll is off. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#scroll).

Fixes:

- A component built again on its own costs itself and its way to the root, not the tree: its output is looked for only down the subtrees that hold a component's, the nodes it brought are numbered and take the disabled state of what they are in without the rest of the tree being numbered again, and the column it is in is patched where it changed without the rest of it being walked again. The tree is no longer looked through for an element that takes every key after every build and with every key, where none was ever built. See [What a Frame Walks](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#what-a-frame-walks).
- A change of an element's state (the pointer on it, a press, the focus, a file dragged over it, its interaction group lit) has the transitions looked at again only on the way from the root to that element and to what its group holds. Any such change anywhere used to send the next frame through every transition of the window: a Tab in a Markdown document of 3,000 blocks spent 1.3 to 1.6 ms there.
- With `perf_profile`, building a tree allocates what it does without it: a frame's sections read no clock and keep nothing until something asks for them.
- A node built with DevTools or MCP keeps what they show of it (its component's props, signals, memos, effects and contexts, what it says of itself) in one box made only where there is something to show: a `Node` is 1,160 bytes with every feature, where it was 1,328, over the budget of 1,272 the size checks hold it to, and the build of every feature's tests passes them again. An app built with neither is as it was.
- A text the clip cuts is cut on whole pixels inside the clip's edge. At a scale where the edge falls inside a row of pixels (150%), the scissor, rounding out, drew that row of a text cut there whole: the words of a `ui::DataGrid`'s rows showed in the top row of pixels of its header, in and out as they scrolled under it, and the header looked as if it jumped.
- Scrolling an element into view (a focus request, `Ctx::scroll_into_view`, `ElementHandle::scroll_into_view`) keeps it clear only of what sticks over it: what sticks to the top or the bottom where it spans the element across, what sticks to a side where it is beside it. A tall list that stuck to the top at the side of a page took the band it reached from the whole width of the page, so a press of a cell in view in the middle (a data grid's, which focuses it) scrolled the page by hundreds of pixels.
- The bars of a scroll container that keeps them clear of what sticks (`scrollbars_clear_of_sticky`, a `ui::Table`'s and a `ui::DataGrid`'s) keep clear along their own axis only: the bar down stands at the container's end edge, over the edge of a column pinned at the end, where it stood in by that column's width over a column that scrolls; the bar across stands at the bottom edge, where it stood above what sticks at the bottom. No other component asks for it.
- `ui::DataGrid`: a cell's editor is laid out in the cell's room at any column width, the select with its chevron and the number input with both its buttons, where each kept its own width (260 for a select) and was cut at the cell's end. A box in a cell turns at one press, focused or not, and once for a double press, where it took a double press. A grid given no id makes one of its own (`grid-N`) for every id it names, where every such grid of a page named its editor `editor`.
- A frame costs what changed and what is in view, not what the tree holds. Its walks (the overlay index, the element refs, the dropped nodes, the scroll check, the transitions' and animations' ticks) pass by a subtree that has nothing for them, which each subtree knows and keeps through changes and rebuilds; a cached subtree served as it was is not prepared again; the paint culls a subtree that does not clip where all it may paint is out of view. A frame scrolling a Markdown document of 3,000 blocks fell from 6.5–14 ms to 0.4 ms. See [What a Frame Walks](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#what-a-frame-walks).
- A mounted Markdown document mounts each of its code blocks as a component of its own, keyed by its place: a block whose code, language and highlights did not change is not built again with the document. A document that grows at its end, as a streamed answer does, builds again in 62–66 ms where it took 72–75 (1,000 blocks) and in 195–211 where it took 208–218 (3,000), measured side by side; what a block draws is as it was.
- A component mounted with `ctx.mount` is in the theme it is mounted in now: one mounted again inside `ctx.in_theme(&other, ..)` (a stage switched to the other appearance) renders in that theme. Its slot kept the theme it was made in, so a Markdown document, or any component mounted on such a stage, kept drawing in the app's theme: white words on a light stage.
- A transition's value that changes because its element is read in another theme now (mounted inside `in_theme`, or out of it) is there at once, as one that changes with the window's theme is. The copy button of a code block on a stage switched to the other appearance faded from the other appearance's color.
- An overlay opened again while it leaves is the same overlay: it ends its leaving, and what had the focus in it has it again, its scroll and what was typed in it are kept, and its fade goes on from where its exit had got to. It was a new one entering over the one leaving, both painted for the time of the exit, a dialog's two backdrops over each other. `Popover`, `Dialog`, `Sheet`, `Menu`, `Tooltip` and `HoverCard` take theirs back this way, and a `Drawer`, a child of its row, by the rule of `presence()`. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#presence-entering-and-leaving-layers).
- A `ui::Slider` is no longer than the room it is given: 240 where it has the room, shorter in a narrower one, its thumb on its track at every value. A slider in a row narrower than 240 was laid out at 240 and cut at the row's end, so that its thumb stood at the end of what showed at 50 and under what was beside it past that: the `PropertyGrid`'s Opacity row, a slider beside a number in a `Field`.
- The line of a window's outline that `WindowChrome` draws (`ChromeBorderPolicy`) takes no pointer. It took the presses on the window's outermost pixel wherever no zone that resizes the window lay over it, so in a maximized window the close control was not pressed in the very corner of the screen, where a thrown pointer stops, nor any control along the top edge; the content's outermost pixels heard nothing either. The pointer there is now on what is under the line.
- `lurq::eyedropper` on Windows: the screen is read with a plain `BitBlt` of the composed desktop, no longer with `CAPTUREBLT`, which hid and showed the cursor at each read (at each move of the pointer and ten times a second) and could make it flicker; on Windows 8 and later the plain read has other apps' layered windows in it as well, and the pick's own windows stay out of it.
- A frame that only scrolls a long content does not walk it again: a subtree served from the layout cache as it was last served, with no text's layout changed anywhere since, is the tree prepared then, and a parent laid out anew no longer prepares again the scroll containers of what its children already prepared. A Markdown document of 3,000 blocks in a scroll view went through every one of its code blocks' scroll areas and every text of it on each frame of scrolling.
- The first frame a `ui::Toolbar` or a `ui::CommandBar` with `overflow(true)` paints at a width is the settled one: its line after the last group shown, its More button's menu and its state of overflowing are those of what fits there. The bar learned which controls had gone into the menu after the frame was painted (`observe_rect`), so the frame at a new width drew a line before the More button where a group had just gone; it learns it before the frame is painted (`observe_rect_before_paint`), as the layout guide says.
- A span of a `RichText` whose size or line height is not its first span's is drawn and advances at its own size. Every span was drawn at the first span's size: a Markdown paragraph that began with a key or with code was drawn all at the key's 11 or the code's 13, and in one that began with words a key and code were at the words' 15. Letter spacing stays in pixels.
- A key (`<kbd>`) and code in a Markdown sentence stand between the spaces of the sentence: their box has room of its own beside its words, as wide as its padding, so "Press <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> to" has a space before each box and after it, where the boxes were drawn over those spaces and touched each other and the words. The room is presentation, copied and selected with nothing; a box goes to the next line whole, as a word does, and a line starts at its box. A key's words copy as they are, without the thin spaces they were set in. Where a space stands beside a box, the box is the design's 6 from the word or the box beside it (`ThemeMarkdown::box_gap`): the space stays the sentence's and the box's room makes up the rest, from a space of about 0.27 of the sentence's size.
- A control a row dropped for want of room (a `ShrinkLimit::Drop` child: a toolbar's group behind its More button, a command bar's behind its chevron) stays dropped when what is in it renders again while the row keeps its size. Closing a `ui::Toolbar`'s or a `ui::CommandBar`'s menu with Escape, after a press opened it, drew every group that had gone into the menu in the bar again, at its full width and over the groups shown, until the bar was laid out anew (its width changed): the layout patched the dropped child in where it stood and lost the mark that it was dropped. A dropped child that changes now lays its row out again, which decides anew what fits.
- `lurq::eyedropper` on Windows: the loupe no longer flickers as it follows the pointer. It was painted with GDI straight onto its window, its panel first and then its cells, its outline and its hex, at each move of the pointer and ten times a second, and the screen was composed with it half drawn; it is now drawn whole into a bitmap and given to its layered window with its place in one call (`UpdateLayeredWindow`), drawn before it shows, and drawn again only when what it shows or its place changes. Its corners are smooth, its edge following them.
- What has the focus in a pane of a mounted `ui::Splitter` keeps it when what holds the splitter renders again: the panes' boxes, given new refs by every render, were new nodes, and the focus, a caret or a scroll in them was lost.
- A `ui::Splitter`, and a `ui::DockLayout` built of them, shows its settled sizes in its first frame: a pane under its least size is at it, and each divider at the edge of its pane, with nothing moving in the frames after. The splitter measured its room after layout and laid its panes out again a frame later, and its dividers a frame after that: the explorer of a dock layout a quarter of a narrow room wide was drawn at that quarter, then at its least width, with the divider through the middle of it meanwhile, whenever the layout was shown or the app set its arrangement. It now measures with `ElementRef::observe_rect_before_paint`, and the copies of the panes under the dividers are laid out as the panes are.
- A child of a `Row` or a `Column` that stretches its children (`align_items(Alignment::Stretch)`), whose length along the line is a share of it (`width(Dimension::Pct(25.0))` in a row, a `max_width` in percent too) and which is not as high as the line on its own, takes its share once. Stretched, it was laid out again under the length it had been measured at and took its share of that: a quarter of a row of 400 was 25 wide, where it is 100. A child that filled the line's height already, or had a size in pixels, was laid out right, which hid it.
- What an observer of `ElementRef::observe_rect_before_paint` changes through an element ref (`ElementRefMut::set_size`) is laid out before that frame is painted, as what it changes through a signal is rendered: it waited for the next frame. A frame is laid out again until what is watched so settles, four times at most, where it was once: what is measured inside what was laid out again (a splitter in a splitter's pane) settles in the same frame. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#a-form-that-follows-its-own-room).
- A text input built anew by the render that moves the focus away from it no longer goes on painting its caret. The render carried its state, its focus with it, into the node that took its place under another id, and the focus left the old node's state alone: a row of an inspector that took the focus back with Escape left a caret in its editor.
- A single-line `Row` or `Column` whose dropped children free more room than it needed gives the rest to its growing children, as it gives free room: a spacer between two groups of a bar keeps the second at the end once a child of the first has dropped. The rest went nowhere before, and the line ended short of its room.
- The room a child that drops out of a line leaves (`ShrinkLimit::Drop`, `shrink_drop_below`) goes to the line's growing children, by their factors. It was left empty: the growing children had been laid out while the line overflowed, so a `ui::Toolbar`'s spacer stayed at nothing and its More button stood in the middle of the bar, after the controls that stayed. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#give-way-order-and-limits).
- A labelled group of a `ui::Toolbar` (a command bar's) has the line at its end and no line of the bar beside it, and the last group, or the last before the spacer, has none: two lines stood between two labelled groups, and one after the last. No line stands between the spacer and More.
- An absolute child of a stack sized in percent where the stack's constraints leave that side unbounded is a share of the stack as laid out, not of nothing: a layer over a stack as high as its content is as high. See [Stack](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md).
- lurq compiles without the `router` feature again: `ui::Navbar` gave its links' paths to a sidebar's items with no router to go to them.
- A `ui::Table`'s cells are as high as their row. A first column that stays covered only the line of its text, so a taller cell that scrolled under it (a badge) showed over and under the name.
- What a scroll container holds is drawn on a whole device pixel. A text is painted on whole device pixels and a box where it is, so at a scale that is no whole number (150%) a scroll offset of half a device pixel moved every label a pixel against its box, and back at the next step of the wheel: the text of a field jumped as its page scrolled. The scroll's own offset is as it was given.
- A `ui::Table`'s titles and the header's checkbox stand in the middle of the header's height, and the skeletons of its loading rows in the middle of theirs; they stood at the top edge.
- A node made sticky (`.sticky(..)`) after it was laid out sticks. Its layout was kept from before, which said it did not, so a bar turned sticky in a page already shown scrolled away with the page.
- With the `devtools` feature, a value of the design system goes in a signal: every variant, size and state name, and its shapes, orientations and positions (`AvatarShape`, `ToastPosition`, `Placement`, `SliderOrientation` and the rest) are `DevtoolsInspectable`. An app that kept an `AvatarSize` in a signal did not compile with DevTools on, and neither did `ui::Combobox` itself.
- A token of a text input in its selected look is drawn over the selection's highlight. The highlight was painted over the chip, so a selected token of a `TokenInput` was a white label on the pale selection between strips of the chip's own fill. A token the selection covers that has no selected look keeps the highlight over it.
- The caret at the end of an atomic token of a text input stands past its chip, and the one at its start before it. It stood where the token's text ends, inside the chip, between the label and the cross of a `TokenInput`'s token. A token the caret goes into is as it was.
- A scrollbar that sets no color of its own is drawn in the theme's `Ring`, light or dark. Its thumb was black at a third of its opacity, which a dark theme does not show. A `ScrollBarStyle` whose `thumb_color` was set keeps it, 0.62's black included; `ScrollBarStyle::THEME_THUMB` is the color that stands for the theme's, a mark of its own, and `thumb_in(&palette)` says what a style is drawn in. `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` keeps 0.62's black thumb, not its palette's ring (`#2563eb`).
- Selected text is in the theme's `Selection` where a text or an input names no selection color. The light theme's blue was drawn on every theme, so a selection on a dark theme was a light bar under light text.
- A child that nothing names keeps its identity when a sibling is put in or taken out before it. Children without a key, an id or a ref were matched by their place alone, so a button that got a sibling before it handed its node id to the sibling: the new button ran the old one's color transition, and took its hover and its focus. When a list of such children grows or shrinks and its end looks as it did (the same kinds of node, the same shape, the same text), the children of the end are now the ones that were there. A list as long as it was is matched place by place as before, and so is a list of children that cannot be told apart.
- A clip inside a clip with rounded corners keeps the rounded corners. When the inner clip was not the same rectangle as the outer one, the outer one's corners were dropped, so what clipped and lay along an edge of a rounded element was drawn square past its corners: a rail along the bottom of a rounded panel, a picture or a header band across its top, a scrolling list down to its bottom corners. A clip now carries the rounded clips it is under with their own rectangles, and content is cut by each of them as nested `overflow: hidden` boxes with `border-radius` are in CSS; a child with corners of its own at a rounded parent's corner is cut by both. Layer and backdrop blurs are cut the same way. A press past a rounded corner no longer reaches what the corner cuts off. `ClipRect::border_radius` is replaced by `ClipRect::rounded` (`RoundedClip`), with `ClipRect::rect`, `ClipRect::rounded_rect`, `ClipRect::intersect` and `ClipRect::contains`. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#visual-modifiers).
- Text cut by a clip is drawn up to the clip and no further. A text's clip is let out by a physical pixel for its glyphs' rounding, and the scissor rounds it out by one more; on a side where the clip cuts the text, that drew the next rows of the cut line past the cut. The line a multiline `TextInput` had scrolled under its top or bottom edge was drawn over the input's border, at a scale of 1.5 up to the box's outer edge, on the frames where the page around had scrolled it to some fractions of a pixel and not on others. The same reached the long line of a single-line input (into its padding) and a text taller than a parent that clips it. A text inside its clip keeps the pixel of slop.
- A disabled `Slider` took a press that landed on its row: with nothing under the pointer the slider was looked up by its row alone, disabled or not.
- The More button of a `ui::Toolbar` opened by Enter or Space gives the focus to the first item of its menu, as a menu opened from its button does: the focus stayed on More, and no item of the menu could be reached from the keyboard. The tooltip of More is anchored to the button and the menu now to a row of its own around it.
- What a `ui::ActionBar` says gives way to the room on one line and is cut with an ellipsis: a long text pushed the actions and the clear button out of the bar, and at a larger text size the clear button was cut by the end of the bar.
- A primary `ui::Button` held down is drawn in its `Primary` fill under the `StatePressed` veil: over its hover fill the veil was lost in both appearances, and a press looked like a hover.
- A `ui::Button` or a `ui::Toggle` with an icon and an empty label is a square, as one with `icon_only(true)` is: it kept the padding and the gap of a label, 56 and 44 wide where a square is 36.
- The focus ring of a `ui::Tag`'s remove button is around the button's edge, clear of the text: at the theme's offset it covered the end of the label. The ring of a focused `ui::Tabs` panel stands 4 out from it, clear of a text that runs to its edges.
- A `ui::Button` or a `ui::Toggle` in a room narrower than its label stays in that room: it keeps its padding and its icon and cuts its label with an ellipsis. Its label never gave way, so the button ran past what held it: a single long button of a `ui::Popconfirm` was drawn past the panel's edge. A button with room is as wide as its label, as before.
- A `ui::Tooltip` names the element it is on only where nothing else does: a button named by its label was named by its tooltip, which now describes it.
- A modal opened while another is open is over it, wherever it is declared. Modals stacked in the order of the tree, so a modal declared before an open one (an app's search in its shell, opened over a dialog of the page) was drawn under it: it got no focus, no keys and no press, and Escape closed the dialog under it. Open modals now stack in the order they opened, as a browser's top layer does: the topmost takes the focus (`takes_focus(true)`), Tab, the pointer and Escape, and as it closes the focus goes back into the one under it. See [modals](docs/src/content/docs/modals.md#behavior).
- A submenu opens beside its item on the side it asks for while the window has room there. A layer was laid out on its own at the size of the window's constraints, so in a window (which lays its page out at its own size) a menu's items were as wide as the window, and a submenu placed by its item found no room on its right and opened on its left. A layer is laid out at the size it is drawn.
- A menu or popup that takes the focus gets it as it opens when its anchor has a tooltip too. The layer the focus went into was looked up by its anchor, and the tooltip's layer on the same element came first: a `Menu` hung from a button with a `Tooltip`, opened by Enter, left the focus on the button. The layer looked up is the one that takes the focus. `ui::Toolbar`'s More menu hangs from its button again, where it hung from a row around it for this.
- Choosing an item of a submenu gives the focus back to what opened the menu, as choosing in the menu does. The focus went back to the submenu's item, which closed with it, and was left nowhere. A popup that takes the focus and hangs from an element of another one that closes with it gives the focus back from the outermost that closed.
- Tab in an open `ui::Menu` closes it and the menus it hangs from, and goes on from what opened it to the next stop. The menu stayed open and let the focus go. Tab in an open `Select` closes its list and goes on to the next stop too, where it kept the focus on the select.
- A `ui::Menu` item's long label gives way with an ellipsis, and its shortcut, check and chevron keep their room and the gap before them. The panel is at most 360 wide; a long label was cut at the panel's edge and pushed the shortcut out of it, and a long shortcut was set against the label.
- Tab goes on from where the focus was when nothing has it, as a browser does from its sequential focus navigation starting point. Enter or Escape in a text field, what had the focus leaving the tree or being disabled, `ctx.blur()`, and a modal that closed with nothing to give the focus back to all left it nowhere, and the next Tab went to the first control of the window. Now the place is kept, and Tab and Shift+Tab go on from it in the Tab order; a press on something that takes no focus puts the place there. See [focus navigation](docs/src/content/docs/focus-navigation.md#where-tab-goes-on-from).
- A `TreeView` row is selected by a click, as the button comes up over the row it went down on, as every other row of the design system is. It was selected as the button went down, so a press dragged off the row had selected it. A double click activates it.
- `force(..)` of a component made of items draws the interaction on one item, the one Tab would stop on: the chosen segment of a `SegmentedControl`, the first selected row of a `TreeView` (else its first), the selected tab of `Tabs`, the first item of a `Menubar` and the first link of a `Breadcrumb`. Each drew it on every segment, row, tab, title or link at once, a picture that never happens, and `Tabs` on its panel and buttons too. `TabsView::entry`, `MenubarView::entry`, `BreadcrumbView::entry` and `BreadcrumbView::step_part` say which item it is and give its parts; `TabsView::part`, `MenubarView::part` and `BreadcrumbView::part` are no longer forced.
- A `MultiSelect`'s input is its one stop of Tab, as a `TokenInput`'s and a `Mentions`'s are: the cross of each tag was a stop before it. With nothing typed, Left and Right go onto the tags and along them, and Backspace or Delete takes away the tag they are on. A choice clears what was typed, so the list shows every option again: after typing "ty" and Enter the query stayed and the list stayed filtered.
- `InputOtp`: what is typed past the end of a full code goes nowhere. It replaced the last cell, and `on_complete` was called again with each character. Ctrl+A (Cmd+A) then Backspace or Delete clears the code, where it took one character away. A value the app sets is taken as typing takes it, and the signal set to that: one that held letters in a code of digits was shown with them.
- A `NumberInput` takes its value into its range as soon as the range changes under it, as a `Slider` does: one bound to a signal shows it, writes it and tells `on_change`; a controlled one shows it clamped, tells `on_change` once and shows what the app answers with. A `max` set lower than the value kept the value over it, with the plus dimmed, until the next key or the focus leaving.
- A `ui::Dialog`'s and a `ui::Sheet`'s title and description wrap inside the panel: each was one line, cut at the panel's padding, so the board's own description of the dialog lost its second line. A drawer over the page is a sheet, and wraps the same.
- `ui::Steps` in a row with descriptions share the width they are given: a step gives way before the lines between the steps, which are never shorter than 24, the descriptions wrap first and a title is cut with an ellipsis only once every step is at its title, the last step's first. The steps ran out of their room and the lines were gone.
- A long text gives way where it ran out of its room or pushed what was beside it out. A `ui::Progress` label and a `ui::Separator` label are cut with an ellipsis, the separator keeping a line of 24 on either side; the hint of a `ui::SearchField`'s key is never wider than 112, where it covered the field; a `ui::ProgressDialog`'s status is cut with an ellipsis after the count, which it cut; a `ui::Tooltip`'s long text wraps across the box with its shortcut under it, where it was squeezed into a column beside the shortcut; a `ui::Popconfirm`'s buttons go one under the other where they do not fit side by side, where the confirm button was cut by the panel.
- A `ui::Field` is at least as wide as its control: a control wider than the field that does not fill it, an `InputOtp` of six cells, widens the field, where the field cut it; beside the label the field is the label's column, the gap and the control. Its description wraps in the field's width where it was one line and cut. Beside the label, a control lower than an input, a switch, stands level with the label; it stood at the top of the row.
- The line under a `ui::Link` the pointer is on is straight and under its text alone: it was the bottom edge of the link's box, which is rounded for the focus ring, so it curled up at both ends and ran under the icon. It shows while the focus is in the link too.
- The placeholder of a masked `ui::PasswordInput` is set as any placeholder: it had the spacing meant for the dots.
- A wrapped text starts where it was laid out at a scale that is not whole. Paint shapes the text again at the window's scale, where a line can hold a word more than the layout's did; the text then had a line fewer than its box and stood lower in it by half a line. It keeps its offset from the layout now: the description of a step next to the others, at 1.5.
- A person dismissing a modal is heard wherever the focus is. The engine's `Modal` told no one, so a `ui::Sheet`'s `on_dismiss` heard Escape only with the focus in the sheet, and a mounted bottom sheet told no one of a finger dragging it down or tapping beside it. `Modal::on_dismiss` runs after Escape closed the modal, and `SheetProps::on_dismiss` after the drag, the tap or Escape put the sheet away; neither runs when the app clears the signal. `ui::Dialog`, `ui::Sheet`, `ui::Drawer` and `ui::CommandPalette` take their `on_dismiss` from it. Tab in a submenu tells `on_dismiss` of every menu it closes, not only the submenu's.
- An element is no longer left drawn with a fill, a border color or a foreground it had only in a state. A transition of a property the element has no value of at rest (a row with no fill and a `hovered` one) ended only where it was heading: with the pointer gone while it ran, it went on to the hovered value and left it drawn, and nothing asked for a frame after it. The run now ends when the value goes away, and the value is forgotten, so the next one is there at once and does not fade from a color the element had long lost. In a `ui::Sidebar` the item of the page that was open before lit up under a pointer that crossed it and stayed lit: its fill faded from the selected one to the hovered one, and the pointer left before that ended.
- An element is under the pointer on the device pixels it covers, up to and not on its right and bottom edges. The first row of pixels under a row hovered it, one of the three rows of a gap of 2 at 150%, and of two rows that touch the upper one took the pointer on the lower one's first pixels; the same for the column beside an element. `HitRect::contains` tells the same.
- The divider of a `ui::PropertyGrid` is as high as its rows in a grid whose height is its rows' own, where it had no height and so was under no pointer: it could be moved with the keys alone.
- The width of a control's recipe is the most it takes, not the least: in a room narrower than it, a window as narrow as a phone or a narrow panel of a wide one, the control is as wide as the room. `ui::Input` (260), `ui::Textarea` (260), `ui::Select` and `ui::Combobox` (260), `ui::MultiSelect` (280), `ui::TokenInput` (260), `ui::Mentions` (280), `ui::SearchField` (280), `ui::InputGroup` (280), `ui::PasswordInput`, `ui::Fieldset` and `ui::ErrorSummary` (400), `ui::FileUpload` (300, 320 with its zone), `ui::DropZone` (320), `ui::Progress` (240), `ui::Outcome` (300), `ui::Toast` (360), `ui::MessageBox` (400), `ui::ProgressDialog` (420), `ui::PropertyGrid` (300), `ui::CheckboxCard` and `ui::RadioCard` (220) kept their width there and were cut by what held them. Each still has its recipe's width where there is room for it, and `full_width` fills as before.
- A `ui::Field` is no wider than the room it has: its label and its description were given the recipe's width in pixels, 260, and ran out of a narrower room with the control. They wrap in the room, and a control that keeps a width of its own still widens the field where there is room for it.
- `ui::DatePicker`, `ui::DateRangePicker`, `ui::TimePicker` and `ui::ColorField` are no wider than the room they have: the field and its panel were held in a box as wide as the field made itself, so the field's greatest width was a share of nothing.
- A `ui::MessageBox` too narrow for its checkbox (or its plain answer) and its other answers on one line puts the answers on a line under it. They ran out of the box.
- What is left of a `ui::Toast`'s time is a share of its rail and not the recipe's width in pixels: in a toast narrower than its recipe (a narrow window) it ran out from past the toast's end, and stood still for the first of its time.
- A `ui::Splitter` gives its panes back what a narrower room took from them when the room is wide again: a pane cut down to its least size, or a share that grew as the others were cut, was kept so in the sizes it writes, and a window dragged narrow and wide again had other panes than one opened wide. While the sizes are the ones its own fit wrote, it fits from what they were before; sizes a person or the app sets are taken as they are. A `ui::DockLayout`'s splits are splitters, and follow the same.
- A `ui::Table`'s and a `ui::DataGrid`'s text cell ends its text in an ellipsis where the text is wider than its column, a number too and the first column of a tree: it was drawn at its own width and cut by the cell with nothing to say so ("table.r"). A column that wraps (`TableColumn::wrap(true)`) wraps as before, and what reads the tree has the whole text. The lines drawn while a table loads are a share of their cell, the board's lengths as shares of the columns it draws them in (0.85, all of it, 0.68 and 0.965 of the first column by turns, 0.78 of the others), where they were 150, 190, 120, 170 and 70 whatever the column: in a narrower column a line ran to the cell's edge and was cut, and in a wider one it is as much longer.
- A `ui::Field` with its label beside the control (`Orientation::Horizontal`) has the label over the control in a room narrower than the label's column, the gap and what the control needs (360 with a control of its own width, by the recipe), and beside it again where there is room: it could not be under that, and ran out of a narrower panel. It is the field's own room that says so, not the window's: the label and the body are a row that wraps, and over the body the label is as high as its words, which is settled before the frame is painted. A control that fills has no less beside the label than the recipe leaves it.
- A `ui::FindBar` gives way in a narrow view. A mounted bar whose field has no room for its options in it (under 64 of text before them) puts them behind one button at the field's end, which shows that one is on and opens a menu of them, each checked while it is on (`options_label` names it, "Search options"); with room again they are back in the field. Under the least the field keeps (40 of text before the one button), the count and the buttons go on to a line under the field, and the replace row's buttons under its field: nothing runs out of the bar. Under about 250 the options lay over what was typed. A bar that is only built keeps its options in its field and room for them, and wraps the same.
- `ui::Steps` in a row keep their marks whole in any room, and `Steps::mount(ctx)` gives them a form for the room they have. A step gave way down to nothing, and its mark of 24 was squeezed with it (to about 7 in a room of 256): a step is never narrower than its mark with the step's padding, so the titles give way first, to an ellipsis and then to nothing, the last step's first, and the lines between the marks keep their 24. Mounted, the steps read their room before the frame is painted: where each title has less than 40 beside its mark, each title is under its mark (the state `Stacked`), and where the marks and their lines have no room in a row, the steps run down the page, as `Orientation::Vertical` has them, with its arrow keys. Steps the app runs down the page are so in any room.
- A `ui::Pagination` gives way in a narrow room. `Pagination::mount(ctx)` reads the room its buttons have before the frame is painted: narrower than them, it draws fewer pages on each side of the current one, down to the first, the current and the last between two ellipses, and narrower than those it says where the list is between previous and next, as the compact form does ("Page 6 of 12"); with room again it draws what the app says. Nine buttons were 356 wide in any room, and ran out of a narrower one. A pagination with a select of how many to a page puts what has no room beside the select on a line under it, and a `ui::Table`'s footer puts the pagination under its count where the two have no room on one line. A pagination that is only built draws what the app says in any room.
`ui::Tabs`: tabs put in the tree with `mount` take their overflowing form themselves while their list along the top is wider than its room, scrolled to the selected tab, with no frame of the other form, and are the list alone again when it fits; `overflow(true)` keeps that form whether or not the list fits. Tabs converted into an element on their own end their labels in an ellipsis in a room too narrow for them, every tab still in it. `TabsView::room_ref` is the ref a theme's renderer gives the box that is the tabs' room. Mounted tabs with a key keep what is theirs by that key.
`ui::NavigationMenu`: a menu is no wider than its room. The items it has no room for go, the last first, behind a More item at its end, which opens a menu of them: a link as an item, the page shown checked, and an item with a panel as its name over its panel's links. More is an item of the row to the keys, drawn as the item of the page shown while that page is behind it, and is gone when every item is back in the row; the open panel of an item that goes closes. `NavigationMenu::more_label` says what it is called, and `NavigationMenuView::slot` and `more` are what a theme's renderer builds the row of. A menu converted into an element on its own keeps what fits and More, whose menu does not open. The links of a `Navbar` are this menu, and do the same.
`ScrollHorizontal::fit_width`: a scroll container that lays content wider than its viewport out in the viewport's width first, so that a line gives way there by its own rules (its children shrink, or drop out of it), and scrolls only to what still has no room. `ui::Toolbar`: what is kept in a bar (a control that `stays`, a select, a field or an element with no `in_menu`) never runs past it: a mounted bar narrower than what stays in it, and one without `overflow` narrower than its controls, scrolls sideways to it, by the wheel, a drag and the keys. `ui::CommandBar` and the bar of a `DataGrid` are toolbars, and do the same.
`ui::ActionBar`: a mounted bar wider than its room draws each action that has an icon as its icon alone, called by its label in a tooltip, while its text stays whole (`ActionBarView::compact`), in its first frame at a width, and has its labels back with room. Narrower than its icons too, its actions go, the last first, into the menu of a More button (`more_label`, "More actions") before the clear button; what it says and the clear button stay, and the text is cut only once every action is in the menu: nothing of the bar is past its room.

## 0.61.1 — 2026-11-13

Behaviour changes:

- A wheel or a trackpad scrolls one scroll container on each axis: the innermost under the pointer that can still move the way the event goes takes that axis of the event whole. What it cannot take, reaching its end within the event, is dropped; it was handed to the container around it, so one notch of the wheel over a small list at the top of a page moved the list to its end and the page by the rest. A container already at its end takes nothing, as before, and the event goes to the container around it. The two axes are decided separately: a strip that scrolls sideways inside a page that scrolls down still takes the sideways part of a slanted event while the page takes the vertical. A finger's scroll is unchanged. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#scroll-containers-inside-one-another).

## 0.61.0 — 2026-11-13

Added:

- lurq runs on an iPhone: the `winit` shell with the `wgpu` renderer draws the same component tree as on a desktop. The app starts in a scene, for which its `Info.plist` names `LurqSceneDelegate`, and a `CADisplayLink` paces its frames. See [iOS](docs/src/content/docs/ios.md); what is not done is listed in `docs/ios-gaps.md`.
- Touch: one finger is the pointer, with the platform's timings, and hovers nothing; `MouseEvent::pointer` (`PointerKind`) tells a finger from a mouse. Nodes and components take a finger's own events (`on_touch_start` and the rest) and gestures: `on_tap`, `on_long_press`, `on_swipe`, `on_pan`, `on_pinch`, `on_rotate`, `on_edge_pan` and `on_history_swipe`. Scroll containers and `VirtualizedList` follow a finger as the platform's lists do, with momentum, a rubber band at the ends, a phone's scroll indicator and a tap on the status bar to scroll to the top. `WindowHandle::haptic(Haptic)` plays a haptic.
- Text inputs on iOS use the system's keyboard, with suggestions, autocorrection and dictation, and the platform's caret, selection handles, loupe and copy and paste menu. `TextInput::keyboard`, `return_key` and `content` choose the keyboard. Over inputs whose `content` is `ContentKind::Username`, `Email`, `Password` or `NewPassword` the keyboard offers the person's saved logins and fills a chosen one in. `ScrollState::set_keyboard_dismissal(KeyboardDismissal)` has a list put the keyboard away as a finger scrolls it.
- `WindowHandle::on_app_event` and `AppEvent`: the app became active or inactive, went to the background or came back, was opened with a link or a shortcut, was turned, or was asked by the system for another window. `WinitWindow::with_orientations` takes `Orientation`s joined with `|`. `WindowInfo::safe_area` (`SafeAreaInsets`, the keyboard's part of the bottom in `keyboard`) turns with the device; `Ctx::safe_area()` adds the bars a component around draws over the content, and `Ctx::scroll_to_top()` scrolls the page to its top.
- `StackOutlet` in the place of an `Outlet` keeps every entry of the history as a screen of its own: a push comes in from the side, back goes out, and a finger from the edge drags the top screen back. `NavigationBar` draws the title and the way back, with a large title that scrolls away, a search field (`NavigationSearch`), a prompt, a toolbar and a blur of the content under it; `NavigationItem`, `StackMotion` and `StackOutletProps::bar_with` change or replace it. It works on a desktop too. See [routing](docs/src/content/docs/routing.md).
- `Sheet` and `SheetHost`: content that comes up from the bottom of the window over everything, stands at its detents (`SheetDetent`) and is dragged between them and away. A list inside gives the finger to the sheet at its top, and the sheet rises with the keyboard. `Sheet::mount_with` makes the content itself, for a stack of screens in a sheet. See [modals](docs/src/content/docs/modals.md).
- `Tabs`, `TabOutlet`, `TabBar` and `TabItem`: a set of tabs, each a router with a history of its own, kept while not shown, with a bar across the bottom or the top that has pictures, titles and badges. `TabBar::floating()` is a rounded bar clear of the window's edges, which `minimizes_on_scroll` draws in as a list is scrolled; `TabItem::search()` stands apart at its end. `NavigationItem::hides_tab_bar` takes the bar away with a screen. See [routing](docs/src/content/docs/routing.md).
- `SystemSettings` in `ctx.window().system`: the appearance, the text scale, bold text, reduce motion, reduce transparency, increase contrast, button shapes. A phone tells all of it, a desktop shell the appearance. The theme follows three of them by itself (`Theme::apply_system`, off with `Theme::set_follows_system(false)`): a palette for each appearance once `Theme::set_palettes(light, dark)` gave two, the typography at the person's text size, and bold text. See [theme](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md).
- `PullToRefresh`, `ActivityIndicator` and `SwipeRow`: a list pulled down to refresh, the spinner it shows, and a row with actions at either side that a swipe uncovers.
- `ScrollState::set_paging(true)` scrolls a list a page at a time under a finger, and `ScrollState::page()` says which page it is on. `ZoomScroll` holds content that two fingers pinch larger and smaller and a double tap zooms.
- `lurq::app::biometry`: `available()` and `authenticate(reason, policy, done)` ask for Face ID or Touch ID, and `keep_secret`, `read_secret`, `has_secret` and `forget_secret` keep a secret in the device's keychain, where the system checks the person before it gives the secret back (`AuthPolicy::BiometryOnly` for the faces or fingers known then). Off a phone the answer is `Unavailable` at once.
- On iOS a window opened with `ctx.window_opener()` is put into a scene of its own, for a tablet or a display that is plugged in. It was tried in a simulated iPad only; see `docs/ios-ipad-checks.md`.
- `Easing::Spring`.
- `crates/mobile_demo`: an app with a screen for each of the above, and its Xcode project.
- `.presence()` on a container: a child with a key or an id and an `.exit_animation(...)` of its own that a render removes stays where it was, in flow and taking no input, until its exit animation ends. It works in the page, in a component that renders by itself and in a popup or a dialog. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#children-of-a-container).
- `Grid`: a container that lays its children out in the cells of columns and rows, as a subset of CSS grid. Tracks are `GridTrack::Px`, `Fr` (a share of the room left) or `Auto` (the size of their content); `.columns`, `.rows`, `.gap`, `.column_gap`, `.row_gap`, `.align_items` and `.justify_items` on the grid; `.column_span`, `.row_span`, `.grid_column` and `.grid_row` on its children. Children fill the cells in their order, row after row, and rows are added as they are needed. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#grid).
- Sticky positioning: `.sticky(Sticky::top(0.0))` on nodes and components holds an element at an edge of the scroll container it is in while that scrolls, for as long as its parent's box has room for it, as CSS `position: sticky`. `Sticky` has `top`, `bottom`, `left` and `right` and `with_*` for more than one edge. The element keeps its room in flow, is painted over its siblings and hit before them, and scrolling places it without a layout. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#sticky-elements).
- Dashed borders: `Border::dashed()` and `Border::dash(dash, gap)`, with `BorderDash`. The dashes run along the element's outline, around its corners, and the period is fitted so the pattern closes. Both native backends and the software screenshot renderer draw them. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#dashed-borders).
- `layout::stroke`: the formulas a rect's stroke is painted with (`StrokeShape`, `stroke_coverage`, `outline_position`, `dash_coverage`), which both quad shaders mirror, and `layout::render_list::StrokeStyle`.
- `PaletteColor::Foreground` and `ThemePalette::foreground`: the color of text that names none. A `Text`, a `TextInput` and a Markdown document whose style keeps the default text color are drawn in it, unless an element above sets a `.foreground(...)`. It is black by default, the color text had, so nothing changes until a theme sets it. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#the-themes-foreground).
- Presence: `.exit_animation(animation)` on nodes and components names the keyframe animation an element leaves with. When an `Overlay`, a `Popup` or a `Modal` closes, what it showed stays on screen until the longest exit animation in it ends; each element plays its own, so a dialog's backdrop and panel can leave differently. The leaving layer is only painted: it takes no pointer, focus or Tab, is no modal, and nothing in it is found by id or by element ref. The animation an element plays from its mount is how it enters, as before. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#presence-entering-and-leaving-layers).
- `examples/engine_gallery.rs` shows borders that follow their corners and dashed ones, a grid, sticky headers, a list whose rows leave with an animation and the theme's foreground on one page; `--capture <file.png>` saves a frame of it.
- `examples/presence.rs` shows two menus and a dialog entering and leaving; `--capture <dir>` runs them by a script and saves a frame at each step.
- Reduced motion: lurq reads the system's accessibility setting on Windows, macOS and iOS, when a window opens and when one takes focus. `Theme::reduced_motion()` and `Ctx::reduced_motion()` report what is in effect, and `Theme::set_reduced_motion(Some(bool))` decides for the app. While motion is reduced a transition of an element's transform, offset, width or height ends at once; fades and color changes still run, and keyframe animations are left to the app. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#reduced-motion).
- `Transition::foreground()`, `Transition::outline()` and `Transition::box_shadow()`, with `AnimatableProperty::Foreground`, `Outline` and `BoxShadow`, and `Transition::all()` includes them: the color of the text under an element, its outline and its box shadows animate, in transitions and in keyframe animations (`AnimatableValue::Outline`, `AnimatableValue::Shadows` with `ShadowList`). An outline grows in from nothing and fades back to it, so a focus ring can come and go smoothly. They are paint only: animating them lays nothing out. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md).
- `App::load_hinted_font(data)` loads a font whose glyphs are drawn grid-fitted: at the nearest whole pixel size and position, with the font's TrueType hints applied along both axes. It is for an icon font hinted for it; text stays unhinted, so a letter looks the same wherever on the line it falls. The font has to ask for native ClearType in its `prep` program for its hints along x to count. A glyph under a transform, or in a text that is not snapped to pixels, is drawn unhinted like any other. See [resources](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#hinted-fonts).
- `examples/icon_hinting.rs` draws lurq's icon font twice at each size, loaded as any font is and loaded hinted, to compare; `--capture <file.png>` saves the window's frame.
- `Text::max_lines(n)`: a wrapped text takes at most `n` lines and ends in an ellipsis on the last one when it had more, as CSS `line-clamp`. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#line-limit).
- A plain `Text` draws the underline of its style, which only Markdown drew before, and `Text::underline(...)` sets one on a text. `TextStrikethrough`, `TextStyle::strikethrough`, `MarkdownTextStyle::strikethrough` and `Text::strikethrough(...)` draw a line through text. Both follow the text line by line, wrapped lines included, in the text's color and at the font's metrics unless set. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#underline-and-strikethrough).
- `.disabled(bool)` on nodes and components, and `ElementHandle::set_disabled`: the element and everything in it take no input. No press, click, tap, long press or drop reaches them, none of them is hovered, pressed or focused, Tab passes them by, focus requests and `ElementHandle::click()` on them do nothing, and a focused element that becomes disabled loses focus. A disabled element still covers what is behind it and a press on it reaches nothing, the elements it is in included; a pointer moving over it still reaches those. Scrolling works as if nothing were disabled: the containers around a disabled element and the scroll containers inside it scroll by wheel, finger and scrollbar. `.when_disabled(|style| ...)` / `.disabled_style(style)` is a paint-only state style applied after every other, on the element and on the elements in it, and `InteractionState::is_disabled()` reports the state, for every element of a tree, in view or not. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#disabled).
- `Outline` and `.outline(outline)` on nodes, components and state styles, and `ElementHandle::set_outline`: a line around an element, outside its box, with an offset, as CSS `outline` and `outline-offset`. It takes no room in layout, follows the element's corner radii and is painted over the element's content and over the elements painted after it, so the ring of a button is whole where it lies over the next one; it stays under the border and bars of the scroll container it is in, under what sticks over it, inside a faded or blurred ancestor's layer, and under a menu or a dialog. The bounds a container clips its content to let the ring of an element at its edge past by the ring's own reach; a scroll viewport and the window cut it where they cut the element. In `focus_visible` it is a focus ring that moves nothing. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#outline).
- State styles (`hovered`, `active`, `focused`, `focus_visible`, `drag_over`) can set `.foreground(color)` and `.opacity(value)`: the color of the text under the element, and the element's opacity, while the state holds. Both are paint only, and `Transition::opacity()` animates a state style's opacity.
- `.foreground(color)` on nodes and components, and `ElementHandle::set_foreground`: the color of the text in an element and under it, as CSS `color`. A `Text` that names no color of its own is drawn in the foreground of its nearest ancestor that has one; `Text::color` and the color of a `Text::styled` style still win, and a text with no foreground above it keeps its typography style's color, so nothing changes for a tree that sets none. The color is a concrete color or a `PaletteColor`. It is paint only. A `TextInput`, Markdown and the content of an overlay do not read it. See [styling](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#foreground).
- `.blur(radius)` on nodes and components: the element and its subtree are painted into an offscreen layer that is drawn through a Gaussian blur, as CSS `filter: blur()` does. The radius is in logical pixels with the deviation at half of it, as for box and text shadows and the canvas's `Filter::Blur`; it is clamped to `layout::opacity_layer::MAX_BLUR_RADIUS` (256), and `0` is no blur and no layer. The radius is in the element's own units and scales with the transforms it is under. The blur spreads past the element's box, is cut by the clips the element is under (rounded corners included) and by the window, and inside them takes in the element's content just past the clip, so a blurred element does not fade out at a scroll container's or the window's edge. `.blur` and `.opacity` on one element share a layer. Both native backends draw it (two passes, on a reduced copy of the layer for a wide blur, so the cost does not grow with the radius), and so does the devtools node screenshot.
- `.backdrop_blur(radius)` on nodes and components: what is painted behind the element is blurred inside its box, as CSS `backdrop-filter: blur()` does, and the element paints itself over the result: frosted glass, for an element with a translucent background. The radius means what `.blur`'s means. The blur fills the box with its corner radii, turned and scaled as the element's transform turns and scales it, inside the clips the element is under (rounded corners included); "behind" is everything painted before the element in the window or, inside a faded or blurred ancestor, in that ancestor's layer; at the window's edge the edge pixels repeat, so glass against an edge does not darken; the element's own opacity fades the blur with it. Both native backends draw it (they stop the target's pass, copy the pixels around the box, blur the copy with the passes of a layer blur and put it back), and so does the devtools node screenshot.
- `AnimatableProperty::Blur` and `AnimatableProperty::BackdropBlur`, `Transition::blur()` and `Transition::backdrop_blur()`: the radii animate in transitions and keyframe animations, and `Transition::all()` includes them. Like opacity, a blur that is animating keeps the node's layout cache.
- `layout::opacity_layer::RoundedBox`: a rounded box in the window, possibly turned or scaled, with its bounds and its coverage of a pixel. A blurred layer carries the rounded clip it is cut to as one (`LayerBlur::clip`).
- `examples/layer_blur_capture_check.rs` renders a scene of blurred nodes and frosted glass through layout and both native backends and compares the captures.
- `RenderEngine::layer_profile()` and `app::profiler::LayerProfile`, with `perf_profile`: what the opacity layers and blurs of the last frame cost the wgpu or the DX12 renderer, as counts: render passes, copies of a target, draws of the blur shader, backdrop blurs run and backdrop blurs put back from a kept result, and the layer pool's textures with their bytes and the part of them that holds kept results. A custom `RenderEngine` reports none unless it implements the method.
- `examples/backdrop_blur_probe.rs` measures the frames of a window of frosted glass (a gradient, three glass panels of lists over most of it, a small glass menu over a list) on both native backends, at several blur radii and scales: a frame where nothing behind any glass changed, one where something behind the small glass changed, one where everything did, and a resize. It reports the CPU phases, wgpu's GPU time, draw calls and the counts above, can save its frames as PNG, and compares two sets of them.
- `examples/hover_frames_probe.rs` (`--features winit,wgpu,perf_profile`) sweeps the pointer over a scrolling list (rows without a hover style, rows with one, or rows without one in a `VirtualizedList`) and prints the frames a profiling session saw presented meanwhile, to measure what a hover costs.
- `App::warm_text_styles(styles)` prepares the fonts the given styles are drawn with. The first text of each family, weight and slant makes cosmic-text score every font face on the system, 4 to 11 ms a combination on a Windows desktop, on the frame that first shows it; this does it when asked instead. It blocks that long, and `App` is `Send`: call it on a clone of the app on a thread of your own, after loading fonts, while the window is created. With the theme's bold text set, it prepares the heavier weights that setting draws. On the `text_chat` screen the first frame is 2.5 ms with a regular and a bold style prepared and 17.2 ms without (19.7 ms before this round's other changes). Nothing calls it by default.
- `benches/text_chat.rs`: a chat screen of short texts (about 40 messages in a `VirtualizedList`, a channel list, a member list and an input; 206 texts a frame) drawn one scripted frame at a time: cold first and second frame, idle, hover, a wheel step through 10,000 messages, a channel switch, a 2 px resize, a typed character and a change of scale factor. Built with `perf_profile,counting_allocator` and run with `LURQ_CHAT_METRICS=<csv>` it writes a row per frame instead, with the phases, the cache counters, the allocations and the sizes of the text caches. `examples/text_chat_gpu_probe.rs` runs the same frames through the wgpu and DX12 renderers and saves captures whose hashes `scripts/compare-text-chat-captures.py` compares between two builds; `scripts/summarize-text-chat.py` gives medians over runs with their spread. See [Chat Screen Text Optimization](docs/src/content/docs/text-chat-optimization.md).
- `SelectOption::leading(element)` and `SelectOption::trailing(element)`: an element before or after an option's label, such as a brand mark, a status dot or a badge (lurq#104). It keeps its own size, sits `SelectStyle::option_adornment_gap` (default 8) from the label and is centred on the label's line, also beside a detail line. The closed field of a single-select shows the selected option's elements, and a custom `Select::trigger` gets them as `SelectTriggerState::leading` and `trailing`. The elements are decorative: type-ahead and automation keep using the label.
- `SelectOption::leading_with(|state| ...)` and `trailing_with`: the element is built for the state it is drawn in. The builder receives a `SelectAdornmentState` (`selected`, `highlighted`, `disabled`, `in_field` and the row's or field's `text_color`), so a mark can take the label's colour on a selected or highlighted row.

Performance (the chat screen of `text_chat`, 1280 × 2160, release build, Windows, Ryzen 9 7950X and RTX 5090; median of the runs taken at the end, before → after the round; every scenario, phase and the method are in [Chat Screen Text Optimization](docs/src/content/docs/text-chat-optimization.md)):

- Texts painted one after another share a draw in both native renderers when they are under the same clip or, in the window, under clips that cut nothing off them: 210 → 9 draw calls for the screen's 206 texts, and 245 → 117 µs of DX12 encoding a frame.
- A frame that rasterises a new glyph no longer copies the glyph atlas (twice 4 MiB at 1024 × 1024): the second frame of a new window 2.9 → 1.6 ms.
- A plain text's size, extents, carets and glyphs are one cache entry found with one fast hash of the text, in place of five caches hashed with SipHash, one of them after copying the text. A frame that changes no text: 198 → 159 µs, with 620 → 414 allocations. The cache drops what no pass used for 16 passes and never empties at once: before, every 43rd frame or so of a fast scroll laid out all visible text again (99th percentile of a scroll frame 3.4 → 1.6 ms).
- A plain text under 1,024 bytes is shaped once and laid out again for a new width, size, wrapping or scale factor: a frame of a window being resized 1.67 → 0.49 ms, the first frame at a new scale factor 4.7 → 1.7 ms.
- A word shaped in one text is not shaped again in the next (cosmic-text's `shape-run-cache`): a channel not seen before 3.4 → 2.0 ms, a wheel step through 10,000 messages 1.46 → 1.18 ms.
- A text painted before costs one cache lookup and one write per glyph a frame, and the atlas finds a glyph with the fast hash.

Behaviour changes:

- `MouseEvent` has a new public field, `pointer`, and `WindowInfo` two, `safe_area` and `system`: code that builds one with every field named must add them.
- A theme given two palettes with `Theme::set_palettes` changes with the system's appearance, and its typography and bold text follow the person's settings on a phone. `Theme::set_follows_system(false)` leaves the theme as the app set it.
- `LayoutKind` has a new variant, `Grid`: code that matches it without a wildcard arm must add it.
