Code that found a link by its own text node, measured its box, or expected its words to wrap separately from the paragraph must change. Images other than a lone one still break a paragraph into a row of texts and images.
- Every Markdown link is a Tab stop in document order, and keyboard focus draws `ThemeMarkdown::link_focus_ring` (2 px `#2563eb` by default) around it. A host's Tab order now runs through the links of its Markdown documents: Shift+Tab from a field below a chat reply reaches the reply's last link first. Set `link_focus_ring: None` for no ring; links stay focusable.
- MCP tools report a Markdown link with the role `link` (it was `text`), named by its words, with the tag `Link`. A paragraph's text (`ElementRef::text_content`, MCP and DevTools text, copied text) is its content only: without the isolate marks and without what a link presentation adds after the words.
- In a Markdown paragraph with a link, explicit directional controls (U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069) and directional marks (U+200E LEFT-TO-RIGHT MARK, U+200F RIGHT-TO-LEFT MARK, U+061C ARABIC LETTER MARK) in the source are dropped, before and after the link too; a paragraph without links keeps them. Such a paragraph takes its direction from its first letter. Its line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029, U+0085, U+001C–U+001E) are hard breaks, and in a link's words a hard break or a separator is a space (`MarkdownLink::text` too).
- A Markdown link stays on one line where it fits, its words with its trailing runs: the line breaks before the link instead of inside it. A link wider than its line still breaks, anywhere in it, no longer at the spaces of its words. (A link with right-to-left words in a left-to-right paragraph, or any link in a right-to-left paragraph, may still break between its words and its runs where it would fit: the text layout breaks where the bidi direction changes.)
- A Markdown link whose words draw nothing (no words, or only spaces, controls and invisible characters such as U+200B ZERO WIDTH SPACE, U+2060, U+FEFF or U+00AD, or only combining marks (General_Category Mn or Me) with no letter to sit on) is drawn as its words, as text: the presentation hook is not called for it, it shows no trailing runs or tooltip, and it is not a link node or a Tab stop. Its `MarkdownLink::index` is still taken, so the links after it keep theirs.
- The containers Markdown builds itself (the document, lists, quotes, footnotes, rows of texts and images) no longer clip what their blocks draw past them, so a link's focus ring shows in full. Code blocks and tables still clip to their boxes; the app's own containers clip as before.
- `TextStyle`, `MarkdownTextStyle` and `ThemeMarkdown` gain public fields: struct literals that list every field need the new ones (or `..Default::default()`).
- An email autolink (`<team@example.com>`) is parsed as a link to `mailto:team@example.com` (it was `team@example.com`), as HTML renders it.
- `MarkdownBlockStyle::padding` is an `Option<Padding>` (`lurq::node::padding::Padding`; it was `Option<f32>`), so a box can have other padding at its sides than at its top and bottom: `Some(Padding::symmetric(8.0, 4.0))`. Where it was `Some(x)`, write `Some(Padding::all(x))`, or `Some(x.into())`: `Padding` converts from an `f32`.

Fixes:

- A Markdown table cell draws its words inside `ThemeMarkdown::table_cell_box`'s and `table_header_box`'s padding. A cell of plain text or with a link was its words, so they were drawn from the cell's edges. The cell's content now sits inside its padding: in its column's alignment (left, center or right) and centered in the row's height, as before.
- An image alone in a Markdown table cell keeps its own size and is placed by its column's alignment, inside the cell's padding. It used to be the cell itself, as wide as its column.
- Copying a selection joins texts drawn side by side into one line, in x order, when their boxes share some height: the cells of a table row copy as one line however tall each cell is. Texts stacked beside a taller one, and blocks stacked in a column, copy one line each, as before.

Added:

- Text underlines: `TextStyle::underline: Option<TextUnderline>` and `MarkdownTextStyle::underline`. `TextUnderline` has a `color` (default: the text's), a `thickness` and an `offset` below the baseline (default: the font's underline metrics). Rich text draws it under every laid-out line its span covers, wrapped lines included; it changes neither measurement nor wrapping. A plain `Text` does not draw it yet.
- `MarkdownProps::link_presentation(f)`, a hook that decides how each link is shown. It gets the link (`MarkdownLink`: destination, title, its words as drawn and, new, its `index()` among the document's links) and returns a `MarkdownLinkPresentation`:
  - `link()` shows a link, in the theme's link looks unless `style(...)` (the words' style over the text around them), `hovered(...)`, `focused(...)` (`MarkdownLinkState`: words color, underline color, a fill behind the words, trailing runs' color), `focus_ring(...)` (`MarkdownFocusRing`: width, offset, color, radius) or `radius(...)` (the fills' corners) say otherwise;
  - `text()` shows the words as the text around them: nothing to press or focus;
  - `trailing(...)` adds `MarkdownRun`s after the words in the same paragraph (a host and an icon glyph after a web link, an address after a link the app does not follow). After a link, the pointer takes them as part of it. They are presentation, like CSS `::after`: copying a selection and inspection tools leave them out, and a selection highlight skips them;
  - `tooltip(...)` shows a tooltip under the link after the pointer rests on it for `ThemeMarkdown::link_tooltip_delay` (500 ms by default), and at once while it has keyboard focus. A tooltip stays with its link (its destination and how many links to it come before) when links are added before it. Its words sit inside `ThemeMarkdown::link_tooltip_box`'s padding, centered, and wrap at the window's width.
  The hook runs once per link and render on the exact `MarkdownLink` that `on_link_click` gets, so what a link shows always comes from the destination it opens.
- A link's words and trailing runs are drawn between invisible bidi marks for the direction of the paragraph they are in, and a paragraph with a link drops every explicit directional control (U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069) and directional mark (U+200E, U+200F, U+061C) from its text, before the link too, so lurq's own marks, three isolates deep at most, are the only ones left. In a left-to-right paragraph a link is `LRM LRI words runs PDI LRM`; in a right-to-left one it is `RLM RLI LRI runs PDI FSI words PDI PDI RLM`, a right-to-left isolate that draws the runs, which come first, right of the words. Bidi reorders each laid-out line on its own, and either way a link that wraps across lines leaves on every line its runs, or nothing, right after its words: never the text before the link or another link's host (tested at every width from 60 to 1600 px). A right-to-left override in the words or anywhere in the paragraph, however deeply nested (Unicode bidi ignores an isolate past its 125 levels, which 63 overrides reach), cannot draw a host or an address after a link backwards. A link's trailing runs follow its words, left to right, in a right-to-left paragraph too (one that starts with a Hebrew or Arabic letter), while the paragraph still reads right to left around the link; a mark can no longer turn a paragraph right to left to draw every host before its words, or put a forged host from an empty link right after another link's words. The marks are invisible and are not copied, and the underlines of a link's words are one line per laid-out line. `lurq::markdown::isolate_bidi`, `strip_bidi_controls` and `is_bidi_control` do the same for text an app builds from a destination (a tooltip), marks included.
- Links show a pointer cursor and paint their hovered and focused looks (`ThemeMarkdown::link_hovered`, `link_focused`), per laid-out line of their words. The focus ring is drawn outside the words on all sides, over the text, and is not clipped by the paragraph. This geometry is laid out at the paint's device-pixel scale, so it stays on the glyphs at fractional scales (1.25, 1.5).
- `MarkdownProps::on_link_click` now runs for Enter on a focused link too (Space does not follow a link). It runs inside the input's dispatch, so `lurq::app::events::is_synthetic_input()` tells a person's press from synthetic input (an MCP client's), and `MarkdownLink::index()` says which link it was.
- `MarkdownProps::list_view(f)`: the app may draw a list block itself. Called with a `MarkdownList` (`ordered`, `start`, `items`, `style`, `theme`), it returns the element to draw or `None` for the usual list; `MarkdownList::inlines` and `blocks` draw content as the document does, links included. Links a declining view drew to decide are forgotten, so they do not shift the numbering.
- `ThemeMarkdown::link_hovered`, `link_focused`, `link_focus_ring`, `link_radius`, `link_tooltip`, `link_tooltip_box` and `link_tooltip_delay`, and the theme types `MarkdownLinkState` and `MarkdownFocusRing`.
- A Markdown document still parses once per change of its source; laying out many links and reference definitions is linear (tested with 3000 definitions and 3000 linked blocks).

## 0.45.0 — 2026-10-15

Behaviour changes:

- A multiline `TextInput` whose text overflows its rows takes the wheel: it scrolls its text instead of the scroll container around it, until its text reaches the end the wheel moves to.
- `ArrowUp` and `ArrowDown` in a multiline `TextInput` move by laid-out row, not by paragraph.
- A future polled by lurq (one not running on a Tokio runtime) must wake its waker when it can make progress, as the `Future` contract requires. A future that returned `Pending` without arranging a wake used to be polled again anyway while the event loop polled; it now waits for the next wake of the loop.
- The pointer over a scrollbar shows the default cursor, whatever cursor the scroll container or text input sets.

Fixes:

- Fix an idle window keeping a CPU core busy. The winit shell polled its event loop without pause (`ControlFlow::Poll`) whenever a timer (`Ctx::create_interval`, `Ctx::create_timeout`) was running or a future, stream or future action was in flight, because the loop had no other way to learn that a timer was due or a future could make progress: futures were polled with a waker that did nothing. An app almost always has one (a clock that ticks each minute, a 2 s poll, a stream subscribed to a channel), so an app with nothing to draw used about one core, nearly all of it on the UI thread. The loop now sleeps until something is due or wakes it:
  - It waits until the earliest running timer fires or the earliest scheduled redraw (`ControlFlow::WaitUntil`), and with neither, until an event (`ControlFlow::Wait`). A timer fires at its time, as precisely as the OS timer allows (a median 0.4 ms late in the `idle_wait` example on Windows); an animation, a blinking caret, the perf overlay and continuous images still get their frames from scheduled redraws, and continuous video still polls.
  - Futures, streams and future actions wake the loop: a future polled on the loop gets a waker that wakes it, a result or stream item handed over from a Tokio runtime wakes it, and starting a future that the loop polls (`FutureAction::run` from another thread, for example) wakes it. A turn applies one item of a stream as before and wakes the loop again for the next one, so items emitted together each get a turn.
  - Starting or restarting a timer wakes the loop, so a timer started from another thread is not missed while the loop sleeps.
  - A `Signal` written off the loop's thread wakes the loop when the write marks a component for render: a `.set` inside a Tokio `ctx.future` (a progress signal, say), from a `std::thread`, or a `StreamEmitter::emit` called from a thread shows on the loop's next turn, as it did while the loop polled. Writes wake the loop once until it renders them, so a thread writing thousands of times between two turns posts one wake, not one per write. A write on the loop's own thread needs no wake.
  - While a redraw is pending, the loop still wakes for its timers, so a minimized or occluded window that does not get the redraw keeps firing them, down to a 1 ms interval; a scheduled frame already due, which the pending redraw serves, does not hide a later timer.
  - The loop still polls while `WinitWindow::on_tick` is set (it runs every turn), while a click waits to tell a single click from a double click, and while a query is ready to start or finish.
- Fix a multiline `TextInput` that has grown to its `rows(min, max)` limit being scrollable only by moving the caret. The input already scrolled its text to keep the caret's line in view, but it ignored the wheel and showed no scrollbar, so once the first lines were scrolled out the person could not see them without moving the caret back. An overflowing multiline input now scrolls with the wheel and a trackpad, clamped to its text, and shows a vertical scrollbar at the right of its content box while its text overflows (by half a pixel or more, so rows of a fractional line height that add up to a hair over their box still fit), styled and driven like a scroll container's: the theme's `ScrollBarStyle` or the input's `.scrollbar(...)` and `.scrollbar_hovered(...)`, hover state, thumb drag and paging on a track press. A wheel or scrollbar scroll stays put through later layouts; the next edit or caret move (or taking the focus) brings the caret's line back into view, as before.
  - The bar honours `ScrollBarStyle::placement`. Placed `Overlay` (the default) it runs over the text along the right edge of the content box. Placed `Reserved` it gets a lane of its own there, as a reserved scroll container's gutter: the bar's width plus its edge inset on both sides, sized for the wider of the resting and hovered styles. The text, caret, selections and pointer hits keep out of the lane, so no glyph sits under the bar, and the lane stays while the text fits, so the text does not re-wrap when it starts or stops overflowing or when the bar is hovered.
- Fix `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` (and with `Shift`) in a multiline `TextInput` moving by paragraph: in a soft-wrapped paragraph, `ArrowUp` jumped to the start of the value and `ArrowDown` to its end, so `Shift+ArrowUp` and `Backspace` deleted the whole paragraph. The caret now moves one laid-out row, as near its x as that row allows; above the first row it goes to the start and below the last to the end, as before. A run of `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` keeps the column it started from, so a short row on the way does not pull the caret to its end for the rows after it; any other caret move or edit ends the run. Decorated inputs (`InputTokens`) already moved by row and are unchanged.
- Fix the caret at the end of a multiline value that ends with a line break when the paragraph before it soft-wraps: it was drawn on the paragraph's second row instead of on the empty row after the paragraph, one row per line break from the top, and `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` started from that row. The empty row now follows the laid-out rows, for every line break text layout reads: `\n`, `\r\n` (a paste from Windows), `\n\r` and `\r`. A value ending with `\n\r` or `\r` had its caret drawn on the paragraph's last row. The input also grows by that empty row: its height counted one row per line break, not the rows a paragraph wrapped into, so after a wrapped paragraph and a final break the caret's row lay outside the input and the caret was not drawn.
- Add the `idle_wait` example (`winit`, `wgpu`, `tokio`): an idle window with a minute interval, a 2 s interval and a stream, which prints when its timers fire and how long a stream item takes to reach a render. On Windows it used about 89% of a core before this release and about 0.4% after.
- No public API change.

## 0.44.1 — 2026-10-14

- Fix `VirtualizedList` undoing a scroll the app asked for when the row keys changed in the same update. A change of the key order (a row added, removed or moved) anchors the first visible row so that the view stays put, and the list restored that anchor with `set_scroll` once every row height was known, after the layout that had resolved a queued `ScrollState::scroll_to_bottom_pending`, `scroll_to_top_pending` or `set_scroll_pending`: the view went back to the old position, on the next frame when every row was measured, or frames later when the list first measured its rows (which also undid a `reveal_key` made meanwhile). A requested vertical scroll now wins: no anchor is captured while one is pending, and an anchor still waiting for heights is dropped once a scroll request takes effect after it (a resolved pending scroll, `ScrollState::set_scroll`, a `reveal_key` or a scroll into view). A content change with no scroll request stays anchored as before.
  - The list now builds its row window where the following layout puts the offset (a pending scroll's target, or the end of a list held at its end while row estimates refine) instead of where it was, so the frame of a jump draws rows instead of an empty viewport, and the `lurq::vlist` warnings "laid-out rows leave viewport hole" and "virtualized window healed after missed scroll events" no longer follow it. No public API change.

## 0.44.0 — 2026-10-12

- Add retained `TextInputController` raw-byte selection, replacement and focus APIs. Replacement uses the input's existing callback, prevent-default and undo history; masked, composing and detached inputs return typed refusals. Keep one controller per keyed input.
- Add themed `InputTokens` decorations over an exact editable String. Paint, caret, hit and selection share whole-paragraph glyph geometry, including directional text, wrapping and padding. Tokens are source ranges, not atomic replacement labels; reparse after source edits.
- Preserve canonical multiline CRLF/LFCR paragraph endings and both editable raw bytes. Single-line Scroll retains its ordinary one-space-per-CR/LF presentation. Keyed overflow and row-limit changes use current declarative props while retaining editing history.
- Breaking: public exhaustive `QuadContent` gains `DecoratedInput`. External exhaustive matches must handle the new payload; a silent omitted drawing branch does not render the decorated input. Its opaque layout is consumed by the supported render pipeline.

## 0.43.0 — 2026-10-10

Behaviour changes:

- `ctx.future`, `ctx.stream` and `ctx.future_action` called in `create` now run and live until the component unmounts. Before, the first render replaced or dropped them, so such an action never ran and such a future or stream was cancelled at once.
- `Ctx::focus` and `ElementHandle::focus` (and `TextInputHandle::focus`) scroll the focused element into view, as Tab already did; scrolling focus into view moves a container only along the axes it scrolls.
- The focus styles of the checkbox box, slider thumb and select trigger, and the form buttons', form checkbox's and form slider's focus borders, show only for keyboard focus (focus-visible). Text inputs keep their focus border for any focus.
- A queued MCP call whose caller stopped waiting is dropped instead of running late.

- Fix a `ctx.future_action` created in `create` never running. Future slots were positional and reset by every render: the first `ctx.future`, `ctx.stream` or `ctx.future_action` call of the first render took the slot of an action created in `create` (and a render that made no such call dropped it), which cancelled it for good, so `run` silently did nothing. `ctx.future`, `ctx.stream` and `ctx.future_action` called outside render (in `create`) now get slots of their own, which no render replaces or drops: an action lives until its component unmounts and can be kept in the component struct; a future or stream starts once with the `deps` given there. Calls made in `render` stay positional, as before.
  - `FutureAction::run` on an action that a render dropped while its component stayed mounted (the render no longer made the call at its position, or made a different one) still starts nothing, but now logs a `tracing` error naming the component. Running an action of an unmounted component stays silent.
- Add `FutureState::outcome()` and `FutureState::fulfilled_data()`, which report how the latest run ended. A rejected run keeps the previous run's `data` (`FutureState::rejected(error, previous_data)`), so code that read "has `data` and is not pending" as success took a failure after an earlier success for a success. `outcome()` is `Some(Ok(&data))` when the latest run was fulfilled, `Some(Err(&error))` when it was rejected and `None` while idle or pending; `fulfilled_data()` is the data of a fulfilled run only. `data` keeps its meaning, the last value that succeeded, kept while a run is pending and after it fails, so a view can still show it next to the error; the field and the docs now say so. No behaviour change.
- Add `FutureAction::run_if_idle(args) -> bool`, which starts a run only when none is in flight and returns whether it started. `FutureAction::run` cancels and replaces a run in flight (its result is never applied), so a double click on a button wired to `run` aborted the first request; `run_if_idle` leaves it to finish. `run` is unchanged, and its docs now say that it replaces a run in flight.
- Fix an MCP call running after its caller was told it timed out. A call routed to the event loop waits for its reply for 15 s (30 s for `lurq_screenshot`, the requested timeout plus 5 s for `lurq_wait`) and then answers the client with a time-out error, but the call stayed queued and ran whenever the event loop reached it: a click or key the client believed had failed landed later, possibly after the client had moved on. `Tree::drain_mcp_requests` now drops a queued call whose caller stopped waiting (its deadline passed, or its client went away) without running it, and the time-out error says so. A call the event loop has already started is not interrupted. Tool denies, scopes and `set_enabled` are checked as before, when a call runs. No public API change.
- Add `lurq::app::events::input_source()`, `is_synthetic_input()`, `InputSource { Os, Synthetic }` and `with_synthetic_input(f)`, so an app can tell synthetic input from OS input and protect a control as "person only". Synthetic input reaches the same entry points as OS input, so a handler could not tell an MCP click from a real one. While lurq delivers synthetic input, handlers and the callbacks they reach see `InputSource::Synthetic`: every MCP tool call that reaches the event loop (`Tree::drain_mcp_requests`: `lurq_interact`, `lurq_act`, `lurq_set_value` and the rest, and the release of input an MCP client held, also by `Tree::shutdown_mcp`), `synthetic_input::apply`, and a `SyntheticInput` injected on a window (`WindowHandle::inject_input`). Anything else sees `InputSource::Os`. The source is per thread and set only during the delivery. No behaviour change.
- Add `MarkdownProps::fit_content(bool)`: a `Markdown` document sized to its content instead of filling its container, like CSS `width: fit-content; max-width: 100%`, so a chat bubble hugs its message.
  - The document is as wide as its widest block (a short message as its longest line) and never wider than its container, where its text wraps. Paragraphs, headings, lists, quotes and footnotes size to their content; a long list item wraps beside its marker.
  - Every block is then stretched to the document's width, as CSS blocks are: a code block's box and a rule span the widest block. A code block does not wrap: it is as wide as its longest line up to the container's width, and a longer line overflows its box, as when filling. A table still fills the container's width, so that its columns line up across its rows. An explicit `.width(...)` still sets the document's width.
  - A `Row` gives its children unbounded width: a bubble in a row needs `.max_width(Dimension::Pct(100.0))` or a `flex_shrink` factor for its text to wrap at the row's width.
  - Without `fit_content`, a `Markdown` document and its blocks fill their container's width as before. No behaviour change.
- Fix `VirtualizedList::reveal_key` not scrolling a list mounted with the key already set. On the list's first layout the reveal checked its target against row positions built before any row was measured, all at 0. A target among the first 64 rows but below the viewport (row 40 of 200, say) therefore looked visible, and the reveal was dropped without scrolling. The positions are now rebuilt from the heights that layout just measured before the reveal is checked, so the row is scrolled to the top of the viewport within a few frames and without further input. A reveal on a list that has no items yet now waits for them instead of being dropped, so a list mounted before its data loads still opens on the row. A key missing from a non-empty list is still dropped, and an already-visible row still does not move. No public API change.
- Add `Ctx::scroll_into_view(&ElementRef)` and `ElementHandle::scroll_into_view()`, the public way to scroll an element into view: every scroll container around it, innermost first, scrolls by the smallest amount that shows it, on each axis the container scrolls, like the web's `scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest", inline: "nearest" })`. An element already in view moves nothing, and one larger than a viewport is aligned to the viewport start. A `Ctx` request is resolved once the render that attaches the ref is laid out; the last request wins and a ref absent from the tree is ignored, as for `Ctx::focus`. When a request moves a container the pass lays out again, so the frame it draws already shows the element. Inside a `VirtualizedList` this reaches elements of mounted rows; `reveal_key` remains the way to reveal a row that may not be mounted.
  - Behaviour change: `Ctx::focus` and `ElementHandle::focus` (and `TextInputHandle::focus`) now scroll the focused element into view, as Tab and Shift+Tab already did and as a browser's `element.focus()` does. Focus by a pointer click still does not scroll. A component that calls `ctx.focus` in every render reveals the element again in every render.
  - Behaviour change: scrolling focus into view (Tab included) now moves a container only along the axes it scrolls; a `ScrollVertical` whose content is wider than it is no longer scrolled sideways.
- Document row state in `VirtualizedList`: a row outside the viewport and overscan is unmounted and created again from its props, so state the row holds itself (fields from `create`, `ctx.signal`, element refs, a text-input value signal it created, focus) starts over. The rustdoc and the layout guide show keeping such state above the list, keyed by row key, in a signal or store the screen owns and provides to the rows. No public API change.
- Add focus-visible, a keyboard-only focus state like CSS `:focus-visible`, so an app can show a focus ring only for keyboard focus.
  - The tree remembers the modality of the last input that can move focus. Tab and Shift+Tab, and any key press other than a bare modifier (Shift, Control, Alt, AltGraph, Super/Meta) made without Control, Alt or Super/Meta held, make focus visible: a key pressed on a clicked button shows its ring, a shortcut such as Ctrl+C does not. A mouse button press (also one that only closes a popup) and `ElementHandle::click()` hide it. A focus request (`ctx.focus`, `ElementHandle::focus()`) inherits the last input's modality; before any input it is pointer, so focus set at startup shows no ring. A focused text input is always focus-visible, as browsers treat text fields. Synthetic input and MCP `lurq_interact` follow the same rules.
  - `.focus_visible_style(Style)` / `.focus_visible(|style| ...)` on every element: merged over the focused style while focus-visible, under the hovered and active styles.
  - `InteractionState::is_focus_visible()`, `core::ElementRef::focus_visible()` and the reactive `ElementRef::focus_visible_signal()` (also on `ElementRefMut`), and `Tree::focus_visible()` for the focused element. Losing focus clears it.
  - MCP `lurq_read_tree` lists the state as `focus-visible`, `lurq_inspect` reports `"focus_visible": true`, and DevTools shows a node's focus-visible style.
  - Behaviour change: the checkbox box (`box_focused_style`), slider thumb (`thumb_focused_style`) and select trigger (`SelectStyle::trigger_focused`, including its default `BorderFocus` border) focus styles now show only while focus-visible (Tab or a key press, not a click or drag), like a browser's native focus rings. So do the form buttons' `border_focus` and the form checkbox and slider focus borders. Text inputs, including `FormTextInput`, keep their focus border for any focus. For a style on every focus, put `.focused(...)` on the control.
  - `focused_style` / `.focused()`, `ElementRef::focused()` and `focus_signal()` are unchanged: they still apply to any focus.

## 0.42.0 — 2026-10-08

- Add hosted MCP: an app with an MCP server of its own (its own port, token, discovery and settings) can serve lurq's tools from it instead of starting lurq's server.
  - `McpConfig::hosted()` makes `Tree::enable_mcp` start no listener and write no discovery file; `McpHandle::port()` is `0`.
  - `McpHandle::service()` returns an `McpService`, lurq's rmcp `ServerHandler` for one client session (formerly the private `LurqMcpServer`). The host mounts it as the session handler of rmcp's streamable-HTTP service, or delegates `list_tools`, `call_tool` and `get_info` to it from its own handler.
  - `lurq::mcp::rmcp` re-exports rmcp, so the host builds on the same version.
  - Scopes, tool denies, `set_enabled`, event-loop execution through `Tree::drain_mcp_requests` and held input work as with lurq's own server.
- A server started without `hosted()` is unchanged.

## 0.41.3 — 2026-10-07

- Add input held across `lurq_interact` calls, so an MCP client can drive a gesture whose samples arrive one frame apart: dragging a node or a resize handle, a marquee, a slider or scrollbar thumb, a middle-button or space-drag pan (#37). `drag` delivers its move, press, six moves and release inside one call, and `key` its press and release, so the whole gesture landed between two frames and no frame ran while the button was down.
  - `press` (a `ref` or `x`/`y`, and `button`: `left` by default, `middle` or `right`) moves the pointer there and presses the button, which stays down. `release` (`button`, optionally a target to move to first) releases it. `key_down` and `key_up` hold and release a key, with the key names of `key`.
  - A `move` in a window that holds a button or key is a held move: it reaches the tree entry points a real move with the button down reaches, so drags, sliders, scrollbars and text selection follow it and keep the pointer outside their bounds. A call that delivers held input makes the winit shell present right after the drain, before any redraw is requested, as a real mouse-move stream presents from inside its events: one move per call is exactly one rendered pass per move.
  - Held modifier keys apply to every `lurq_interact` action in their window, as real keys that are down do, together with the action's own `modifiers`: a held `Shift`, `Control` or `Alt` sets `shift`, `ctrl` or `alt` on later pointer events (moves, presses, releases, clicks) and key events (`key`, `type`, `key_down`, `key_up`); a held `Meta` or `Super` sets `meta` on key events only, since pointer events have no `meta`. Scroll events carry no modifiers, so `wheel` passes them only to its pointer move.
  - Holds belong to a window and to the MCP session that pressed them: holding needs a session-based connection (protocol 2025-06-18, or another version before 2026-07-28; 2026-07-28 serves requests without sessions and is refused), and any client may release. Releasing a button or key that is not held, pressing a held button again, `key_down` of a held key, and `click`, `double_click`, `drag` or `key` of a held button or key are refused. Each held action's result, and a new `held` field of every window in `lurq_windows`, report what a window holds.
  - Held input never stays down once its client can no longer release it. It is released when the session that holds it ends (the client deletes it, it expires after five minutes without a request, or the server stops), when the window loses focus or closes, and when `lurq_interact` stops being available (MCP disabled, the Interact scope removed or the tool denied). A button ends the way the OS ends a press it takes over (`Tree::mouse_press_taken_by_os`: an up event, no click, and a drag ends without a drop), a key with its up event, and the next `release` or `key_up` says why nothing is held. `Tree::drain_mcp_requests` makes these releases and returns `true` when it made one; `Tree::shutdown_mcp` releases what is still held.
- Fix a held drag keeping the event loop busy: while a scrollbar, slider, text selection or `on_drag_*` drag was held, the winit shell polled its loop without pause and every pass counted as required, so a pointer held still spun a CPU core for as long as the button stayed down (about one core while an MCP client held a drag handle). A held drag changes only on input, and every input that moves it asks for a redraw, so it no longer counts as a tick source: held still, the loop waits and a pass with nothing to draw is skipped. A move during the drag is presented as before.
- Apart from held modifier keys, the other `lurq_interact` actions, and a `move` in a window that holds nothing, are unchanged.
- No public Rust API change.

## 0.41.2 — 2026-10-06

- Fix a long text stopping partway down when scrolled to, with blank space below it, in a multi-line `TextInput` or a wrapped `Text` inside a scroll view or another clip. When a text was taller than its clip and painted at a size its layout had not shaped (a display scale other than 1, for example), lurq shaped it only down to the clip's bottom. If that bottom fell just below the top of a line, the shaped rows ended at that line without one being skipped for the clip, and the cut glyph list was cached as the whole text's. Every later paint of the same text, scrolled or not, drew only those rows, until an edit changed the text. Such a paint is now cached for its clip only.
- Fix MCP discovery files piling up. An app removes its `<pid>.json` discovery file when it shuts down, but an app that was killed (a test process, a crash, a task manager kill) left its file behind, with the port and bearer token of a server that no longer listens; one Windows machine had several hundred. An MCP server now removes, when it starts, every `<pid>.json` in the discovery folder whose process no longer runs, as the operating system reports it (`OpenProcess` on Windows, `kill(pid, 0)` on Unix). Files of running processes, of processes it may not query, and files not named `<pid>.json` stay. Unix builds with the `mcp` feature now depend on `libc`.
- No public API change.

## 0.41.1 — 2026-10-05

- Fix the first full Canvas frame after a font change shaping and uploading almost every label of a large page again. Loading, installing or registering a font, and `set_scale_override`, replace the app's canvas text engine, and 0.41.0 created the new engine's shaped-text cache least recently used within its 8 MiB budget until a GPU renderer ended its first frame. A first frame charged more than the budget evicted its own first texts while drawing it. None of its texts counted as the previous frame's, so each text the next frame shaped again evicted the next one it needed, and that frame missed on nearly the whole page. When a GPU renderer has ended frames of the engine being replaced, the replacement is now framed from its first frame: that frame keeps a page up to the 16 MiB ceiling, as any later frame does, and the next frame shapes nothing again. An engine that no GPU renderer has framed, such as one used only by software canvases, is still replaced by a least recently used one.
- No public API change.

## 0.41.0 — 2026-10-05

- Fix Canvas text and images being shaped and uploaded again every frame once a page drew more of them than the caches held, which made panning and hovering a large page redraw every label from scratch:
  - Shaped text: the 256-entry least-recently-used cache, which missed on every label once a page drew more than 256 distinct texts in the same order each frame, is replaced by a frame-stamped policy with a byte budget (the existing 8 MiB, with 256 bytes charged per entry beyond its text and pixels). Text that the current or the previous frame used is never evicted, and lookups are hashed instead of scanned.
  - Shaped text that stays cached keeps its asset id, so its texture is uploaded once instead of every frame.
  - WGPU and DX12 now share one Canvas asset cache. It never evicts a texture that the current or the previous frame drew. When those two frames alone need more than the budget, it keeps them up to twice the budget; a texture beyond that is drawn but not kept past its encode, so the rest stays resident instead of the overflow being evicted and uploaded again every frame. Once frames need less, it returns to its budget.
  - A frame ends when a GPU renderer encodes, so passes that draw no canvas do not age entries. A replacement presentation drawn in batches over several encodes is one frame, which ends at the encode that commits, aborts or supersedes it, or resizes or detaches its canvas, so a page larger than a budget is not evicted by its own later batches. A pass that only draws retained artwork ages nothing. The completed front and the captured artwork are render targets outside both caches, so eviction never changes what is shown.
- Add `lurq::canvas::CanvasAssetBudget` (`new`, `bytes`, `ceiling_bytes`, `DEFAULT_BYTES` of 64 MiB, `MAX_BYTES` of 1 GiB, and `Default`) and `with_canvas_asset_budget` on `WgpuRenderEngine` and `Dx12RenderEngine` to set a renderer's Canvas asset budget. The shaped-text budget is not configurable.
- Profiler: `CanvasProfile` (`render.canvas.counts`, WGPU and DX12) adds `asset_cache_budget_bytes`, `asset_cache_stretch_bytes` (the most the cache was charged above its budget during the encode), `asset_cache_uncached` and `asset_cache_uncached_bytes` (textures drawn but not kept). `CanvasTextProfile` (`canvas_text.counts`) adds `shape_cache_uncached` and `shape_cache_stretch_bytes`. The JSON export keys are additive.
- Behaviour change: DX12 `asset_upload_details.cache_evictions` now also counts textures evicted while drawing to make room, not only those evicted after the encode, so its counts are not comparable with earlier versions.
- Memory: while the current and the previous frame need it, each cache can settle at up to twice its budget, by default 128 MiB of charged Canvas textures per renderer and 16 MiB of shaped text per app. These are policy charges, not GPU allocation sizes. While a replacement drawn in batches is open, neither cache ages; each stays within its ceiling, and textures it cannot keep are retired at every encode.
- Breaking: `CanvasProfile` and `CanvasTextProfile` have new public fields. Both have only public fields, so code that builds them with a struct literal needs the new fields or `..Default::default()`, and exhaustive patterns need the new fields or `..`.

## 0.40.1 — 2026-10-05

Security fix:

- Fix sensitive text being readable in inspection screenshots. `Text::sensitive()` (0.40.0) was redacted in every MCP and DevTools text surface, but `lurq_screenshot` and DevTools node screenshots captured the window as painted, so any MCP client could read a one-time password from the image. A capture made for an inspector now covers every pixel a sensitive text's glyphs can paint (anti-aliased edges, text shadow and transforms included, limited to its clip) with an opaque grey bar, at the place and size the text has on screen: `lurq_screenshot` of a window, a region or an element (by ref), on every render backend, and a DevTools node screenshot, from the GPU or drawn in software. The window keeps showing the text, and the app's own captures (`WindowHandle::screenshot`, `screenshot_region`, `screenshot_node`) and `Tree::painted_quads` still show it. The `lurq_screenshot` tool description says so.

## 0.40.0 — 2026-10-05

Behaviour changes:

- `WinitWindow::on_size_changed` reports the size the window took, read back from the window, instead of the size a resize requested; it is never called with a minimized window's client area, and is called once per size rather than for every event or request that repeats it. An app that persisted the requested size, or counted calls, sees fewer and real values.
- `WindowHandle::resize` of a minimized window restores the window and applies the size (on Windows it was ignored). A maximized or full-screen window stays in its mode, as before.
- `lurq_resize` takes a maximized or full-screen window out of its mode, answers only once the resize has been applied, with the size the window took, and fails when the window did not take the requested size, has no native window yet, or belongs to a headless tree (it used to answer `"ok": true` at once). Its reply has no `note` field any more.
- An `Enter` marked `KeyboardEvent::composing` no longer submits a form, activates a control, inserts a newline or blurs a text input; `on_key_down` handlers still receive it.

- Fix resizing a minimized window. A window started minimized (`Start-Process -WindowStyle Minimized`) kept its minimized frame (237x39 on one Windows machine), and `lurq_resize` could not bring it back: Windows does not resize a minimized window, yet the tool answered `"ok": true`, `on_size_changed` was told the requested size, and the next state sync read the minimized client area back. Now:
  - Every resize restores a minimized window before requesting the size, the same on Windows and macOS. `WindowHandle::resize` does only that: a maximized or full-screen window stays in its mode, so an app restoring a saved size or fitting its content never takes the user out of full screen. `lurq_resize`, which asks for an exact size, also leaves full screen and maximized, in the order full screen, minimized, maximized (a window minimized from maximized comes back maximized); it is how an agent restores a minimized window.
  - `lurq_resize` answers once the shell has applied the resize, with the client size the window really took and the modes it left (`restored_from`, and `still` for a mode the platform has not finished leaving). When the window did not take the requested size it fails with the real size and why: still minimized, maximized or full screen (macOS animates leaving full screen), or limited by the window's minimum or maximum size or the screen. A window without a native window, and a headless tree, fail at once instead of answering `ok`. Sizes above `u32::MAX` are refused. A call that times out leaves nothing waiting in the window.
  - `lurq_windows` reports `minimized`, `maximized` and `full_screen` for each window.
  - `WinitWindow::on_size_changed` reports the size the window took, read back from the window, rather than the size requested. It is never called with a minimized window's client area, and is called once per size rather than for every event that repeats it.
- Fix an `Enter` passed on during an input method composition submitting a form. 0.39.0 delivers a press other than `Process` that arrives while a composition is shown as usual, marked `KeyboardEvent::composing`, and lurq's own `Enter` handling acted on it: the form of the focused single-line input submitted (with the composition still uncommitted), a single-line input outside a form blurred, and a multi-line input took a newline. An `Enter` marked `composing` still reaches `on_key_down` handlers, but no lurq default acts on it (form submit, button or select activation, newline, blur), as the web's `isComposing` `Enter`; a form, which exposes no key handler, could not guard it itself. Once the composition is committed, `Enter` submits and activates as before.
- Add sensitive text: `Text::sensitive()` draws text that is kept out of inspection, for a one-time password or a secret the user must read on screen. It is laid out, painted and captured as usual, but the MCP tools (`lurq_read_tree`, `lurq_inspect`, the `lurq_find*` lookups, accessible names derived from it) and DevTools (tree, inspector, snapshots) show `•••` in its place, whatever its length, with the attribute `sensitive=true`. Selection follows `selectable` as for any text (off by default). New `lurq::core::Sensitive<T>` holds such a value elsewhere: its `Debug` output and DevTools rendering are `•••`, so it stays out of DevTools' signal values and history and the props inspector; it has no `Display`, and `expose()` returns the value. `lurq::core::REDACTED` is the marker.

## 0.39.0 — 2026-10-04

- Add input method (IME) composition to text inputs, on Windows and macOS alike. Until now the winit shell never let a window's input method compose (winit leaves it off until the app allows it), so Japanese, Chinese and Korean input could not compose in a text input, and lurq had no notion of a key the input method takes: Windows reports one as `Process` with the physical key as its code, so the `Enter` that confirms a composition would have reached `on_key_down` handlers and lurq's defaults as an `Enter`.
  - While a text input that is not masked has focus, the window lets the input method compose (`set_ime_allowed`) and places its candidate window at the input's caret (`set_ime_cursor_area`); other focus, and masked inputs, turn it off.
  - The composition text is shown in the input at the caret, in place of the selection, underlined in the text colour, with the caret at the input method's cursor; the value and `on_input` wait for the commit. A commit inserts the text like typing: `on_input` handlers see a `KeyboardEvent` whose `key` is the committed text and whose new `composing` field is set. A cancelled composition leaves the value; moving focus away cancels it.
  - The keys the input method takes, including the `Enter` that confirms a composition, no longer reach `on_key_down` handlers or lurq's own key handling (newline, form submit, button activation): Windows reports them as presses of `Process` (which never count as an ordinary key), macOS not at all. Their releases reach `on_key_up` handlers with the new `KeyboardEvent::composing` set. Any other press is delivered as usual, with `composing` set while a composition is shown, so a key the input method passes on (a space after a Korean syllable) is not lost and a composition the platform never ends holds no key back; `ImeEvent::Enabled` and a focus change end a composition left behind.
  - New API: `Tree::ime(ImeEvent)` applies `ImeEvent::{Enabled, Preedit { text, cursor }, Commit(text), Disabled}` (`lurq::app::events`) to the focused text input, for custom shells and tests; `Tree::is_composing()`, `Tree::ime_allowed()`, `Tree::ime_cursor_area()` (an `ImeCursorArea` in logical pixels), `TextInputHandle::composition()`, and the `KeyboardEvent::composing` field. The winit shell maps `NamedKey::Process` to the key `"Process"` (it was an empty string).
- Fix a `Select` trigger without a background painting white. A trigger part with no `background` (`SelectStyle::unstyled()`, or a part passed to `SelectStyle::trigger` that sets none) was filled with a fixed white, whatever the theme, so an app that styled the trigger on a dark surface got a white pill under light text. Such a trigger now paints no fill, like a button or text input without a background; its border, radius and shadows paint as before. The default `SelectStyle` still fills the trigger with the theme's `SurfaceInput`, and a part that sets a background is unchanged. Behaviour change: an app that relied on the white needs `.background(...)` on its trigger part.
- Fix checkboxes and sliders falling back to fixed colours where their style set no fill: a white unchecked box, a green checked box, a slate track and thumb, whatever the theme. A checkbox or slider the app has not styled now takes the theme's roles, as the form theme does: the box `SurfaceInput`, the checked box `Accent`, the track `Border`, the thumb `Accent`. A part the app styles without a fill paints none, like the select trigger: `.box_part(...)` or `.checked_box(...)` without `background`, `.track(...)` or `.thumb(...)` without one. A checked box whose `checked_box` part is not set keeps the unchecked part's fill, or the theme's `Accent`. Fills the app sets (including generic `.background()`) are unchanged. Behaviour change: an unstyled checked checkbox is `Accent` rather than green, an unstyled slider `Border`/`Accent` rather than slate, and an app part that relied on a fallback fill needs `.background(...)`.
- Add `Tree::painted_quads()`: what the last layout paints, as `Quad`s in paint order, without a window or render engine, so tests (including an application's, after `Tree::pass_headless`) can check fills, borders and carets the element tree does not show.

## 0.38.0 — 2026-10-04

- Add complete presentation transactions to GPU Canvas, so that an application rebuilding a canvas after a zoom or a document change no longer exposes a blank or partly drawn frame. `Context2D::begin_presentation()` returns a token and records the following drawing batches, over as many frames as they take, into a replacement surface while the previous complete surface stays presented. `commit_presentation(token)` publishes that replacement only if it is still the current one, and `abort_presentation(token)` discards it and keeps the completed front. A new `begin_presentation()` supersedes an unfinished replacement, whose token can no longer publish. Software canvases isolate the replacement in a pixmap.
- Add retained artwork: `capture_artwork()` captures the replacement's artwork before transient overlays, without a CPU readback on GPU backends; `draw_retained_artwork(matrix)` reprojects the last complete artwork through a device-pixel camera matrix inside a presentation; `forget_artwork()` releases it when the document or page changes.
- Snapshots (`CanvasHandle::snapshot`) taken while a replacement is pending return the completed front and its revision.
- When the renderer cannot take another replacement target, the canvas reports the new `CanvasError::PresentationBusy` and keeps the completed front and its revision. Ordinary writes are then refused until a later `begin_presentation()` admits a new replacement; treat it as temporary pressure and retry once in-flight submissions complete.
- Replacement and artwork targets have their own allowance, `lurq::canvas::MAX_PRESENTATION_BYTES` (256 MiB), separate from the uploaded-asset charge, and a retired target is reused only once the GPU has finished with it.
- `status().gpu` (`CanvasGpuStats`) adds `presentation_workspace_bytes`, `presentation_commits`, `presentation_token`, `fallback_quads`, `presentation_allocations`, `presentation_reuses` and `presentation_refusals`. They count renderer submissions, not physical display scanout; WGPU accounting reports logical texture extents.
- The WGPU and DX12 Canvas renderers are split into modules (initialization, drawing or encoding, presentation, artwork and the target pool), with the drawing paths unchanged.
- Breaking: `CanvasError` gains the `PresentationBusy` variant and `CanvasGpuStats` gains public fields, so exhaustive matches on `CanvasError` and struct literals of `CanvasGpuStats` need updating.
- Coverage at release: the real-adapter presentation tests ran on WGPU; the DX12 presentation code was covered by compilation.

## 0.37.0 — 2026-10-03

- Fix children that gave way coming back at a narrower width. When a later order's child dropped and freed more than the line needed, the line gave way again from the start and gave the rest to earlier orders, including an item whose own row had dropped a child (a status item's time): it got its room back and showed the time again, so a line could show more at a narrower width (Orchester's status bar: at 1210 px the version showed and the time was dropped, at 1200 px the version dropped and the time came back). What gives way whole now only accumulates as the line narrows:
  - A shrinking child whose own row holds droppable children (`ShrinkLimit::Drop`, `shrink_drop_below`, or such an item nested in it) keeps its natural size until its line needs it to give way. It then collapses, as one step after the order's drops: its row drops all of them (and collapses every such nested item) at once, so it holds its padding, its other children at their natural size and their spacing, and it shrinks on from there toward its floor without growing back. The line decides this and the item's layout follows it, so they cannot disagree. Inside such an item, children give way only once it collapses.
  - Within an order the whole steps come in a fixed sequence (drops, then drops below a size, then collapses, each last first), each triggered only when the order's shrinking children cannot absorb the overflow, so a narrower line takes the same steps and more.
  - Width a step frees beyond what the line needs goes back only to children that trimmed; a dropped or collapsed child stays so.
- A dropped child no longer counts its spacing when the line is distributed again.
- Behaviour change: inside a shrinking item whose own row holds droppable children, the children no longer give way by their own orders before the item collapses. In 0.36.0 an item of [icon, label with `flex_shrink`, time with `ShrinkLimit::Drop`] (or a label and a time of the same order) trimmed the label first and dropped the time only once the label could not absorb the overflow; now the item keeps its natural size until its line collapses it, and then the time drops at once and the label trims from its natural size. To keep the old effect, make the label and the time separate children of the line that gives way, the time in a later `shrink_order` than the label. Lines that give way themselves (not shrinking items inside one) still drop a child only while the trimming children of its order cannot absorb the overflow.

## 0.36.1 — 2026-10-03

- Fix Windows text clipboard writes losing their owner after Copy returns. The writer now uses a persistent caller-thread-owned window and checked UTF-16 allocation, retaining the public boolean API. Ownership lasts until that thread exits; failures after the clipboard is emptied remain non-atomic, and success requires both data transfer and clipboard closure.

## 0.36.0 — 2026-10-03

- Add `.shrink_drop_below(size)` for the shrinking children of a single-line `Row` or `Column`: the child first shrinks like its `ShrinkLimit`, but no further than `size`, and when its order still cannot absorb the overflow with every shrinking child at its floor, it drops like `ShrinkLimit::Drop` (zero size, no spacing, not drawn, no focus or Tab) before the next order shrinks. A child already narrower than `size` drops without shrinking; a negative or NaN `size` counts as 0. Within one order, `Drop` children drop first, then `shrink_drop_below` children, each last first; both count as gone in an ancestor's content minimum. It has no effect with `ShrinkLimit::Drop`. It is a method rather than a `ShrinkLimit` variant because `ShrinkLimit` is an exhaustive public enum, and a new variant would break matches on it.
- Fix the space a drop frees staying empty. A dropped child frees its whole size and spacing, which can be more than the line needed; the rest stayed as slack at the end of the line while children of earlier orders remained shrunk. The line now gives way again without the dropped children, so those children take the rest back and the line stays filled.
- Fix a blank left where a nested child dropped. A shrunk child whose own line (or a line nested in it on the same axis) dropped a child to fit kept the whole size it was given, so the dropped child's width stayed as an empty gap inside it (a status item that dropped its words while giving way by a few pixels). Such a child is now laid out again at what it still holds, and its line is distributed again with it at that size, so the space goes back to the line.
- Fix an ellipsizing text with `ShrinkLimit::Drop` and a `flex_shrink` factor ellipsizing instead of dropping: it was measured inside its row's width, so its natural size could fit after being cut. It is now measured at its full width. Without a shrink factor it still ellipsizes within its row.

## 0.35.0 — 2026-10-03

- Add a give-way order and limits for the shrinking children (those with a `flex_shrink` factor) of a single-line `Row` or `Column`. Until now the overflow was shared among all of them at once, in proportion to their factors, and clamped only at an explicit minimum, so a toolbar or status bar could not let one item give way before another, nor keep an icon and a count whole.
  - `.shrink_order(order)` (an `i32`, 0 by default): the children with the lowest order absorb the overflow, down to their limit, before any child of the next order shrinks at all. Children of one order share their part by shrink factor.
  - `.shrink_limit(ShrinkLimit)` (`lurq::layout::layout_kind::ShrinkLimit`): `MinSize` (the default) shrinks down to `min_width`/`min_height`, or 0; `Content` never below the child's content minimum (for a `Row` in a row or a `Column` in a column: its padding and spacing, the natural size of each child that does not shrink and the limit of each that does; anything else keeps its natural size, like CSS `min-width: auto`), nor below an explicit minimum; `Drop` keeps the natural size or drops the child. Within one order, droppable children are dropped, last first, only while the shrinking children of that order cannot absorb the rest.
  - A dropped child is laid out at zero size, takes no spacing, and is neither drawn nor hit. Nothing inside it is a Tab stop or takes focus (`focus()` and `ctx.focus` requests for it are refused, and keys never activate it), focus inside it is blurred when it drops, and the element refs and `bounds()` of everything inside it report a zero-size rect where the next child starts. It stays mounted and keeps its state. `LayoutResult::is_dropped()` reports it; devtools and the MCP tree mark it `dropped`.
  - A line that uses orders or limits shares each order's part in whole pixels: every child loses a whole number of pixels except the one with the largest factor, which takes the fraction, so a sliver of overflow truncates one label instead of putting a stray ellipsis on several, and each child gives up a pixel at a time as the line narrows. Keep factors within one order of similar magnitude: shares are computed in `f32`. A line without orders or limits shrinks exactly as before.
- Devtools: the layout rows of a node show its shrink order and limit when they are set.

## 0.34.2 — 2026-10-03

- Fix async tasks outliving their component. A task started on Tokio by `ctx.future`, `ctx.stream` or `ctx.future_action` was not aborted when its component unmounted (removed by its parent, removed while offstage, replaced by mounting another root, or dropped with its `Tree`), because dropping a Tokio `JoinHandle` only detaches the task. A pending future still ran to the end, with its side effects, and a stream that loops until `StreamEmitter::emit` fails kept running until its next emit, which might never come. An unmounting component now aborts its tasks: the runtime drops their futures, and on a runtime that is shutting down or gone the abort does nothing. A dependency change and a render that no longer reaches a `ctx.future`/`ctx.stream`/`ctx.future_action` call cancel the task as before. `FutureAction::run` on an action whose component has unmounted does nothing, rather than starting a task whose result nothing applies.
- Docs: an offstage component (`Ctx::mount_offstage`, `mount_keyed_offstage`, `Router::mount_offstage`) is still mounted, so its tasks are not cancelled. A task running on Tokio keeps running, and what it produces while offstage is applied once the component is active again; work polled cooperatively (without a Tokio handle) waits. The guides said only that offstage components get no future polling. Futures and Timers has a new Task lifetime section.

## 0.34.0 — 2026-10-02

- Fix a window-sized layer laid out at an earlier window size. A component that declared a `Modal` itself (with any `ModalTarget`: `Root`, `Parent` or an element), a modal nested in such a layer, or a `Popup`/`Popover`/`Overlay` sized against the window, and re-rendered after the window was resized back to an earlier size (1440x960 → 960x700 → 1440x960), drew the layer's content at the size before (960x700). Each layer takes over the previous pass's layout caches, which held results for both sizes, and serving the older one left the other size's constraints first, so the re-render laid the changed content out under them. A layout served from the cache now makes its constraints the current ones, and a node served from its older cached layout is laid out again, rather than patched, when its content next changes. The same could happen to any changed content whose constraints had returned to an earlier value; while constraints stay the same, the layout cache serves and patches results as before. A child result that a parent reuses while laying itself out again (a repaired child of a `Reserved` scroll container, which measures its content at full width before laying it out beside the gutter) likewise becomes the child's current layout.
- Add shared profiling sessions (`perf_profile`). `Tree::profiling_handle()` returns a cloneable `lurq::app::profiler::ProfilingHandle`, usable from another thread, that starts independent bounded CPU captures (`start(SessionOptions)`), reads a live snapshot (`read(id)`) and finalizes one session (`end(id)`) without requesting a redraw, layout or event-loop roundtrip. Up to `MAX_ACTIVE_SESSIONS` (8) sessions overlap, and ending one never stops or resets another. `SessionOptions` sets `max_samples` (1 to `MAX_SAMPLES_PER_SESSION`, 240; default 120; oldest dropped first) and `include_devtools` (DevTools windows are excluded by default). A sample is a completed pass, a root rebuild or subtree refresh, or an input dispatch; only operations that start and finish inside the session are kept. A `ProfileReport` also lists the work still unfinished when the session is read or ended (window, current `Phase` and elapsed time), separately from completed samples, so a stalled UI is visible. Timings are CPU wall-clock milliseconds; GPU timestamps are reported as unavailable. Without `perf_profile`, `start` returns `ProfileError::FeatureDisabled`. With `serde` or `mcp`, `SessionStarted::to_json` and `ProfileReport::to_json` export the version-1 report. See the new Profiling sessions guide.
- MCP: add `lurq_profile_start`, `lurq_profile_read` and `lurq_profile_end` (Observe scope). They run on the server thread, so they answer while the UI is blocked. `lurq_profile_read` without an id reports the build's profiling availability. The MCP server can only read and end sessions it started; revoking Observe, disabling MCP, denying a profiling tool or shutting the server down cancels its sessions. `McpConfig::include_devtools` also decides whether its sessions include DevTools windows.
- Profiling: layout compute and the component `after_layout` hooks are separate phases inside the layout update. Captured passes with `canvas` record synchronous Canvas text work (`Context2D::measure_text`/`fill_text` calls, shape cache hits, misses and evictions, produced bytes and per-stage time). The built-in WGPU and DX12 backends report Canvas CPU work in the new `RenderProfile::canvas` field (`CanvasProfile`: total, tessellation, asset and buffer upload, recording, submit and counters), and a DX12 Canvas encode captured by a live session also reports `asset_upload_details` (`CanvasAssetUploadProfile`: texture creation, pixel packing, staging and command recording, descriptor writes, cache eviction, and asset-cache counters).
- `Context2D::measure_text` no longer renders and composes the text's pixels; it shapes the text and returns the same metrics. Measured text is cached separately from drawn text. The Canvas glyph image cache keeps its 2048-entry and 16 MiB bounds but tracks its size as glyphs are added, instead of summing every cached image on each glyph.
- `RenderEngine` has two new methods with default implementations: `profile_backend()` (a static backend name, `"custom"` by default) and, with `perf_profile`, `set_profile_context(ProfileContext)`. Existing implementations compile unchanged.
- `lurq::app::profiler` is now public without the `perf_profile` feature. It exports `FrameProfile`, `RenderProfile` and `RuntimeMemoryProfile` as before, now also `GlyphEngineProfile`, the type of `FrameProfile::glyph_engine`, which could not be named before, and adds `ProfilingHandle`, `SessionOptions`, `SessionId`, `SessionStarted`, `ProfileReport`, `ProfileError`, `ProfileSample`, `SampleData`, `PassSample`, `InputDispatchSample`, `InputKind`, `UiUpdateSample`, `UiUpdateKind`, `InFlightObservation`, `WindowStatus`, `BuildAvailability`, `Phase`, `PhaseGuard`, `ProfileContext`, `CanvasTextProfile`, `CanvasProfile`, `CanvasAssetUploadProfile` and the `MAX_ACTIVE_SESSIONS`, `MAX_SAMPLES_PER_SESSION`, `MAX_TRACKED_WINDOWS`, `MAX_WINDOW_ID_BYTES` and `MAX_ENDED_SESSION_IDS` limits.
- DX12: Canvas assets that are only sampled (images, glyph atlases) are allocated without render-target capability, which lowers their memory use on DX12 drivers.
- Breaking: `FrameProfile` has a new public field `render_profile_available` (false for a custom backend without CPU stage profiling, whose render timings are then not meaningful), and `RenderProfile` has a new public field `canvas`. Both structs have only public fields, so code that builds them with a struct literal, such as a custom `RenderEngine` returning a `RenderProfile` from `last_profile()`, no longer compiles: add `render_profile_available` and `canvas: CanvasProfile::default()` (or the backend's Canvas timings), or fill the rest with `..Default::default()`.

## 0.33.1 — 2026-10-02

- Fix presses next to a scrollbar selecting text instead of scrolling. Only the painted thumb, as thin as 4 px, counted as the scrollbar; a press beside it went to the content underneath, and the selectable-text drag start, which accepts presses up to 0.75 of a line's height (at least 8 px) off the text, reached the bar under the last line. Trying to drag a `ScrollHorizontal` over selectable text sideways selected the text, and the same happened beside a vertical bar. Even a press on the thumb cleared an existing selection.
  - A plain overlay bar (the default, with a transparent track) now takes the pointer on its thumb and 4 px around it on every side. The rest of its lane stays with the content, so trailing row buttons and menu options next to the bar keep their clicks and hover.
  - A bar that visibly owns its lane, a `Reserved` gutter or an overlay bar with a painted `track_color`, takes the whole lane: the track along its length and, across it, the bar plus 4 px on each side (or the edge inset, if larger), up to the container's edge.
  - Where a scrollbar takes the pointer, a press reaches no content underneath and never starts a text selection. A press on the thumb drags it. A press on the track of a bar that owns its lane, which did nothing before, pages one viewport toward the press, once per press, on Windows and macOS alike. The scrollbar holds the pointer until the release, which clicks nothing.
  - Where scrollbars overlap, the one painted on top wins: an outer scroll container's bar over those of the containers inside it, and the horizontal bar over the vertical one in a two-axis scroller's corner.
  - A bar shown by `ScrollBarVisibility::Always` while the content fits takes no pointer, so its thumb is never dragged.
  - `.scrollbar_hovered(...)` (and `ScrollState::is_thumb_hovered`) applies over the same area that takes the pointer, and ends when the pointer leaves the scroll container.
  - Mouse wheel and Shift+wheel scrolling are unchanged.

## 0.33.0 — 2026-10-02

- Fix text drawn from a stale layout after its width changes back. Text layout writes what it computes for the current width into the node's state: the ellipsized string, whether the renderer wraps, and the caret geometry of selectable text and text inputs. A layout served from the layout cache skipped that step, so the state kept what the last real layout, at another width, had written. A truncated label that got wider again kept its old ellipsis (`connect-to-…` in a 400px box), whether at the root, shrunk in a `Row` or in a component that re-renders on resize; text measured once without a width bound stopped wrapping; and selections, caret placement and clicks in selectable text and multiline inputs used the other width's line breaks. Each text leaf's layout result now carries its output, and serving a cached result writes it back.
- Breaking: `LayoutResult` has a private field, so it can no longer be built with a struct literal outside lurq.

## 0.32.0 — 2026-10-02

- Fix wrapped Row percentage-width children to resolve against finite parent content constraints, preserving natural compact sizing and wrapping for ordinary controls.
- Add explicitly opted-in MCP file selection: register `FileDialogBroker` with `McpConfig::file_dialogs` and grant `Scope::custom("file_dialogs")`. `lurq_file_dialogs` lists pending requests; `lurq_file_dialog_respond` selects absolute paths or cancels by request, window and operation identity. Adapt the normal picker handler to await the broker; existing native dialog calls are not automatically intercepted. The app remains responsible for file validation, IO and overwrite policy.

## 0.31.0 — 2026-09-30

- Add semantic items to `Canvas` so charts and other drawings can be hit-tested and read by tooling. `CanvasHandle::set_items` replaces the canvas's set of `CanvasItem`s: `CanvasItem::rect` or `CanvasItem::point` with a stable id, a role (`"bar"`, `"point"`, `"label"`, …), an optional `.label(...)` and `.value(...)`, in content coordinates (the space `point_from_window` returns). `item_at(x, y)` hit-tests them for hover tooltips, `items()` returns them and `item_window_bounds(id)` gives an item's window-logical box, including padding, scrolling and ancestor transforms, clipped to the canvas. Ids are unique per canvas: of items sharing an id, `set_items` keeps the last, as `item_at` treats it as drawn on top, and logs a warning once per set of duplicated ids. A logical resize discards the items together with the pixels. `CanvasItem` and `CanvasItemShape` are `#[non_exhaustive]`: build items with the constructors. Item ids, roles, labels and values are visible, unmasked, to MCP clients with the observe scope. New example: `canvas_chart`.
- MCP: canvas items appear as children of their canvas in `lurq_read_tree` (`- bar item:tue [ref_3] "Tue" @159,63 75x216 {value="18 runs"}`; item ids are marked `item:` so they never read as element `#id`s), with refs, labels, values and bounds in screenshot pixels, clipped to the canvas and to what its ancestors (placed with their transforms) and the window leave visible; the canvas shows `{items=N}`. Items with nothing visible are listed as `(not visible)` and refuse input, and input by item ref must hit the canvas, so it never lands on a neighbouring control. Item refs work with `lurq_interact` (`move`, `click`, `double_click`, `drag` at the item's center; `scroll_to` scrolls the item into view), `lurq_screenshot` (crops to the item) and `lurq_find`; `lurq_find_by_id` falls back to item ids when no element has the id. Actions re-resolve the item's live bounds, so a ref follows its item across redraws and fails once the app stops registering it. Pointer input reaches the canvas's own handlers, so the app's hover tooltips open. Only canvases with items or pointer handlers are labelled; `lurq_read_tree` lists up to `max_items` items per canvas (default 200) and counts the rest. Lookup lines say when bounds are unknown instead of printing `@0,0 0x0`.
- MCP: add `lurq_inspect`, a structured semantic view of a window (role, accessible name, state, bounds, actions and refs; with `query`/`role`, the matching nodes and their ancestor paths), and `lurq_act`, which invokes a ref without coordinates after checking its live role, name and hittability. Masked input values stay redacted. Canvas items are children of their canvas node there too (`canvas_item: true`, `state.value`), and match `query` and `role`; searches check every item without counting items against `max_nodes`, return at most `max_nodes` matches (default 500; `truncated: true` beyond that) and mint refs only for the matches returned.
- MCP: `lurq_act` accepts `hover` besides `invoke`: it moves the pointer to the center of an element or canvas item whose element has mouse-move or mouse-enter handlers. Each `lurq_inspect` node lists the actions it accepts in `actions`. A clickable `Canvas` has the role `canvas` instead of `button`.
- MCP: app-provided text in `lurq_read_tree` outlines and ref lines (element ids, classes, `describe` attributes, tags, window names, canvas item ids, roles and values) is printed as-is only when it is a plain word of letters, digits and `-_.:/`, and quoted with escapes otherwise, so it can never start a new line or forge an element or ref. Attribute values and item labels longer than 80 characters end in an ellipsis, like element text. `describe` values with spaces now appear quoted: `{role="commits the form"}`.
- Fix `lurq_interact` `scroll_to` overshooting through nested scroll containers: each outer container was adjusted from the target's position before the inner container scrolled, so an element inside a scrolled page's inner scroll area ended up past the outer viewport.
- Fix drawing lost on the GPU canvas backends when a component re-renders in the same frame. Reconciling a re-rendered component detached every canvas in its old output, which discarded the surface's queued drawing, and then carried the same surface over into the new output. A handler that drew and changed state (painting a hover highlight and showing its tooltip) lost the drawing. Now only canvases the new output does not carry over are detached, including those of overlays that close; carried-over surfaces keep their queued work, and refs rebind at the next layout as before. Software canvases draw synchronously and were not affected.

## 0.30.4 — 2026-09-29

- Fix text selection disappearing on mouse release at fractional display scaling. Drag handlers stored click suppression in logical coordinates, but the release compared physical coordinates and dispatched a click that cleared the selection. The comparison now uses logical coordinates, so a completed drag keeps its selection and a following ordinary click still works.

## 0.30.3 — 2026-09-29

- Add `WinitWindow::with_start_without_focus(true)` for automation-driven windows that must open without taking keyboard focus. On Windows and macOS, the initial window is created visible and inactive; the normal startup path is unchanged.
- Read the native focus state when main and secondary windows are created, so `WindowHandle::info().is_focused` and MCP `lurq_windows` report an inactive window correctly from the first query.

## 0.30.2 — 2026-09-28

- Add `SelectStyle::min_menu_width(width)` for a select menu that needs more room than its trigger. The default remains the trigger's width; a configured minimum widens the menu without resizing the trigger, caps it at the viewport width, and positions it inside the viewport.

## 0.30.1 — 2026-09-28

- Fix `OutsidePress::Consume` allowing a press on a custom `WindowChrome` title-bar control to activate that control while dismissing a popup opened from the window content. The persistent chrome layer no longer counts as a popup stacked above the content popup: the first press closes only the popup, and a second press activates the title-bar control. Popups and select menus genuinely stacked above another popup still receive presses without dismissing it.

## 0.30.0 — 2026-09-28

- Fix the caret of a focused text input not blinking in a running app. The blink phase was only recomputed during a pass, and nothing requested a pass when the phase changed, so the winit shell kept showing the caret; it only blinked while something else redrew the window. A pass with a focused blinking input now schedules a redraw at the next toggle, and the shell waits for it.
- Fix multi-line text inputs placing their lines by the ink of the glyphs shown. The run was aligned so that its topmost ink touched the top of the content box: "as" sat about 3 px higher than "asd", so a line jumped when a letter with an ascender was typed, and the text (and a placeholder starting with a capital) sat off the caret, which follows the font's metrics. Lines now sit in their line boxes as the font's ascent, descent and `line_height` place them, like the caret, with the new `VerticalAlign::LineBox`.
- A text input's selection now covers the font's ascent and descent in each line instead of the whole line box: with a relaxed `line_height` (1.7 at 13 px) it was a 22 px block around 13 px text. The caret and selection share the line box's center.
- The caret at the start of a left-aligned line (an empty field with a placeholder, or before a line's first glyph) is drawn just before the line instead of over its first glyph, which has about no side bearing. Elsewhere it still starts at its insertion point. Where an ancestor clips right at the line's edge it stays at the edge.
- Breaking: `VerticalAlign` has a new variant, `LineBox`; exhaustive matches on it need an arm.

- Add `WindowHandle::set_title` (`ctx.window().set_title(...)`) to change the OS window title after the window has opened: the title bar, the taskbar button and Alt+Tab on Windows, and the Window menu and Mission Control on macOS. It maps to winit's `Window::set_title` on the main and secondary windows. Before, the title could only be given once, with `WinitWindow::with_title` or when opening a secondary window. `WindowHandle::title()` returns the title last requested or the one the window was created with, without subscribing. Setting the current title queues nothing, so a component can set a title derived from its state in `render`. `lurq_windows` in the MCP server reports the main window's title too, and a secondary window's current title.
- Fix scroll offsets lost when an offstage component becomes active again (`Ctx::mount_offstage`, `mount_keyed_offstage`, `Router::mount_offstage`). A resumed component takes its runtime state back from its last render, but once its parent had re-rendered it with new props (or a changed context or slot children) while it was active, that render held fresh scroll states: the active tree kept the live ones, so the offset stayed on screen, but it was reset to 0 after the next trip offstage and back. Such a render now takes over the previous render's runtime state, as a component's own re-render already did, so scroll areas keep their offsets across tab switches whether or not the app holds their `ScrollState`. Canvases, whose drawing surface is carried over the same way, are fixed too. Text-input carets and selections already came back; a test now covers them.
- Breaking behaviour: a left press outside an open popup now only closes it, like a native menu. This applies to `Popup`/`Popover` and `Overlay` with `dismiss_on_outside_click(true)`, and to `Select` menus.
